# KB: File structure — per-file operating essays

Moved VERBATIM out of CLAUDE.md (tier-2 pass). Each `### path` section is that file's full operating essay — invariants, incidents, gotchas. **READ the entry BEFORE modifying the file; UPDATE it in the same commit when behavior changes.**

### server.js

server.js              — BOOTSTRAP + WIRING ONLY (~1910 lines since the 2.325.0 拆分; the arch suite RATCHETS it ≤2100). Everything mechanical lives in src/server/* `create(deps)` factory modules: usage-pool-engine (pool auto-switch/estimator/anchors/sealed orders), session-stdout (setupSessionPty = caps.streamProtocol → src/server/stdout/ consumer lookup + attach since S5, plus onExit death classification / attachToDtach / session-meta store), boot-restore (migrations+restoreSessions+R6 reopen+keeper re-adopt), session-brain (parity comparator+sbSeenFirst+claudeSideEffects), account-usage-routes, mounts-plugins-wiring, cli-env (X display/adapters/CLI probes/model registry), dial-pairing, exit-routes, ops-routes (version/self-update/maintenance), incident-wiring, sysinfo-wiring, goal-sync, agent-tool-generators. server.js destructures each factory's exports under the SAME names, so grep-by-name still lands. Late-boot singletons cross as lazy getters; mutable `let` state as explicit `get*()`.

### data/bin/editor/code

data/bin/editor/code   — Fake "code" script for Ctrl+G (generated by server; moved OUT of data/bin in 2.267.0 — data/bin is PATH-prepended into every local session and the fake `code` shadowed real VS Code there; EDITOR= stays the absolute path, boot unlinks a stale data/bin/code; remote layout was already ~/.vibespace/editor/). **ANCHOR RULE (2.267.2 regression, self-caught): ONLY `code` lives in EDITOR_DIR — every OTHER generator (vibespace-status/vibespace-hook.mjs/hook-register) and USAGE_STATUSLINE_CMD anchors on AGENT_BIN_DIR (= data/bin, the PATH dir); the 2.267.0 move dragged them all into editor/ because they shared the constant (tools off PATH + statusline pointing at a missing file + global hook registration rewritten). Before changing a shared path constant, grep every consumer. data/bin/editor/ is gitignored (generated; tracked generated files block self-update — 2.111.26 class).**

### data/bin/vibespace-hook.mjs

data/bin/vibespace-hook.mjs — Dual-harness hook (SessionStart→task context OR a baseline tools intro for no-task sessions, UserPromptSubmit→first-prompt context + status-override notices + per-turn micro-reminder, Stop→bookkeeping nudge (2.79.0, CLAUDE-ONLY registration): calls GET /api/agent/stop-check and emits {decision:'block', reason} when the server says the board state is stale — the agent gets ONE follow-up mini-turn to set status/mirror asks/log progress, gated by stop_hook_active (never loops), server-side staleness+cooldown thresholds (agents.stopNudgeStaleMinutes/CooldownMinutes, defaults 10/30min, clamped 1-240/2-720 — 2.89.0; an EXPLICIT 0 = every-stop mode: 0 stale = always stale, 0 cooldown = none — 2.210.0 user request; stop_hook_active/nudgeTurnActive still cap it at ONE mini-turn per user turn); codex's equivalent rides codex-chat-wrapper's turn/completed (maybeStopNudge → synthetic <vibespace-reminder> turn, nudgeTurnActive guard) since the app-server has no blockable Stop in JSON-RPC mode; generated at startup; env-gated no-op only when VIBESPACE_API/TOKEN are absent — a MISSING VIBESPACE_TASK_ID no longer no-ops (2.38.0), so any VibeSpace session still learns `vibespace-status`; emits ONLY nested hookSpecificOutput). Registered idempotently for BOTH events in ~/.claude/settings.json + ~/.codex/hooks.json by ensureAgentHooks — only the vibespace entry is ever touched. **TEMP-SERVER GUARD (2.225.1, real incident): a server running from under os.tmpdir() skips ALL global hook writes (register AND strip; hookRegistrationSafe) — a /tmp worktree smoke server once rewrote the global hook command to its throwaway path, and after cleanup every hook errored MODULE_NOT_FOUND for two days (the CLI snapshots hook config per session, so healing the file doesn't reach running sessions). Any test harness that boots server.js should also set VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS=1 (all four worktree smokes do); VIBESPACE_FORCE_AGENT_HOOKS=1 overrides.** **INTEGRATION MASTER SWITCH (2.190.0, settings category "Integration"): `agents.vibespaceIntegration` default ON; OFF = the model gets a pristine CLI. SCOPE = AGENT-VISIBLE integration ONLY (2.190.1, user decision): model-invisible plumbing is EXEMPT — the usage statusline, billing/account env, Ctrl+G editor, keeper transport all keep working either way. PER-FEATURE toggles under it (2.211.0, user request '注入context但不给ask/progress'): `agents.contextInjection` (group payloads/diffs; per-group injectContext stays the finer grain) + `agents.toolStatus`/`toolAsk`/`toolTask` — a disabled tool is neither TAUGHT (sessionToolsIntro()/renderContext's _toolsSectionParts/per-turn reminder/stop-nudge steps all compose from enabledTools(); teaching a refusing command trains dead ends) nor SERVED (its write endpoints 403 with skip-and-continue-do-not-retry guidance); toolStatus off also silences the stop-check arbiter (it is keyed on status staleness); contextInjection off falls back to the baseline tools intro; reads (GET /api/agent/task = `vibespace-task show`) stay governed by the master, not toolTask. Route smoke: scripts/test-tool-toggles.mjs (21 asserts).** Hook entries stripped live on toggle (persistence writeSettings' `onSettingsWrite` callback → stripAgentHookEntries; boot re-checks the setting so a flip-while-down converges; the boot ensureAgentHooks call is DEFERRED until after setupPersistence so the setting is readable), new spawns get NO VIBESPACE_API/tools-PATH/remote-prelude (keeper still ships; VIBESPACE_SESSION_TOKEN stays for Ctrl+G editor auth, inert alone since every consumer guards on api+token), and agent-routes gates every model-visible CONTENT response for pre-toggle sessions too: the three deliveries return empty (prompt-context also consumes-and-DROPS pending status-override notices — deferring them injected stale reminders on re-enable) and GET /api/agent/task 403s (it returns the same steering substance; `vibespace-task show` must not bypass the pristine state) while WRITE endpoints stay live. Both Install routes (local /api/agent-hooks/install AND host /api/hosts/:id/agent-tools/install) refuse while OFF; the Manage-Agents host row shows the residue + keeps Remove as cleanup. Dial spawns drop the `$HOME/.vibespace/bin` PATH segment too (leftover tools from earlier ON spawns must not be name-resolvable). Distinct from the data/.agent-hooks-optout marker (manual Manage-Agents Remove) — the setting never writes the marker, and re-enable does NOT override it. E2E: scripts/test-integration-toggle.mjs. vibespace-hook-register.mjs = self-locating installer distributed to remote hosts (P3); its `--uninstall` mode (2.129.0) strips ONLY our entry from the remote configs (foreign hooks untouched) — the Manage-Agents remote Remove path. **SSH→DIAL GRADUATION (2.248.0, B-6640; **AUTOMATIC since 2.311.0** — `hosts.onSshConnected` fires after every successful ssh device connect and the server graduates in the background. Gates, each of them a way to do something the operator did not ask for: setting `agentd.autoGraduate` (default on) + an operator-declared `agentd.publicUrl` (it must NEVER self-publish this instance to the relay — that side effect is the operator's choice) + per-machine `autoGraduate:false` + one attempt/machine/6h; failures serverNotice and leave the machine on ssh. The install half is `graduateHostToDial()`, shared by the button and the auto path. Test: scripts/test-auto-graduate.mjs): an ssh machine can install the daemon as a persistent service and DIAL BACK over ws — `POST /api/hosts/:id/graduate-dial` (`{serverUrl|viaRelay}`, `{remove:true}` rolls back). The record keeps `transport:'ssh'` and gains `deviceId`+`dialTokenHash`+`dialRoot`; `_deviceConnect` prefers the live dial link and falls through to ssh on ANY failure (never worse than before), `findByDeviceId` falls back to non-dial records so dial-in auth resolves, `onDialIn` evicts a cached ssh dm, `list()` exposes `graduated`/`dialLive`. INVARIANTS: ssh stays the bootstrap+rescue channel (never removed); the base URL is explicit>publicUrl>relay and the MACHINE is asked whether it can reach it BEFORE anything installs (unreachable ⇒ abort, stay pure-ssh — a daemon that can never dial in is worse than no daemon); `dialRoot` must mirror the installer's own derivation byte-for-byte (hostname-only + `tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9.-'`) or removal tears down the wrong directory; removal always clears the local record even if the host is dead. E2E: scripts/test-graduate-dial.mjs (10 asserts against a real host; needs an ssh reverse tunnel so the host can reach the throwaway server). REMOTE-TEST GOTCHAS the harness encodes: server.js honours NO data-dir env (a worktree's own data/ IS the isolation), an inherited VIBESPACE_PASSWORD turns auth on and 401s everything, a worktree is at HEAD so uncommitted changes must be copied in, and `pkill -f vibespace-agentd` over ssh matches its OWN command line and kills the session (use `pgrep -f "[v]ibespace-agentd" | xargs -r kill`).** **BOUNDED DEVICE CONNECT (2.246.2/2.247.0, B-fa6f — userN's flapping H200s): `hosts.deviceBounded(id, connectMs=6000)` races device() against a deadline for every READ-ONLY/interactive data-plane consumer (~26 sites: discovery/RemoteFs/dirComplete/slab sync/usage harvest/mount heal/ports/exit) — the connect ladder runs up to ~2.7min and on a lossy path where TCP opens but the ssh banner hangs (ConnectTimeout only bounds the TCP connect, verified live) every surface sat on it BEFORE its own ssh fallback. THREE INVARIANTS (adversarial-review-hardened, do not regress): ① session-ESTABLISHING consumers keep the FULL ladder or ≥15s — findKeeperFor (adopt-vs-respawn: a deadline reads as 'no live keeper' → writer sweep SIGTERMs the healthy claude), _hostShell (accountsStatus rides it inside CREATE's linked/host-held rescue), homeDir (feeds the create cwd default, B-0d70 shape); ② the race OBSERVES the loser promise (p.catch(()=>{})) — an abandoned ladder's late rejection is an unhandledRejection = process crash on Node≥15; ③ device() DEDUPES in-flight connects per id (_deviceInflight → _deviceConnect) — without it every timed-out probe stacked another 13×ssh ladder under a 5s-polling sidebar. Any hosts-like TEST MOCK must also implement deviceBounded (two suites broke on the missing method). **B-218d FIXED in 2.247.1-2.247.3** (2.247.2: EXPLICIT-host ssh resumes probe findKeeperFor too — before, only host-inference and dial did, so a sidebar resume with the host selected always swept; 2.247.3: findKeeperFor rewritten — the ssh leg scanned only legacy run/ and NEVER saw agentd sessions, and grep-in-state-json only matches RESUMED spawns (--resume embeds the cid) — now ONE scan over both stores + a childPid→~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json lock leg for fresh spawns, returning {sid, kind} so the consumer routes to the right attach binary): findKeeperFor's agentd pipe sids are tagged `keeperKind:'agentd'` and the ssh branch adopts them via the attach-cli with a NO-spawn-spec cfg (the dial branch's contract) — before this, they were fed to the legacy keeper binary (which only reads ~/.vibespace/run) so the 2.218.0 keeper-attach optimization silently never worked over ssh and every such resume degraded to sweep+respawn. Legacy keeper sids (client discovery K lines) keep the old runTail; provisioning failure degrades to it too.** **NODE RESOLUTION (2.244.2-2.244.4, userN's Novita chicken-and-egg): hook entries register with the ABSOLUTE process.execPath (hooks run via /bin/sh with claude's PATH — nvm-managed node never resolves there, and claude is a native binary), and ALL FOUR register invocation sites (ssh prelude / dial install / manual Install / Remove) run a POSIX node finder (PATH → newest nvm → common paths) then EXPORT the found node dir onto the session PATH — a bare `node` invocation of the register silently no-op'd on dash hosts, so the fix could never apply itself AND every #!/usr/bin/env node agent tool was dead there. Any new remote code path invoking node must use the finder, never bare `node`.**

### data/bin/vibespace-task

data/bin/vibespace-task — Agent task-reporting CLI (STATIC tracked file, not generated — inline template-in-template escaping was unmaintainable): show [--full]/progress "summary" [--detail]/backlog/backlog-add/backlog-done/backlog-drop → /api/agent/task* (scoped to the session's own context task). **progress SUCCESS OUTPUT reminds the agent (2.210.0, the vibespace-ask 2.111.21 pattern): the activity log is agent-facing — deliverables (URLs/paths/results) must ALSO go in the chat reply (real pattern: agent logged a URL via progress and ended the turn without saying it).** **CHECKLIST REMOVED (2.121.0, user decision):** `plan-check/plan-uncheck/plan-add` are gone — a group-level list of agent WORK ITEMS never made sense (those live at the SESSION level = the agent's own todo list, surfaced as the card's Steps). The subcommands now print a redirect; `/api/agent/task-plan` answers old CLI copies (remote hosts) with 410 + guidance; stored `plan` arrays stay DORMANT in data/task-groups.json (never rendered/written; update() ignores plan patches; config-bundle import passes them through). **BACKLOG ADDED (2.122.0; CLAIM MODEL 2.123.0 — a DIFFERENT concept on the same shelf):** the group's PARKING LOT for NON-immediate items (deferred user decisions, "later" work; item = {**id: stable `B-xxxx`**, text, detail?, status open|done|dropped, **claimedBy: [session keys]**, addedBy/At, resolvedBy/At}); agents park via `backlog-add` when the user defers something (parking AUTO-CLAIMS for the caller) and are taught to NEVER start parked items unasked. Refs everywhere = id / 1-based OPEN-list index / unique substring; **the id travels** — the user copies it from the viewer and hands it to ANY member agent ("看一下 backlog B-ab12"), which runs `backlog <id>` (full item view, works on resolved items) and `backlog-claim <id>`. **INJECTION IS SUMMARY-ONLY + TARGETED (user directives): the hook never dumps the backlog** — renderContext gets `sessionKey` and emits a compact reminder block ONLY for open items THAT session CLAIMED (+ a one-line "N open — `vibespace-task backlog`" pointer otherwise); change events (PARKED/RESOLVED/DROPPED/CLAIMED/UNCLAIMED/reworded/REMOVED) ride the diff updates but reach ONLY sessions that created or claimed the item — diff matching is by the stable id (text edit ⇒ "reworded", not REMOVED+NEW). TASK.md/`show` carry the full open list with ids+claimants. One-time seed: dormant plan's unchecked items → open backlog items; id + creator-auto-claim backfill for pre-2.123.0 items (constructor, idempotent). **SUMMARY+DETAIL split (2.69.0):** progress entries carry a one-line `note` (what everyone sees inline — board rows, injected context marked `†`) + optional `detail` (≤6000 chars; TASK.md renders it as an indented blockquote, `show --full` prints it, the board's Activity rows expand on click) — this keeps the byte-budgeted injection dense without losing information. session-status records gained the same: `reason` = one-liner on chips, `detail` (≤2000) shown in Session Properties + `vibespace-status show`

### data/bin/vibespace-ask

data/bin/vibespace-ask — Agent CLI for the GLOBAL USER-TODO inbox (2.65.0, STATIC tracked): files items the USER must act on (decision/input/review) → POST /api/agent/user-todo (vsst_ scoped to own session). `"text" [--detail] [--urgency]` / list / resolve <id|text>; `help|-h|--help` and any flag-looking first arg print usage instead of filing (2.76.0 — a real agent filed the literal item "--help"); idempotent re-file by same text; the SUCCESS OUTPUT reminds the agent (2.111.21) that the inbox is only a notification mirror — the full content must ALSO go in the chat reply (real pattern: agents filed and ended the turn silently). **Semantics (2.76.1, user directive): MIRROR every chat question** — whenever an agent asks the user anything in chat / ends a turn waiting on them / sets needs-input|blocked|review, it must ALSO file the item (the user is often not watching that window), and the moment the user answers ANYWHERE (chat counts) the agent resolves it itself — taught in all three surfaces (SESSION_TOOLS_INTRO, renderContext tools section, the CLI's own usage text); taught in SESSION_TOOLS_INTRO + renderContext; distributed to remote hosts like the other tools

### data/bin/vibespace-status

data/bin/vibespace-status — Agent status tool (GENERATED at startup like data/bin/code — NEVER git-track it: tracking it dirtied the tree every boot and blocked `git pull --ff-only` in self-update (real incident 2.111.26); gitignored (NOTE: .gitignore has NO inline comments — a `path  # comment` line becomes the whole pattern and never matches, which silently re-tracked it once) and update.sh resets any regenerated tracked data/bin file before pulling. GENERATOR-TEMPLATE GOTCHA (P0 2.111.29): a shell example ending a single-quoted JS template string with `\\` rendered as `\'` → escaped the closing quote → every generated file was a SyntaxError and the whole CLI crashed on 2.111.24-28 — never end a JS template string with a bare backslash. WAITING STATES REQUIRE COMPLETE REASONS (2.111.24): blocked/needs-input/review need BOTH --reason (board chip) AND --detail (full context) — CLI pre-flight (show-lookup grace for same-state urgency tweaks) + agent-route both reject otherwise; done/working exempt; setting a waiting state prints a point-of-use reminder to also say it in chat + mirror via vibespace-ask)

### data/bin/vibespace-usage

data/bin/vibespace-usage — PASSIVE usage capture (STATIC tracked file, not generated; 2.60.0). A `statusLine` hook injected via `--settings` into LOCAL CLAUDE TERMINAL sessions only (2.62.0: the ws-handler injection is gated `backend==='claude'` — only the claude CLI understands `--settings`; un-gated it rode on `zsh -l` and codex spawns too, making every shell terminal (incl. the Manage-Agents Update/Login helpers) and local codex terminal exit instantly as "terminated"; NOT gated by the Integration master switch — 2.190.1 user decision: the switch scopes to AGENT-VISIBLE integration only, and the statusline is model-invisible plumbing) — the CLI feeds it the payload's `rate_limits` (5h/7d used_percentage+reset, "only after first API response" — and NOTHING else: model-scoped buckets like the Fable weekly cap are NOT in the statusline payload, verified vs the 2.1.206 payload builder) which it writes to data/usage-cache/<accountKey>.json (throttle 8s; **PRESERVES `scopedWeekly`/`scopedFetchedAt` from the existing file** — 2.70.0, clobbering them to [] every write is what made Fable vanish from the quota popup) + passes through the user's own statusline. Also harvests `model.id` → data/usage-cache/__models__.json (passive model discovery). ZERO Anthropic calls — this is why VibeSpace makes no background /api/oauth/usage poll by default (that off-CLI fixed-cadence sub-token call is the #1 ban trigger — see §ban-safety on accounts.js). Chat/stream-json has no statusline → those accounts show last-known.

### data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan

data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan — REMOTE-side usage ledger scanner (2.127.0, STATIC tracked; **PARITY-GUARDED since 2.275.0: it is a shipped SINGLE FILE (a host has no checkout ⇒ it cannot require the local walker), so it silently lagged its twin twice — the 2.265.0 subagents/workflows walk took 6 weeks to reach it. scripts/test-usage-walk-parity.mjs runs THE THREE walkers (local UsageHistory walk + this file + src/usage-walker.js) over one fixture and demands identical coverage + incremental-cursor behaviour; negative-controlled against the pre-2.271.0 walk. Any one-sided change fails it.**; shipped over ssh stdin per harvest, never argv): walks the host's ~/.claude/projects with remote byte cursors (~/.vibespace/usage-cursor.json), emits per-request NDJSON events (claude-only v1); cursor persists ONLY after stdout fully flushed (interrupted transfer ⇒ re-emit, rid-dedup absorbs). **R4 step 1 (2.286.0): daemon hosts prefer the `usage-scan` OP instead — src/usage-walker.js is the walk as a module BUNDLED into the daemon, run in a child process (single-artifact rule: the daemon re-execs its own bundle with `--usage-scan-child`), events streamed count-gated; the walker NEVER persists its cursor — hosts.harvestUsage commits it back over the device link (fsWrite+fsRename) only after the full transfer landed (two-phase; the script's flush-then-persist model LOST events on relayed dial paths when the link died mid-transfer with the cursor already advanced). Per-op capability gating: the daemon hello-ack `capabilities` array (['probe','transcript-op','usage-scan']) — consumers gate on it and throw fast, NEVER parse daemonVersion (unknown ops on old daemons get no reply = a hang until timeout). Fallback ladder: op → script-ship over the device link → ssh. E2E: scripts/test-usage-scan-op.mjs (13 asserts, real daemon).** **R4 step 2 (2.287.0): ①PUSH-triggered harvest — the daemon's discovery watch (now also `.codex/sessions`) pushes debounced `discovery-dirty`; hosts._armDirtyPush (armed at every _deviceConnect; client re-arms on reconnect like reverse forwards) fans it to discovery cache invalidation + server-side per-host debounced harvest kick (60s floor unchanged) — remote ledger/billing-popup freshness ≈1min for ANY activity incl. mid-turn/codex/external; the dirty signal previously had NO consumer. ②CODEX ROLLOUTS in all three walkers (synthetic rid = cumulative total, turn_context model/cwd persisted in cursor, input−cached split, heartbeats skipped, CODEX_HOME honored); ingestRemoteEvents honors `be` — remote codex usage lands in the ledger. Codex parity leg in test-usage-walk-parity (21).** Harvest chain: hosts.harvestUsage (15min/host idle throttle; **EVENT-DRIVEN since 2.284.3: a remote session's `result` kicks a prompt harvest with a 60s/host floor** — the ledger/billing-popup lag is ~1min, not 15; the pool control loop never depended on this cadence, it eats relayed stdout live via noteLive) → usageHistory.ingestRemoteEvents (**PER-ACCOUNT since 2.294.0**: resolves the real account by the SAME by-time attribution walk local events use — VibeSpace records the spawn account for remote sessions too, the ingest just discarded it; pool tag included. Only sessions VibeSpace never spawned (external terminals, pre-attribution) keep acct=<hostId>/atype='host'/billing 'remote-host', which now honestly means 'that machine's own login'. No back-billing past the grace window; `host` field always kept so the device dimension still works; rid namespaced `h:<id>:`; host-bucket events stay EXEMPT from _loadEvents re-attribution — the local log would re-bucket them to global) → POST /api/usage-stats/harvest-hosts (kicked on Usage-window open, reload on added>0). Quota side: /api/usage/refresh {host} = on-demand ⟳ with hosts.readRemoteOAuth (READ-ONLY remote token peek, never refresh, §ban-safety) → usage-cache/host-<id>.json → GET /api/usage `hosts` field (every configured host listed) → popup "Remote hosts" section (bars + per-host ⟳, class usage-host-refresh). **Walker v3 (codex per-message meta, 2026-09-06; mirrored line-for-line from src/usage-walker.js): codex events ADD `mid` = the `response_id` of the 0.153 `token_usage_record` that PRECEDES the token_count (parked in the CURSOR as `pendMid`/`pendTotal` so a harvest landing between the pair still pairs on the next run; adopted only when its `usage.total_tokens` equals `last_token_usage.total_tokens`; pre-0.153 rollouts get no mid — never invented) and `effort` (turn_context.effort, persisted like model). `rid` is UNCHANGED — it is the dedup key already baked into every permanent ledger; changing it would double-count every scanned rollout. Parity leg 21→32 incl. the scan-boundary case.**

### data/bin/vibespace-remote-keeper

data/bin/vibespace-remote-keeper — REMOTE-side persistence for remote CHAT sessions (2.124.0, STATIC tracked; distributed to remote ~/.vibespace/bin with the other tools): claude runs setsid-DETACHED on the host under a keeper daemon (stdout→buffer file, stdin←unix socket) so an ssh drop kills only the pipe; the local chat-wrapper reattaches with a byte offset (`run <sid> <offset> -- cmd`; stdout carries ONLY raw buffer bytes — diagnostics on stderr), `_remote_exit` sentinel = claude really ended (run never restarts an exited sid); stop mode = the kill path. See the 远程session稳定性 bullet + scripts/test-remote-keeper.mjs

### transcript-service.js

  transcript-service.js — ONE interface over the transcript parse stack (R3 of docs/design-three-tier.md; 2.283.0 step 1 = server-hosted, HTTP read family rerouted through it). **SWITCHED LIVE in 2.292.0** — remote page/turnmap/searchIndexed/status/taskState run as `transcript-op` on the owning machine (parsed KBs on the wire, no whole-file pull). LADDER not replacement: device op → transcript-cache pull + local parse → honest error; capability-gated (an old daemon is never asked — unknown ops hang), failures log throttled and degrade. TWO CARVE-OUTS, both load-bearing: ① a LIVE session never reads from the device (the server owns the stdout buffer overlay + live normalizer — the device would drop the un-flushed tail); ② the huge-file SEEK family switched ATOMICALLY in 2.293.0 once the daemon gained a `searchFull` op — `gapInfo` returns an OPAQUE HANDLE (`\0device:<host>` or the local path) that every follow-up (slab/search/turnmap) honours, so line numbers never come from two sources (the route already treated fp as opaque, so nothing above the service changed); device streaming search degrades honestly to one batch. Ladder test: scripts/test-transcript-switchover.mjs (7). **2.285.0 step 2: the DEVICE DAEMON hosts the SAME service** — agentd's `transcript-op` op (lazy-constructed, no buffer overlay device-side — the stdout overlay stays a server concern) runs page/turnmap/searchIndexed/status/taskState/gapInfo/gapSlab/fullTurnmap next to the bytes and streams JSON over a count-gated byte channel (read-range contract); client = DeviceManager.transcriptOp. DARK: no production consumer until the parity substrate soaks (retirement-order law). scripts/test-transcript-parity.mjs (19 asserts) pins BYTE-IDENTICAL results across in-process vs real-daemon — incl. >½MB multi-window payloads, a ~36MB fixture's real seek family, codex, and honest unknown-method/missing-file shapes. Its first run caught the tool-card ts bug (below). SafeFs/transcript-worker degrade INLINE inside the bundle (worker file absent next to the bundle → spawn fails → sync fallback, by design). **B-21e4 item 5:** `view()` warms through `harnessOf(backend).store.warmTranscript(id, cwd, {remote})` (claude parse-cache warm as before; codex thread/read fallback for a missing rollout, local only) — no backend ternary.

### session-store.js

  session-store.js     — SessionMessages + JSONL parsing + session discovery helpers. **Merge is POSITION-PRESERVING (2.74.0):** surviving buffer records interleave at their true chronological spot (anchor = jsonl index of the last buffer record that deduped; a rotated buffer's unmatched head anchors just before the first match) instead of all appending at the end — end-append made an EARLIER turn's stream-json-only `result` replay AFTER a still-running tool_use, flushing the live pending tool card to ✗ Interrupted on every server restart (real report), and scrambled turn boundaries in the replay

### codex-chat-wrapper.js (P2 verbs, 2.369.20)

  data/bin/codex-chat-wrapper.js — chat-input: slash commands are served by the WRAPPER (`applySlashCommand`: /compact → `thread/compact/start` (real compaction; `context_compacted` from the contextCompaction item or the RPC), /review → review/start uncommittedChanges, /model + /effort → the set-* verbs, `command_applied` notice), then **queue-while-busy**: `meta.activeTurnId` set ⇒ `thread/queue/add` + `queued_input` event, never `turn/start` (codex steers a regular turn / rejects review+compact turns). Both `wrapper_meta` records advert `slashCommands`; CodexMessageManager stores them in `_status.slashCommands`, fills the init card and patches it in place (edit op) when they arrive after the card. Live item visibility: mcpToolCall/dynamicToolCall/webSearch/imageView/contextCompaction are recorded as function_call/function_call_output twins (names chosen for collapseKindOf: `mcp__<server>__<tool>` → mcp fold; web_search → search fold, view_image → image fold since 2.369.33/34). The imageView stub records `{path}` only while codex's own rollout copy of the same call carries `{path, detail}` and an `[{input_image, image_url:data:…}]` output twin — the normalizer's call_id dedupe + input_image lifting (2.369.43) is what keeps that ONE card; the stub contract itself is pinned by test-codex-p2-wrapper and test-image-cards.. **Race guard**: `turn/completed` can be processed before the `turn/start` reply's promise resolves (same stdout chunk) — `completedTurns` makes startTurn skip re-marking a finished turn active. Pins: scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs (30); test-chat-frame-guard's stub completes turns at once. **Fork echo (B-21e4):** the wrapper's session_meta record dedupes `forked_from` (`[...new Set(chain)]`, own id dropped) and echoes `forked_from_id` from the thread/start|resume|fork response (`Thread.forkedFromId`, 0.153.4 bindings); the fork BOUNDARY ordinal is not on the wire — only the rollout's own session_meta carries it, and extractCodexThreadMeta reads it from the file. **webSearch completion = codex's OWN rollout shape (2.369.43, owner: every card read `{"query":"","action":null}` / "(empty)"):** the v2 WebSearchItem is an EMPTY stub at item/started (query '', action null — the function_call recorded there is the pending card and stays so) and complete only at item/completed, which the wrapper records as `event_msg web_search_end {call_id, query, action, results?, error?}` (key order mirrors codex-rs; `results` omitted when the item has none, `error` only when present) — NOT a function_call_output of raw JSON. The normalizer renders it (src/search-card.js), so the live record and the rollout's own web_search_end are one renderer and one card (the two copies fingerprint-dedupe on re-attach or edit the same call_id in place). Pin: test-codex-p2-wrapper (stub emits the real started-stub + completed pair; asserts the event shape, key order, no output twin, ONE complete card with the rendered result list). **imageView parity (2026-09-06):** a real `ImageView` item carries a `file://` URL (48/48 in the 0.153.4 corpus) and the normalizer strips it when it routes the rollout's own `item_completed` copy — the wrapper's live `view_image` twin strips it too, or the ONE card (both copies use the item id) rewrote its own text on re-attach. Pin: the same suite asserts the stripped path in the live `function_call` arguments + output AND that feeding the rollout copy afterwards leaves one card with unchanged text. **The 0.153.4 rollout writes NO `web_search_end`** (it persists the search as `item_completed` `Extension web.search`) — the wrapper's `web_search_end` is a LIVE-only relay of the same v2 item under the same `exec-…` id; see the cli_version table in the codex-message-manager.js essay before changing either side. **THE INPUT QUEUE — QUEUED vs STEERED (2026-09-06, owner ask; §queue-steer):** the wrapper OWNS the queue but does not KEEP it — `thread/queue/list` is the truth, re-read on every `thread/queue/changed` (the notification carries only `{threadId}`) and published as ONE typed event `queue_changed {items:[{id, msgId, preview≤120, ts, kind, from}], turn_id}` (fingerprint-deduped; also at boot and at each `turn/started`, so a client attaching mid-turn can rebuild the strip from the buffer). `queueMeta` (clientUserMessageId → {kind, msgId, ts, from}) is registered BEFORE the `thread/queue/add` RPC — the change notification's refresh can beat the add's own reply, and an item whose msgId we learn late renders with no bubble chip. The `queue-op` stdin verb serves `steer` / `remove` / `steer-all`. **MEASURED ON A LIVE 0.153.4 app-server, all four load-bearing (the design assumed a `thread/queue/remove` that does not exist):** ① the removal verb is `thread/queue/delete {threadId, queuedSubmissionId}` → `{deleted:true}`; the ClientRequest enum (printed verbatim by the server's own unknown-method error) is add/list/update/delete/reorder/start — **no `remove`** ② `turn/steer {threadId, input, expectedTurnId, clientUserMessageId}` → `{turnId}`, and it does **NOT** dequeue the item even when it carries that item's own clientUserMessageId ⇒ the wrapper deletes it or the message runs twice ③ several `turn/steer` calls inside ONE turn are accepted ⇒ `steer-all` is sequential steers in queue order, each keeping its own input (images + per-message ids survive), never one concatenated blob ④ a `thread/queue/add` on an IDLE thread starts a turn by itself — the app-server drains its own queue, we never call `turn/start` for a drained item. **ORDER IS STEER-FIRST, DELETE-ON-SUCCESS:** a steer failing is a DESIGNED path (`cannot steer a review turn` / `cannot steer a compact turn` = CodexErrorInfo::ActiveTurnNotSteerable, and `expected active turn id \`X\` but found \`Y\`` when the turn ended between the click and the RPC), and on that path the item must stay queued IN PLACE — which delete-first could not restore (re-adding appends). The wire carries only `{code:-32600, message}` (the typed CodexErrorInfo variants never reach the client), so `classifySteerFailure` discriminates on the message TEXT — deliberately, with the strings quoted from the binary's own table. Every outcome rides `queue_op_result {op, id, msg_id, ok, reason, detail}`; a steer that succeeded but whose delete failed reports `ok:true, reason:'steered-not-dequeued'` so the user is warned about a possible double run instead of it happening silently. A REFUSED `steer-all` emits ONLY the per-item result (which carries the real reason AND the real turn kind) and logs the abort to the wrapper journal — the `{op:'steer-all', ok:false, reason}` summary it used to emit as well rendered a SECOND card from the same failure, and, carrying no `kind`, made the normalizer name a compact turn "review" (round-1 review). The `ok:true, done:N` summary stays: the normalizer renders no card for a successful op. **STOP CLEARS THE QUEUE (owner decision 2026-09-07 — the ACP divergence is closed):** the `interrupt` verb runs `clearQueueForStop()` FIRST and only then `turn/interrupt`. Order is load-bearing twice: ① the app-server drains its own queue when a turn ends **including a turn ended by an interrupt** (measured; the stub keeps draining on interrupt on purpose, so a delete-after-interrupt implementation fails the suite for the RIGHT reason — the negative control leaves the session streaming a drained turn), and ② each `queue_op_result {op:'remove', ok:true, msg_id, reason:'stopped'}` is emitted BEFORE the emptied republish, because the normalizer clears the chip of anything that left the queue with no result (= "it ran") — the ACP round-1 lesson, same frame, same reason. `queueSweepActive` makes the per-delete `thread/queue/changed` refreshes no-ops while the sweep runs (they would overtake the results); the sweep ends with its own `refreshQueue()`, so an item whose delete FAILED is still listed — that failure is reported (`ok:false, reason:'error', detail`) and journalled, never a silently "cleared" queue. A dropped PEER/job entry gets the explicit-remove path's treatment verbatim (`peer_message_result ok:false` ⇒ the delivery ladder re-stashes it). Deliberate codex-side difference from ACP: no extra "Stop also dropped N queued messages" notice — every dropped codex item owns a bubble whose chip flips to `Removed`. Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper (a stub app-server implementing the measured queue: two queued → steer #2 → turn/steer carries expectedTurnId + only #2's input, #1 keeps its place, the steered copy is DELETED; remove; steer-all in order; a review turn refuses and the item stays; a turn ending ON ITS OWN drains and the chip clears; then Stop with two queued (one user, one peer) → exactly two deletes, both BEFORE the single turn/interrupt, two `reason:'stopped'` removals ahead of the empty republish, the peer re-stash frame, `queueState` `removed` through the real normalizer, and NOTHING running afterwards) + test-queue-steer (the framework rows + both wrappers' Stop pins).

  **THREAD GATE (B-7473, the owner's "根本没区分出这是subagent消息"):** the app-server relays notifications for EVERY thread it hosts, and the old `item/started|completed` handler took `params.item` and IGNORED `params.threadId` — a CHILD's `agentMessage` was recorded as a ROOT `message role=assistant` (forensics: production buffer `sess-4-1788639964629.buf` line 414 is `/root/usecases_v4`'s FINAL_ANSWER; the root rollout holds the same text as an `agent_message` from that child ⇒ the owner also saw it TWICE). Now: **the gate is INVERTED (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06)** — the question is not "is this method in my list of thread-scoped methods" (a method whitelist goes stale: `error`, `thread/compacted`, `thread/queue/changed` and `turn/diff/updated` all carry `threadId` in the 0.153.4 bindings and were ALL missing from the first cut, so a CHILD's error notification became the ROOT's `task_failed` + error card + turn end) but "does this notification NAME a thread, and is it mine": any `params.threadId !== meta.threadId` is FOREIGN unless the method is in the (today empty, documented) `THREAD_ID_NOT_SCOPE` allowlist; `thread/started`/`thread/resumed` are handled ABOVE the gate because they are what TEACHES us our id, and a notification arriving before `meta.threadId` is known cannot be judged and passes. A foreign `error` is recorded as the CHILD's `sub_agent_activity {kind:'errored', detail:<message>}` — a collab activity row, never this conversation's failure. Every foreign notification passes `foreignThreadOf(params)` FIRST; a foreign-thread `agentMessage` becomes an attributed `agent_message` record (`{type, id, thread_id, author, recipient, content:[output_text], phase, delivery, msg_type}`, author from `meta.subagents` = the agentPath→threadId map learned from `subAgentActivity`, else the thread id), every other foreign kind is DROPPED with a once-per-thread log + a `meta.foreignDrops{total, threads{<tid>:{dropped, agentMessages, agentPath}}}` counter. An OWN-thread `agentMessage` whose text starts with the inter-agent envelope (`Message Type: … / Task name: … / Sender: /root/x`) is ALSO an `agent_message` (author = the Sender line) — it was written FOR another agent, never a reply to the user. **The ENVELOPE is the only signal; `delivery === 'async'` is NOT (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06, MAJOR):** on an own-thread `agentMessage` that flag marks a question/async reply the USER is meant to read, and treating it as inter-agent DROPPED the text from the transcript entirely (an asked question simply vanished). Pinned with the stub: an own-thread `delivery:'async'` message with no envelope must appear as an assistant message. **`meta.agentPath`** (this thread's own path, the reference point that makes a message inbound or outbound) is ASSIGNED from the thread/start|resume reply when it carries one and echoed in the wrapper's `session_meta` record — it used to be READ in three places and never written; 0.153.4's `Thread` struct carries agentNickname/agentRole/parentThreadId but NO agentPath (checked against the installed binary's serde field list), so the '/root' default is what a VibeSpace-spawned thread actually is, and a RESUMED sub-agent conversation gets its real path from codex's own `session_meta` at line 0 of its rollout. `subAgentActivity` items fill the map and emit the live `event_msg sub_agent_activity` (the 0.153.4 rollout carries the same fact as `item_completed/SubAgentActivity`; the normalizer routes both to ONE path). Every item record now rides `recordItem()`, which stamps the notification's `thread_id`/`turn_id` — **both are stripped from `recordFingerprint` (codex-session-store) and `CodexMessageManager.recordKey`, or every buffer record becomes a stranger to its rollout twin (double cards on each attach)**. `caps.threadScoped: true` adverts it. `AGENT_ENVELOPE_RE`/`parseAgentEnvelope` are MIRRORED in src/codex-message-manager.js (a shipped single file cannot require it) — change one, change both. Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper ④ (stub pushes a child agentMessage / an own-thread envelope one / a subAgentActivity / a plain root reply ⇒ exactly ONE assistant record). RESIDUE: records a PRE-B-7473 wrapper already wrote keep their root-assistant shape (the normalizer cannot tell them from a real reply) — they age out with the buffer; only NEW traffic is gated, and a restart of that session is the immediate cure.

### plugin-loader.js / plugin-manifest.js / plugin-client.js (Plugin Ph2 minimal, 2.369.24)

  The plugin system's first runtime slice (docs/design-harness-plugins.md §3.2–3.3). **Trust model as shipped**: iframe tier = untrusted UI (opaque origin, the published-pages CSP posture — NEVER add allow-same-origin; the bridge exposes only namespaced localStorage, toasts, close); server tier = a separate PROCESS with an IPC-only API (`route`/`tool`/`shutdown`) — plugin code is never require()d into the orchestrator; agent tools = generated shims that carry the calling session's vsst_ token to `/api/agent/plugin-tool/<id>/<name>` (cookie-exempt prefix ⇒ the route validates the bearer itself; unknown token = 401). Invariants: an invalid manifest is LISTED with its errors (never silently skipped) and cannot be enabled; enable/disable is atomic + broadcast; a crashing plugin restarts with backoff and is parked after 5 crashes/10min (never a crash loop against the box); shims are regenerated on every change and removed on disable; `hostVersion` is read from `<rootDir>/package.json` (never a relative require — the 2.341.1 lost-binding class). Trusted client modules, contributed settings/themes/keybindings, install UI, capability enforcement and remote shim shipping are the next slices (B-bb56/B-93f9). **SECURITY POSTURE after the 2.369.43 review (kb-bugfix-invariants “PLUGIN SECURITY BATCH”): a manifest is untrusted input — its free text may reach generated code only as a JSON literal (jsLiteral) and is sanitized once in the validator (cleanText); a declared fs path is collapsed — and its `*` wildcard MODELLED as the string prefix node actually matches (2.369.44) — before the forbidden-root test; contributed agent tools NEED consent (a shim is a program on every session PATH, outside the sandbox — the shipped hello-plugin example is consent-gated now); consent is recorded per PACKAGE (contentHash + source) and the reset is keyed on the REGISTRY RECORD, not on a folder having been replaced (2.369.44 — consent outlives a hand-deleted plugin dir), so replacing the code behind a trusted id disables it and re-prompts; proxied /x/* replies are data (nosniff + sandbox CSP, no document content-type from an untrusted plugin).** Pins: scripts/test-plugin-security.mjs (62) + scripts/test-plugin-loader.mjs (43: validator matrix + the shipped docs/examples/hello-plugin driven end to end — assets/CSP/traversal, IPC routes, tool + shim + 401, lifecycle).

### window-types.js (client window-type registry, Plugin Ph1)

  window-types.js — the first client contribution-point registry (2.369.19, docs/design-harness-plugins.md §3.2 `windows[]`): `registerWindowType({ type, icon (svg), label, replay(app, spec) → winInfo|null, persist?, singleton? })`, `getWindowType`, `windowTypeIcon`, `replayOpenSpec(app, spec)`. replayOpenSpec used to be an 18-case switch with no default — an unknown `openSpec.action` was silently dropped; now it warns + emits telemetry `openspec-unknown`. Core registers every existing kind at module load in the module that owns the window (session-lifecycle chat/terminal, file-viewer/editor, usage/settings/task/workflow/session-props/browser/jobs/desktop…), byte-identical behaviour; `TYPE_ICONS` (tab-group.js) is now a live view over the registry so existing importers keep working. A plugin's `windows[]` contribution will call the same registerWindowType. Pins: scripts/test-window-types.mjs (53: DOM-free node import, fake type dispatch, unknown-action warning, the exact core type/action sets, no switch/TYPE_ICONS literal left).

### contributions.js / gear-menu.js (client commands + menus + keybindings registry, Plugin Ph1)

  contributions.js — the second client contribution-point registry (docs/design-harness-plugins.md §3.2 `commands[]` + `menus[] {where, when, group}` + `keybindings[]`), built to window-types.js's rules: DOM-free at import (only telemetry-client), node-imported directly by its gate. `registerCommand({ id, title (string | ctx→string), run(ctx), when?(ctx), icon?, signal? })`, `registerMenuItem({ menu, command | label+run, id?, group?, order?, when?, label?, labelHtml?, disabled?, style?, tooltip?, children?, expand?, separator?, kind?, icon?, danger?, keepOpen?, decorate?, signal? })`, `menuItems(menu, ctx)` → showContextMenu-shaped items, `registerKeybinding({ key, command, when?, inTerminal?, signal? })` + `installKeybindings(document, { signal, getCtx })` (ONE bubble-phase dispatcher, app-lifetime AbortController in app.js), `runCommand(id, ctx)`. **Semantics (do not regress):** items sort by group ('navigation' first, then lexicographic), then `order`, then registration; `when` gates SURFACES (menus/keys) — `runCommand` never consults it (VS Code executeCommand); **SEPARATORS ARE EXPLICIT contributions** (VS Code's automatic between-group rule cannot reproduce the hand-built window menu, which joins Minimize and Close with NO rule when the session block is absent) and leading/trailing/doubled ones collapse; an EMPTY `children` result drops the item (the legacy `if (groups.length)` guards); `mod` = ctrl OR meta (the app's `(e.ctrlKey || e.metaKey)` convention), unlisted modifiers must be up; keys inside `.xterm` never match unless `inTerminal`; later bindings win. **Loud:** duplicate/malformed registrations THROW at module load; `runCommand` of an unknown id THROWS + telemetry `command-unknown`; an unknown command named by a menu item / keybinding warns once + telemetry (`menu-unknown-command` / `keybinding-unknown-command`) and is skipped; a throwing `when()` hides the item and warns once; a throwing keybound command is logged + `command-failed`. Every register* returns dispose() and honours `signal` — the plugin host API (plugin-client.js `api.registerCommand/registerMenuItem/registerKeybinding/runCommand`, ids namespaced `plugin:<id>:<slug>`) binds all three to the plugin's AbortController so contributions leave with the plugin. **Core dogfoods it, byte-identical:** the sidebar card menu = `session.*` commands + `registerSessionCardMenu()` in session-card.js (ctx `{ app, state, settings, s, card, event, displayName, customName, originalName, onRename, agentOpts }`); the window title-bar/taskbar/window-list menu = `window.*` commands + `registerWindowMenu()` in taskbar.js (ctx `{ app, id, win, s, switchSubmenu, closeLabel }`; `showWindowContextMenu`'s wrapAct fires `onAction(kind)` after items AND submenu children — Task Groups / Move to Desktop — never the Switch-window submenu; the session block REUSES `session.restart` (Restart / Resume labels via the item label override), `session.locate`, `session.properties`); the ⚙ menu = gear-menu.js `registerGearMenu()` (groups 0_prefs/1_admin/2_maint/2p_plugins(expand: plugin windows)/3_help, `decorate(el, ctx)` for the Language sub-menu row and the two-line Update row, `buildGearMenu(app, pop)` renders with escHtml'd labels; mobile-nav's gear shares it via `_showGlobalSettings`). Command mode registers every single-key action as `activeWindow.*`/`layout.*`/`desktop.*`/`session.new`/`sidebar.toggle`/`browser.open`/`explorer.open`/`commandMode.toggle`; the palette registers `session.palette`. **Their KEY DISPATCH deliberately stays where it was** (NOT registerKeybinding): the legacy checks are modifier-LENIENT (Ctrl+K opens on Ctrl+Shift+K too; Ctrl+\ ignores shift) and run in the capture phase in a load-bearing listener order, while the registry matcher is strict and bubble-phase — migrating them would change behaviour. Each register block is self-contained (`export function registerX() {…} registerX(); // end registerX`) so the gate can extract + replay it with its closed-over names injected. File-explorer menus (file-explorer-ops.js) are NOT migrated (out of the slice). Gate: scripts/test-contributions.mjs (functional in node + the three menus diffed against VERBATIM legacy builders over a state matrix + the REAL ws-handler default + wiring pins).

### ws-handler.js (heartbeat + attach rebuild)

  ws-handler.js — **`default:` case (Plugin Ph1): the `switch (data.type)` had NO default, so an unknown type vanished — no log, no telemetry, no reply, the sender waited forever.** Now `noteUnknownWsType(telemetry, type)` logs once per type per 10 minutes with the running count (a chatty stale client cannot flood the journal), records telemetry `ws-unknown-type` per occurrence, and replies `{ type:'error', code:'unknown-type', message, unknownType, reqId? }` — **deliberately WITHOUT sessionId**: ws.js routes session-scoped handlers by `d.sessionId`, and every client `error` consumer (chat-view's branch, terminal's per-session handler, session-lifecycle's create/attach/view handlers) keys on `msg.sessionId`/`msg.reqId` — a session-scoped error frame is what flips a live window into the exited/read-only path, and an unknown TYPE is not a dead session. `reqId` IS echoed so a `ws.request()` caller fails fast. `telemetry` joined WS_CTX_CONTRACT for it.

  ws-handler.js — **2.369.16 (userW inc-mtndq0vb): the first-attach history rebuild is TIME-SLICED and single-flight (`normalizers.rebuildHistory` → `convertHistoryAsync`, ~25ms slices yielding to the loop; concurrent attaches await one promise; live records arriving mid-rebuild queue on `session._rebuildQueue` and replay in order; `_historyLoaded` set after success). Every live record enters a session normalizer through `normalizers.feedLive(session, msg)` — a direct `session._normalizer.processLive` call is a gate bypass (test-attach-rebuild counts them = 0). Rebuilds are FIFO (one at a time — fair slicing landed every window at the total time) and the attach re-acks every 10s with `{progress}` while waiting/converting (client ladder extends on fresh acks, 15min bound), then re-checks the session is still live before sending `attached`. The heartbeat lives in src/server/ws-heartbeat.js and is STALL-AWARE with TWO detectors: tick lateness AND a 1s loop-gap pulse (a 30–35s block starting right after a ping ends with the tick only seconds late but the pong unread — the review's blind band); a tainted round terminates nobody, bounded at 6 consecutive pong-less tainted rounds; terminations are logged + telemetered. The old inline interval terminated a live client after a 4-minute rebuild stall and dropped its queued kills. `kill` replies `killed {sessionId: REQUESTED id, resolvedId, ok, reason}` to the requester in both outcomes (`exited` only reaches attached clients) — the client's `killSession` sends the backendSessionId fallback frame as a one-shot and resend-chases only the plain kill (a resent fallback would kill the resumed successor), with its watchdog disarming the request.**
  ws-handler.js — **B-21e4 item 5:** the first-attach warm and the view-only path both call `harnessOf(backend).store?.warmTranscript(id, cwd, {remote: !!host})` — the descriptor's pre-read hook (claude: worker parse-cache warm, unchanged; codex: the 0.153 thread/read fallback when the rollout file is missing, local only). The view-only path now warms claude too (a cache warm before the same sync parse — the 2.235.0 optimization, not a behaviour change).

### message-manager.js

  message-manager.js   — MessageManager (Claude stream-json → normalized messages with stable IDs). **Tool messages take `ts` from `_currentTs` (the record's own timestamp), never bare Date.now() (2.285.0, caught by the transcript parity suite's first run): the hand-rolled tool-message path bypassed _create's ts ladder, so every REBUILD-rendered tool card (restart/view-only/resume) was timestamped with the rebuild time — msg-meta popup showed the wrong time for all historical tool calls.** **convertHistoryAsync (2.369.16) = the time-sliced twin used by the ws attach path (both normalizers; parity-pinned byte-identical to convertHistory); callers hold live records back while it runs (feedLive gate).** **convertHistory is per-record isolated + _historyLoaded set AFTER successful rebuild (2.89.2)** — a single throwing record amputated every restart-rebuilt view at that point and the flag-before-work made it permanent (real fleet-wide incident: the 2.80.0 `output`→`raw.output` hook-card typo threw on every buffered hook_response; live path swallowed it per-line so it hid for 9 releases). **queued_command attachments = mid-turn user messages (2.88.0)**: the JSONL's ONLY record of a message sent while the agent works — normalized into a real user message (typed-flagged, echo-deduped by exact text vs the last 12) or the user's words vanish from every JSONL-rebuilt history (restart/resume/view-only; real incident, 211 records). msg.typed (promptSource/_fromWebui) beats notification text-shape in renderUserMsg; msg.synthetic (isSynthetic) forces it **任务生命周期独立索引(2.368.15): 一切task closer不得经pendingToolCalls找卡——tool_result秒删表项而完成通知晚数分钟(每对话都攒永久running卡的根因); taskMsgByToolUse/taskMsgByTaskId整会话存活, _taskMsgFor三级解析; test-task-wakeup-card钉真实record顺序(tool_result在通知之前)。** **历史可用的生命周期真源(2.368.30, owner: c1206711很多subagent任务没识别): task_started/task_progress/task_notification系统subtype只在活流上存在——602MB现场转录里为0条——所以resume后一切派生自它们的状态全灭; 落盘的是启动ACK与<task-notification>用户记录, parseBackgroundLaunch(PURE导出, session-store taskState扫描共用, 无twin)从ACK合成taskInfo(Agent的agentId行/Workflow的Run ID/后台Bash的Command running行), 通知记录关闭并带summary(卡片优先显示); 幽灵裁剪=合成running且启动时间早于当前wrapper startedAt者删除(OS任务活不过CLI进程, 现场16个僵尸watcher); test-task-lifecycle(19, 真实转录fixture)。**通知有三条运输(2.368.31, owner复报'又大量running'): 空闲=user记录, 忙碌=queue-operation(enqueue/remove)+attachment(queued_command)——重工作流会话几乎全走后者, closer只认user记录时17个phantom; _closeTaskFromNotification统一三运输(normalizer+scan), queued投递渲染notification卡而非'You'XML气泡; resumeFromRunId同id再启动supersede原卡; taskState()=scan打底+wrapper覆盖MERGE绝非fallback(1个活条目曾顶掉全部41个, 双形态载荷律第二击)。** 2.369.35 LAW: binary never enters a normalized message — splitToolResultContent lifts image blocks out of tool results as {mediaType, bytes} (a Read of a PNG returned ~600KB base64 per card; stringified into `output` it made a 12MB attach payload and froze the renderer — two windows 'stuck opening'); chat-renderers shows the file from disk (/api/file/raw) or a size chip. **2.369.48 (owner: "view image 能不能也多媒体化"): the lifted `images` now drive a MEDIA CARD** — `imageMediaHtml` in chat-renderers renders every image a tool looked at (claude Read of a png/jpg — whose branch had returned BEFORE the generic thumbnail splice, so the exact case 2.369.35 was built for never showed a thumbnail, and the splice's URL carried a stray `''` — codex view_image, MCP tools naming an image file) as an OPEN `<details class="chat-media">` with the `/api/file/raw` thumbnail (lazy; `&host=` from the renderer's `_sessionCtx()` for remote sessions) + the standard .chat-img zoom; no path on disk = size chip; a failed load flips `.chat-media-broken` through ChatView's capture-phase error delegate ("Image not available on this machine"). **THE LIFTED BLOCKS DECIDE, NEVER THE EXTENSION** (a refuted first cut classified a `Read` by `IMAGE_EXT_RE` and dropped the source silently): `viewsImage` for a Read is `images.length > 0` and nothing else — claude returns numbered TEXT for a text-source image format, measured for .svg (real fleet shape, cli 2.1.85: all 10 local `Read *.svg` tool_results are plain strings starting `"1\t<svg …"`; .ico is the same class, unmeasured), which keeps its ordinary code-block card and only gains a thumbnail appended BELOW it because the browser can draw that type. `IMAGE_EXT_RE`/`isImagePath` answer ONE question — can the browser decode this as an `<img>`? — so they gate thumbnail-vs-chip only: a tiff/heic Read that DID return image blocks renders the image card with the type/size chip and NO `<img>` (an unrenderable format must not become a broken thumbnail). The SAME evidence rule governs the fold: `messageKind` (src/lib/chat-run-summary.js) calls a Read 'image' only when `content[0].images` is non-empty, so a text `.svg` read is counted and folded as an ordinary file read (image-card review round 2, 2026-09-06). Gate: scripts/test-image-cards.mjs against the REAL renderer module in node (both directions pinned, negative-controlled).
  ws-handler.js — **`queue-op` (2026-09-06, the ONE new case for QUEUED vs STEERED):** `{op:'steer'|'remove'|'steer-all', id}` validated against `capsOf(session.backend).inputModes` — a CAPABILITY, never a backend id (the frame-file-by-backend-id incident's rule). `queueOps:false` ⇒ "<Harness> owns its own input queue"; `steer` on a `steer:false` harness ⇒ "<Harness> cannot steer: a message sent during a turn runs after it. You can remove it instead." `adapter.formatQueueOp` is the second line of defense (its throw becomes the same refusal). **A SECOND GATE, the RUNNING WRAPPER:** `wrapperCaps(BUFFERS_DIR, id, socketPath).inputQueue` (the sidecar advert the wrapper itself writes) — or, for a REMOTE wrapper whose sidecar lives on ITS OWN machine, the in-band proof `session._normalizer.queuePublished()` — must also be true — the caps row describes a KIND of agent, while a dtach session started before this release satisfies the row and would have its `queue-op` frame dropped SILENTLY. Its refusal names the wrapper's start time and says "Terminate + Resume the session to get the controls"; the verdict is re-read every time (never cached negative — a wrapper resuming a huge thread writes its sidecar late; the 2.364.1 law). Every refusal is `{type:'error', code:'queue-op-unsupported', scope:'action', sessionId, error, message}` — and the CLIENT rule is an EXPLICIT SET, not "anything coded": **a per-session `error` is a scoped refusal (renders in chat, window untouched) when it carries `scope:'action'` or one of `SCOPED_REFUSAL_CODES` (input-rejected, not-codex-chat, queue-op-unsupported); everything else is the attach failure it used to be read as** (inc-mt2arppw was `input-rejected`; `not-codex-chat` had been read-only'ing live windows the same way ever since — but `ended-during-attach`, raised right here after the history rebuild, is CODED AND FATAL and must keep taking the view-only rescue; round-1 review caught the over-generalization). The attach payload gained `queue` (`session._normalizer.queueState()`) + `queueSupported`, and `created` carries `queue: []` + `queueSupported: false` (a just-spawned wrapper has reported nothing yet; its own baseline `queue_changed` turns the controls on) — the 2.368.4 enumerate-every-window-birth rule.

Codex stamps usage per RESPONSE, never per item — real 0.153.4 rollouts go `reasoning/message/function_call items → token_usage_record{thread_id,turn_id,response_id:'resp_…',usage,turn_token_usage,thread_token_usage} → tool outputs/item_completed → event_msg token_count` (surveyed 79 local rollouts / 830 pairs: the record PRECEDES its token_count by 1–5 lines, same turn_id, `usage.total_tokens === last_token_usage.total_tokens`, `thread_token_usage.total_tokens === total_token_usage.total_tokens`, never a response item in between; 65 token_counts had no record = resume/fork heads). So `_threadUsageMeta` (called from the token_count branch) attaches ONE meta object to every assistant/tool message created since `_usageMark` (the index the previous usage-bearing token_count closed at — the codex twin of claude's recMeta-per-block), then advances the mark; user/peer/system records never get it; heartbeats (info:null/empty) attach nothing. Meta shape = claude's + honest kind markers: `requestId` = `cx:<threadId>:<cumulative total_tokens>` derived EXACTLY as usage-walker.js mints the ledger rid (the dedup key already baked into every permanent ledger — the only join old rollouts have; `requestIdKind:'ledger'`, never passed off as a vendor id), `msgId` = the `resp_…` response_id from the preceding `token_usage_record` (`_processTokenUsageRecord` parks it; consumed by the next token_count after a total_tokens check; `msgIdKind:'response'`; NULL on pre-0.153 rollouts and on the LIVE v2 stream, which has no such record — never invented), `usage` {input_tokens = FRESH = input−cached (the ledger's `i`), cache_read_input_tokens = cached, cache_creation_input_tokens AND codex's own cache_write_input_tokens, output_tokens, reasoning_output_tokens}, `model`, `effort` (turn_context), `stopReason:null`. **Thread id = the conversation being RENDERED, mirroring the walker's per-FILE sid** (adversarial verifier 2026-09-06, real data: a codex SUB-AGENT rollout carries its OWN session_meta at line 0 and the PARENT's at line 1 — 29/79 local rollouts carry two, 79/79 have their own FIRST; "last session_meta wins" keyed 11 rollouts / 2320 messages `cx:<parent>:…` and 64 of those keys COLLIDED with the parent conversation's real ledger events, so the billing row named the wrong conversation's account). **ROUND 3 (2026-09-06, the verifier refuted the absolute reader pin with byte copies of two real rollouts): the ledger thread id is a fact about the RECORD's FILE, never about the reader.** A merged read (CodexSessionMessages prepends fork ancestry — native `forked_from_id`+`forked_from_ordinal_exclusive` parents cut at the boundary, the wrapper `forkedFrom` chain whole) tags every record with the rollout it came from (`__threadId`, NON-enumerable, codex-session-store.tagRecordThread — survives the sort copy, never reaches JSON.stringify/fingerprints/wire) and `_threadUsageMeta` keys by `record.__threadId` first (`_recordThreadId`, captured per record in `_processRecord`): parent-half records key `cx:<parent>:…` exactly as the walker keys the parent FILE (the absolute pin keyed them `cx:<child>:<parent cumulative>` — keys the ledger never minted for that thread, 0/30 parent messages matched the parent's ledger, two collided with real child events). The constructor `{threadId}` is the DEFAULT for provenance-less records only (gap slabs, tail-only reads, the live stream and the wrapper buffer; normalizers.rebuildHistory passes session.backendSessionId||claudeSessionId, ws-handler's view-only attach backendSessionId, transcript-service view()/gapSlab() r.sessionId — a gap slab carries NO session_meta at all), and `_adoptThreadId` is: ① wrapper_meta.threadId REPLACES the default, constructor id or not (the wrapper's OWN record naming the file it writes NOW — after a mid-life thread/fork codex-events re-points session.backendSessionId on that same record and the normalizer follows it IN-STREAM through feedLive, ordering-correct across a rebuild queue where a direct re-pin from the consumer is not; a pinned manager kept minting `cx:<old>:…` while the walker keyed the new file) ② the FIRST session_meta fills a void only (a prepended fork-ancestry session_meta or the copied parent-provenance one never overwrites) ③ token_usage_record.thread_id fills a void only. Same round, adapters/codex.extractCodexThreadMeta: `forked_from_id` is read from the thread's OWN session_meta only, like the boundary ordinal — "last wins" took the copied parent meta's `forked_from_id` (= the GRANDPARENT) and paired it with the child's own boundary, so the merged read cut the wrong file. **DUPLICATE token_counts** (codex re-emits an identical record after later items — 369/8490 in the local corpus; real window: token_count → item_completed → assistant message → identical token_count → token_usage_record → next token_count) return on `ridKey === _lastRidKeys.get(threadId)` (PER thread since round 3 — the walker's cursor is per file and a merged read interleaves files) BEFORE the pending record or the mark is touched — the walker's `rid === cur.lastRid` twin; the first cut stamped the PREVIOUS response's key/numbers onto the NEXT response's messages and advanced the mark past them for good (45/1143 wrong). `_ledgerKeys` records every minted {rid, mid, n, tid} (tid = the file the key belongs to; n=0 = a CARDLESS response: back-to-back token_counts with no item, an agent_message event without a response_item twin — 676/4344 in the local corpus; a per-message check alone can never see them). Both token_count shapes handled (rollout snake_case; the wrapper's relay of thread/tokenUsage/updated keeps v2 camelCase inside `last_token_usage`/`total_token_usage`). Live emits the SAME `{op:'edit', fields:{meta}}` claude uses so an open window's popup refreshes.
