Gate: test-codex-0153 ② + test-codex-history (sub-agent card). **WEB SEARCH — WHICH SHAPE DOES WHICH VERSION WRITE (2.369.43; the first cut targeted a shape the INSTALLED CLI does not write and was refuted on real data 2026-09-06 — re-cut from a READ-ONLY fleet scan of every local rollout keyed on `session_meta.cli_version`, 82 files):** **view_image = ONE card per call_id (2.369.43):** a real rollout carries the triple `function_call view_image {path, detail}` → `event_msg view_image_tool_call {call_id, path}` → `function_call_output [{input_image, image_url:data:…}]`, and a live session ALSO has the wrapper stub's `{path}` twin + `viewed <path>` output — the merge fingerprint keeps all of them (different payloads), and a second card for a known id used to leave the first pending forever. `_absorbTwinCall` (function_call/custom_tool_call for a known call_id merge input, no new card), `view_image_tool_call` is ROUTED (left SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES: the ≤0.130 engine's own record, and the ONLY trace when the call pair is absent), and `_processFunctionCallOutput` lifts `input_image` blocks to `images:[{mediaType, bytes}]` + a `[image …]` marker (the 2.369.35 law's codex twin — base64 never enters a card). **…but on 0.153.4 NONE of those shapes is what a rollout persists.** CORPUS FACT (82 local rollouts, measured 2026-09-06): the 25 files whose session_meta says cli_version 0.153.4 hold 48 image views and every one is an `event_msg/item_completed {item:{type:'ImageView', id:'exec-…', path:'file:///home/…/studio.png'}}` — ZERO `function_call view_image`, ZERO `view_image_tool_call` (those live only in the ≤0.130 rollouts, 98 calls + 15 events, and in the wrapper's LIVE stream). **ONE ROUTER, ONE DECODER:** the ImageView case of `_processItemCompleted` (the item_completed ALLOWLIST above) and the `view_image_tool_call` branch both call `_processViewImageEvent`, the single image-view card path — a KNOWN call_id only learns the path, an unknown one becomes a complete `view_image` card a later output twin edits in place — and `fileUrlToPath` is applied THERE and nowhere else (file:// → plain path, percent-decoded ONCE; a plain path, which is what the live RPC hands the wrapper, is never touched, so a literal `%` in a filename survives). LIVE + ROLLOUT CONVERGE ON ONE CARD because the wrapper's live stub uses `call_id = item.id` — the same `exec-…` (verified against a live buffer: call_id `exec-a0e11416-…` is the id of the ImageView item the rollout wrote for that view). Gate: test-codex-history (real-order fixture + the ImageView pin) + test-image-cards ⑧ (14 ImageView records cut VERBATIM from the 0.153.4 root rollout → 14 complete cards, none pending; negative control: every other item type still produces no card and no unknown-record telemetry). **QUEUE STATE IS SESSION STATE, NEVER A MESSAGE (2026-09-06):** `queue_changed` → `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue', items}` + `queueState()` for the attach payload; nothing about the queue ever enters the transcript. The user bubble a queued message came from wears the state instead: `_stampQueueChip(msgId, 'queued'|'steered'|'removed'|null)` joins through `userMessageIds` and rides the ordinary `edit` op, so an open window updates in place. Three rules that took a chip's lifecycle to get right: ① `queued_input` stamps the chip and returns — the old system card is gone, and survives ONLY as the fallback when there is no bubble to stamp (a peer/job message has no `webui_msg_id`), because a queued message must never be invisible ② an item that leaves the queue with no explicit steer/remove was drained by the turn ending, so its chip CLEARS — a bubble that keeps claiming to be queued forever is a lie ③ a failed `queue_op_result` becomes a VISIBLE notice, and `queueOpFailureText` (PURE, exported for the suite) words every branch in terms of what happens to the message NOW ("stays queued and runs when this turn ends" / "will simply run next" / "already ran"). The ACP normalizer carries the byte-identical trio. Two more, from the round-1 review: ④ the user bubble is stamped with **`webuiMsgId`** where `userMessageIds` is written — the map is server-side only, and the CLIENT chip has to join a rendered bubble back onto its queue row (without the stamp every chip click answered "That message is no longer queued") ⑤ `queuePublished()` — a wrapper that has never emitted a `queue_changed` (i.e. one that predates this release, but still emits `queued_input` as it has since 2.369.20) gets the OLD system card instead of a chip, because a chip there could never clear and never be acted on; the meta op carries `supported:true` so the client learns the same fact in band.
| cli_version | how a search persists | `results` |
|---|---|---|
| 0.120 / 0.125 / 0.130 | `event_msg web_search_end {call_id:'ws_…', query, action}` **+ an id-less Responses-API `web_search_call {status, action?}` on the next line** — the TWIN | never (1698 ends, 0 with the key) |
| 0.149.1 | `web_search_end` only (`call_id:'exec-…'`), no `web_search_call` anywhere | always (35/35) |
| **0.153.4 (installed)** | **no `web_search_end` at all** — only `event_msg item_completed {item:{type:'Extension', kind:'web.search', id:'exec-…', query, action, results}}` | always (245/245, `[]` = really none) |
| live (wrapper) | `function_call web_search` at item/started (EMPTY stub = the pending card) + `web_search_end` at item/completed (the v2 item relayed) | as the item carries |

  Three 0.153.4 rollouts with 20/43/30 searches rendered ZERO cards because `item_started`/`item_completed` sat in `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` — and a skipped type emits no telemetry, so nothing said so. `_processEvent` now dispatches `item_completed` to `_processItemCompleted` BEFORE the generic skip (which the type stays in, for every other kind). **All carriers land on ONE card keyed by the item/call id** (the Extension id IS the exec-… id the wrapper's live card uses, so live + rollout edit the same card): `_finalizeToolCall` merges `{query, action}` into the input (mergeToolInput) and renders results through `renderSearchOutput` (src/search-card.js). **Dispatch is an explicit ALLOWLIST on `item.type`** (`ITEM_COMPLETED_SKIPPED_TYPES`, exported + pinned), never a fall-through: an unrecognised TOP-LEVEL type fires `codex-unknown-record:item_completed:<type>` once per type, an unknown Extension kind `…:item_completed:Extension:<kind>`, an unknown sub-agent kind `…:item_completed:SubAgentActivity:<kind>` — the telemetry means "never seen upstream", so a type we deliberately do not card is in the allowlist instead. **0.153.4 `item_completed` census (all 25 rollouts, snapshot 2026-09-06 — the corpus is LIVE: two scans an hour apart differed ~4%, so the RATIOS are the claim, not the totals; `item_started` is NEVER persisted — 0 records):** RENDERED ELSEWHERE ⇒ skipped by name — Reasoning 1480 / AgentMessage 84 carry an id that IS a response_item id (1480/1480, 84/84); CollabAgentToolCall 5 carries the function_call's call_id (5/5); CommandExecution 588 + FileChange 135 have no id twin but each is the RESULT of the `exec` custom_tool_call in front of it (876 `exec` calls, the only shell tool name in the corpus), whose card already shows the command and its output — **0.153.4 has NO standalone `apply_patch` tool call at all (0 in the corpus; the earlier "apply_patch exec count == FileChange count" claim was false): a patch rides the SAME `exec` call, whose input text carries the `*** Begin Patch` payload, and 135/135 FileChange items follow an exec naming the changed file**; UserMessage 31 is the already-rendered prompt; SubAgentActivity started 18 + interacted 214 carry the id of the tool CALL that caused them (spawn_agent 18/18, send_message 197 + followup_task 17), which renders its own card; ContextCompaction 1 is deliberately unrouted — the live wrapper emits its own `context_compacted` for the same item and the two spellings do NOT fingerprint-dedupe in the buffer⇄rollout merge, so routing it would print the notice (and bump turnIndex) twice on every live session; **known cost: a rollout-ONLY rebuild shows no compaction notice.** **ONLY CARRIER, so routed here: `Extension web.search` 245 → the search card, `Extension image_gen.generation` 3 → the existing image_gen card path (`revisedPrompt` in, `status` + `savedPath` out — the 3 MB base64 `result` NEVER enters a message, the 2.369.35 binary law), `ImageView` 54 → the view_image card path with `file://` stripped (the wrapper strips it too, so the live and rebuilt copies of that one card are byte-identical), `SubAgentActivity` kind `completed` 32 (over 18 distinct threads) → the `sub_agent_activity` card path.** The sub-agent completion is the second carrier this CLI MOVED: 0.153.4 persists no standalone `sub_agent_activity` event at all while 0.149.1 persists 349 of them (started 64 / interacted 277 / interrupted 8 / completed 0), and the completed records' id `subagent-completed-<uuid>` twins nothing — a sub-agent finishing was recorded NOWHERE else, so `_processSubAgentActivity` (extracted from `_processEvent`, now shared by the standalone event, the thread/read mapper and item_completed) CREATES the card when no 'started' opened one and is keyed by THREAD so any repeat is idempotent. Terminal kinds only: routing 'started'/'interacted' would double-render against their spawn/send cards. Old-vs-new whole-corpus replay: 0.153.4 gains exactly 18 Sub-agent cards and changes nothing else (search 246 / image 56 / bash 911 identical), 0.149.1 is card-for-card identical, telemetry 0 in both. **Twin pairing (0.120-0.130) is POSITIONAL:** one slot holds the immediately preceding SEARCH record (end or id-less call — intervening records never touch it, and both handlers overwrite it), so an orphan call later in the file with the same action never pairs with a stale end. Whole-file replays: the 0.125 file = 96 orphan calls then 104 end+call twins ⇒ 200 cards; the 0.130 file (2,119,710,127 bytes = **2.0 GiB**, 1,116,410 lines — `ls -l` on the named rollout; the first cut said "512 MB") = 1524 ends + 1324 calls (every call an adjacent twin) ⇒ 1524 cards, 0 empty, 0 'no results'. An end with action `{type:'other'}` is twinned by a call carrying NO action key at all — keyed apart they rendered the owner's exact second empty card (`{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed'), so `searchActionKey` puts `{type:'other'}` and no-action in ONE bucket. An orphan call still renders its `status: …` card and `action` stays the OBJECT (the client's title chip reads `action.queries/url`); an orphan carrying NO action either (exactly ONE such record in the whole local corpus, 0.120.0) is LABELLED — head `web search (no details recorded)`, input `{note:'no details recorded'}` from the shared `NO_SEARCH_DETAILS` — so no card can reproduce the owner's reported empty shape `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' byte for byte (old-vs-new replay of the 0.120.0 corpus: that shape 1 → 0, card count unchanged); `web_search_begin` (never persisted; live-stream tolerance) = pending card if none, else a query patch that keeps it pending. Gate: test-codex-history's web-search block — VERBATIM 0.153.4 records (search / other / openPage / findInPage-with-url-null / results:[] / image_gen / ImageView, cut from named rollouts + ordinals, anonymised) + verbatim 0.120.0 twin records + PATTERN replays of three whole rollouts (scripts/fixtures/codex-web-search-patterns.json: the real record order and action identity; `expectedCards` is ground truth computed OUTSIDE the normalizer) + the live+rollout one-card check + the unknown-kind telemetry control. **Regenerate the fixture** by re-walking ~/.codex/sessions READ-ONLY, grouping by `session_meta.cli_version`, and emitting E/C tokens per search record (`E~` = end with `{type:'other'}`, `C-` = action-less call).

  **MULTI-AGENT VISIBILITY (B-7473; supersedes the two card shapes above):** `item_completed` LEFT the skip set — corpus-scanning every local 0.153.4 rollout showed it is the ONLY carrier of sub-agent activity there (214 `item_completed/SubAgentActivity`, ZERO `event_msg sub_agent_activity` — that shape exists only on the wrapper's live stream). **ONE router, ONE dispatch (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06 integration):** `_processItemCompleted` is the ALLOWLIST router described above — it runs FIRST in `_processEvent` (the type stays in the generic `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` as the fall-through, and test-codex-history pins the ORDER, not the membership), skips the `ITEM_COMPLETED_SKIPPED_TYPES` twins by name, routes Extension web.search / image_gen.generation / ImageView to their card paths, and sends EVERY SubAgentActivity kind to `_processSubAgentActivity`; anything else fires `codex-unknown-record:item_completed:<type>` (Extension kinds namespaced as `…:Extension:<kind>`) once per type.
  **SUB-AGENT LIFECYCLE — ONE implementation, ONE shape (the merge rule; state it before changing either side):** `_processSubAgentActivity` ① ALWAYS learns `agentPath → agent_thread_id` into `status().subagents` (that map is what makes an agent NAME clickable; it is learned even when no row is drawn), ② renders the fact as a one-line collab ACTIVITY row — **the old standalone "Sub-agent" tool CARD is RETIRED** (a card per lifecycle event buried the conversation; the row carries who/kind/thread plus the click-through), ③ draws NO row when the record's own id IS a rendered call: on 0.153.4 `started`'s id is the `spawn_agent` call_id (18/18) and `interacted`'s the `send_message`/`followup_task` call_id (197+17), and that call already drew its own collab row — the durable `_collabSeen` marker `call:<id>` (set when the collab call renders, NOT the `_collabByCall` entry, which is deleted when the call's output arrives) makes the suppression order-independent. Terminal records synthesise `subagent-completed-<uuid>`, twin nothing, and always draw. Dedupe keys `sa:<thread>:<kind>:<id>` and `sa:<thread>:<kind>` (a repeated 'started' is idempotent; 32 'completed' records over 18 threads ⇒ 18 rows). Three carriers, one path: the 0.149.1 standalone `event_msg sub_agent_activity` (349 records) and the wrapper's LIVE stream, the thread/read mapper (src/codex-thread-read.js), and 0.153.4's `item_completed`. A FOREIGN thread's `error` arrives here too, as kind `errored` with the message in `row.detail` (hover) — a child's failure is never the root's task_failed. Sub-agent traffic is a first-class `collab` shape on the message — `{dir:'in'|'out'|'spawn'|'wait'|'activity', agentPath, agentName, nickname, msgType, encrypted, target, cellId, threadId, kind, detail, rows:[…]}`: an inbound PLAINTEXT payload becomes an ATTRIBUTED **sub-agent report** (role 'tool', **collapseKind 'report'** — its OWN fold kind since B-7473 integration 2026-09-06, deliberately NOT in the default `chat.collapseKinds`: a child's written answer is CONTENT, and folding it under 'agent' hid the thing the owner opened the window to read; the rows stay 'agent' — envelope stripped, body rendered as markdown by the renderer), while encrypted inbound, outbound `send_message`/`followup_task`, `spawn_agent`, `wait` and lifecycle events are ONE-LINE rows that COALESCE into a single message (edit-in-place live, same on rebuild). The collab tool family never becomes a tool CARD: its `message` argument is a fernet blob upstream, and `collabDetailOf` only ever reads plaintext keys (prompt/description) — **no encrypted blob may reach a rendered string**. Twin dedup: live (wrapper, id `msg_…`) vs rollout (id `amsg_…`) by id, else by the CONTENT leg within the turn — the two ids differ, so the (author, text) leg is what actually kills the owner's duplicate. **That content leg only exists where there IS content (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06, MAJOR, measured on the owner's real root rollout):** an ENCRYPTED inter-agent message has `body === ''` and `msgType 'MESSAGE'` for EVERY message, so the `(turn, author, msgType, body)` key degenerated to one key per (turn, author) and SILENTLY DROPPED distinct messages — the verifier measured 101 of 140; re-measured on the same (still-growing) rollout at 3648 records, 120 of 168. Every one carries a distinct id AND a distinct blob, so the id leg never fired. Now: the (author, msgType, body) leg fires only when `body` is non-empty; an encrypted message dedupes by ID plus a discriminator taken from the fernet blob's first 32 chars (version + timestamp + IV). Pinned with three VERBATIM encrypted records from one agent in one turn ⇒ 3 rows coalesced into ONE "3 messages · water_research" item, never 1 (negative-controlled: the old key scores 1). `session_meta.agent_path` (own meta only) sets `status().agentPath`, the reference point that makes a message inbound or outbound. Labels/HTML come from the PURE src/collab-row.js. Gate: test-codex-0153 ⑥ (fixtures verbatim from the real 0.153.4 root rollout) + test-codex-subagents. **ONE IDENTITY PER SUB-AGENT (round-5, MAJOR, measured on the owner's real root rollout at 4807 records):** codex names the same child TWO ways — the OUTBOUND legs (`spawn_agent`/`send_message`/`followup_task` arguments) carry a BARE `task_name`/`target` ("interior_research"), while the spawn OUTPUT (`{"task_name":"/root/interior_research"}` — the proof of the intended shape), every inbound `agent_message` author and every SubAgentActivity record carry the absolute agent path. Keying rows on the raw value split each agent in two: **36 distinct row identities for 20 real children**, the fold header drew the same agent as two chips, the "N sub-agents" count over-reported, and the `agentPath → threadId` map (absolute keys) never matched an outbound row — **98 of 409 rows carried no thread id and therefore had NO click-through at all**. `absAgentPath(name, ownPath)` normalises any value WITHOUT a '/' to `(status().agentPath || '/root') + '/' + name` at the ONE place each row is built (collab tool call, agent-message envelope leg, spawn output, SubAgentActivity), so chips / map lookup / back-fill / summary all see one identity: after = **20 identities, 20 map entries, 408/409 rows with a thread id** (the 409th is the agent-less `wait` row) and the message/report/row counts UNCHANGED. `_noteSubagentThread` carries the BELT: its back-fill also matches a row whose own path has NO '/' against the key's last segment — deliberately one-directional, because two parents may each own a child named `research` and mapping one onto the other's thread would open the WRONG conversation (a wrong answer is worse than a missing one; negative-controlled). **ERRORS ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE (round-5, MINOR):** every other lifecycle kind repeats the same fact, so `(thread, kind)` is the right coalescing for them — but an `errored` record carries a MESSAGE, and that key silently dropped every error after a child's first, differently worded or not. `errored` now keys on `sa:<tid>:errored:<id>|<detail>`: a genuine re-read of one record collapses (both legs equal) while two differently-worded failures never do, EVEN IF their ids collide — the wrapper's synthesized `foreign-error-<tid>-<Date.now()>` was not unique for two failures inside one millisecond (it now appends a counter too, but the key does not depend on that). No eviction on that lane on purpose: `_collabSeen` also holds the durable `call:<id>` double-render markers and an FIFO sweep would drop one while its twin is still to come. Pins: test-codex-subagents ④ (real-shaped fixture: one identity, every row's thread id, one map entry, the same-leaf-name negative control, three distinct errors ⇒ three rows, the id-collision case, and 'completed' still coalescing).

### mounts.js

  mounts.js            — MountManager (rclone mounts, MULTI-SOURCE: typed records s3/drive/onedrive/gmail/webdav/sftp/vibespace/rclone via _rcloneFor; detached + boot adoption; one-click rclone install (data/bin, pinned 1.65.2); guided Google Drive OAuth (2.226.2: all four OAuth entry points render the auth URL as a copyable row — consent completable from ANY browser, paste-back relay finishes; popup-block reported honestly); rclone.conf import; per-mount S3 share minting mc→STS; NATIVE cephfs type (2.109.1, all-flash deployment storage — kernel `sudo mount -t ceph`, per-user quota, env-provisioned My storage VIBESPACE_CEPHFS_* replaces S3, no CSI; **2.237.2: _mountCephfs ensures a no-op /sbin/modprobe shim before EVERY attempt — mount.ceph hardcodes that path and treats exit-127 as fatal, so a kmod-less image broke every connect even with the ceph module loaded host-side (a container can never modprobe the shared kernel; when kmod existed the call always failed harmlessly). Never remove the shim 'because modprobe exists in the current image'**); flat-connections model — no special my-storage slot, env auto-migrates to a normal mount. **SUBMOUNT model (2.108.0→2.108.2, user-refined twice): ANY top-level storage record can hold submounts — children reference the parent via `parentId` + their own path fields (`remote:path`) and resolve connection through `_connOf()` at use time (addChild allows any non-child parent; no nesting). `kind:'credential'` now means CREDENTIAL-ONLY (root unmountable — bucket-scoped token): UI shows a key ICON in place of the status dot (no text badge, user directive) and NO Connect action; mount() auto-heals kind on a later successful root probe — refreshing the credential's token/keys heals every child (update() bounces mounted children). A credential itself mounts fine when its token can list the remote root (GDrive, account-wide keys); mount() AUTO-DETECTS bucket-scoped tokens (pathless s3-family + root-list probe AccessDenied → convert to credential + guidance) because the fuse mount otherwise "succeeds" and EIOs on every IO (the FishR2 trap — real bucket was `example-prod-data`; S3 names are lowercase). AccessDenied WITH a path fails fast pre-mount with a check-the-bucket-name error (v2Auth probe extension; v2Auth stays undefined so a fixed path re-probes). addChild/convert/remove-guarded; export re-links children by parent NAME. Drive re-auth: startDriveAuthForMount(id) runs the guided OAuth with the mount's own client creds, applyDriveToken() writes back + reconnects children — the invalid_grant recovery path; **applyDriveToken accepts drive AND onedrive AND cloud holders (2.268.8 — it rejected non-Google types, leaving the edit-dialog re-auth button dead for them), and the client re-auth surfaces word themselves per provider (`_oauthProviderNames` in sidebar-mounts.js), never hardcoded "Google Drive". ONEDRIVE REQUIRES RESOLVED drive_id+drive_type IN CONFIG (2.268.8, real report: every fresh native add failed with rclone's cryptic "upgrading from older versions" error — imported rclone.confs worked only because the conf carried both): `_resolveOneDriveDrive` (Graph /me/drive, region-aware GRAPH_BASE) runs as a mount-time backstop when driveId is missing (error recorded into _errors — "token expired" wording surfaces the row re-auth button) + after each re-auth write-back; explicit driveId never overridden. Test: scripts/test-onedrive-resolve.mjs.** Non-env mounts expose ALL connection params in the edit dialog PREFILLED with their real current values, SECRETS INCLUDED (2.108.8, user directive: no more blank "=default" fields — `_showEditMountDialog` fetches the decrypted config via `GET /api/mounts/:id/config`/`config()` and fills every input, keys/tokens/passwords/rclone params too; single-user instance, inputs stay type=text); save is a DIFF — a field is sent only when it changed from the fetched original (unchanged secrets aren't needlessly re-encrypted; server ignores an emptied secret so clearing one is a no-op there), an emptied rclone per-param row removes it. Only my-storage locks connection fields (edit shows name+mountpoint only; `config()` returns `{envLocked:true}` with no secrets). **HUNG-MOUNT DEFENSE (2.108.3, real outage)**: a fuse mount to an unreachable backend (home-LAN SMB host from a datacenter pod) hangs every IO — node's libuv THREADPOOL fills with stuck fs ops and the whole server stops answering while the event loop stays idle (ep_poll; /login 130s → readiness dead → pod out of Service). mount() runs a post-mount child-process `ls` probe (6s guard; error-exit=responsive, only a STUCK child trips) and startHealthWatchdog() sweeps every mounted record (60s + 15s-after-boot, covers boot-ADOPTED mounts that skip mount()) — hang ⇒ lazy unmount + `_killMountDaemon` (exact /proc cmdline argv match — a wedged rclone survives fusermount -uz) + desired=unmounted persisted + row error. Never probe mountpoints with node fs — child processes only. 2.108.4: pathBlocked() circuit breaker — mount() blocks the root for the WHOLE connect window and hang-detection blocks 90s; a files.js router middleware fails every op under a blocked root fast with 503 (an open explorer window otherwise stuffs the pool during the probe window — real follow-up incident); UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=32 at the top of server.js (before any pool-touching require). **2.109.0 STRUCTURAL FIX: src/safe-fs.js worker_threads pool** — every LOCAL user-path fs op in routes/files.js runs in a worker with its OWN libuv pool + per-op deadline + kill/respawn (path resolution/traversal stay in main; worker executes the resolved absolute path only; ?host= untouched; app.locals.safeFs wired in server.js, inline fallback if the pool fails). Revoked/expired share SURFACING: _probeMountpoint is 3-state (ok/error/hung) + _probeBackendAccess (uncached rclone lsf re-auth, gated to _revocable = imported/vibespace/expiring) catches a 401/403 that a cache-served mountpoint ls hides → row shows "connected but every file errors" (clears on re-grant). 2.108.5 SELF-MOUNT GUARD: a vibespace-bridge record whose bearer token exists in OUR OWN MountTokens store points back at this instance — fuse→HTTP→self deadlocks the threadpool (real incident); refused at add/import/mount via mounts.selfTokenCheck = mountTokens.has(raw) (no lastUsedAt bump)**. **DIRECT CEPHFS SUBTREE SHARING (2.111.7): sharing a folder from a cephfs My-storage mount calls an in-cluster minter (env VIBESPACE_CEPHMINT_URL/_TOKEN) to mint a PATH-SCOPED cephx key (`ceph fs authorize <fs> client.vibespace-share-<rand> <path> <r|rw>`) and emits a `vibespace-cephmount:v1:<b64>` link; import → a normal `cephfs` mount that KERNEL-mounts the subtree directly (full flash bandwidth, no WebDAV relay). Key scoped to exactly the subpath (mds allow r path=…), recorded in `_state.shares` (kind:cephmount), revoke deletes it cluster-side. Env-gated — absent the minter, sharing falls back to the WebDAV bridge; cross-cluster/external always uses the bridge. mintCephShare/parseCephMountLink/revoke path in mounts.js; canCephShare in list(); `_showCephShareDialog` UI. Helm: storage.existingClaim (mount a pre-made PVC — the home-SC migration lever) + cephMint.{url,token}.** **2.110.0 HARDENING (user directive 最稳定+性能最好+自动恢复): every rclone mount runs `--vfs-cache-mode full` with a PERSISTENT per-mount cache dir (data/vfs-cache/<id>, root overridable VIBESPACE_VFS_CACHE_DIR; setting mounts.vfsCacheMaxSizeGB default 10G) — dirty writes survive a daemon crash and resume uploading on remount (verified live: SIGKILL 0.5s post-write → auto-remount → object lands); bounded IO (--timeout 60s --contimeout 15s + retries) so flaky backends error instead of hanging; new flags gated via _rcloneHasFlag (old system rclone falls back to writes mode). AUTO-RECONNECT supervision: desired now ONLY reflects user intent — internal teardowns (hang defense) keep desired=mounted and _healthSweep's dead-mount branch remounts with backoff 1→2→5→10min (_maybeAutoRemount; _noteReconnectBackoff), auth-class errors (denied/revoked/expired/AccessDenied…) excluded from retry so the actionable message survives; unmount(id,{internal:true}) is the internal teardown that doesn't rewrite intent; mount() is wrapped by a _connecting guard (one connect in flight per record). rcloneBin() NFS trap: executing the 57MB binary from a network FS demand-pages it per exec (~22s measured) — _fastBin copies it once to ~/.cache/vibespace keyed by size+mtime (22s→0.03s), detection via /proc/mounts longest-prefix fstype**) **OAuth-death detection (2.368.6, real OneDrive incident): a dead refresh token hides behind a HEALTHY-looking mount — fuse dir cache keeps listings working while every download fails 'unauthenticated: Unauthenticated' (EIO on every file open, zero UI signal). _oauthBacked mounts (drive/onedrive/cloud/rclone-OAuth) now get the fresh-process backend probe on a slow clock (10min; every sweep while an auth error shows or the mountpoint fails), the denied-regex knows the OAuth phrasings (unauthenticated/invalid_grant/InvalidAuthenticationToken), the health message names the fix (re-authorize), and the client button regex matches it. Gated by scripts/test-mount-oauth-probe.mjs (15). Do not narrow _revocable back to 'my own Drive can(not) expire' — it can. SECOND HALF (2.368.7): an EIO-wedged daemon SURVIVES fusermount -uz, so the re-auth bounce stacked a fresh daemon on the dead-token one and changed nothing (4 leaked daemons found); unmount() now polls _daemonAlive and kills survivors BEFORE resolving (bounce callers mount() right after — a later kill would murder the fresh daemon), and mount() kills any stale daemon before spawning. Never resolve an unmount while the daemon lives. TRUE ROOT CAUSE (2.368.8, corrects 2.368.6's dead-token diagnosis): the token was ALIVE — refresh/list/upload all worked, only the /content DOWNLOAD endpoint 401d (Microsoft's migrated consumer OneDrive vs rclone 1.65.2; A/B with identical config: 1.69.3/1.75.0 download fine). Pin bumped to v1.69.3 (stays in the Cloudflare-STS-safe 1.63–1.69 range) + restore() self-heals data/bin/rclone to the pin at boot (nothing else ever re-runs the installer — a pin bump alone reaches no existing install). The sweep also runs _probeBackendRead (1-byte cat of the first root file) because this failure mode PASSES lsf — auth-class failures must be located per-ENDPOINT (refresh/list/upload/download), never collapsed into 'token dead'.** **STRANDED WRITES (2.369.15, real OneDrive "is not empty, use --allow-non-empty" incident): a mount point that is NOT mounted is a bare local directory, and anything written there (the task store's generated TASK.md recreated a whole tree while OneDrive was down) both disappears once the storage reconnects and blocks that reconnect. Never `--allow-non-empty` (it hides the files). `_ensureMountpointDir(mp, {quarantine:true})` on BOTH connect paths (rclone + cephfs; never on the mount-point edit path) moves leftovers aside to a sibling `<mp>.stranded-<ts>/` — never delete, never merge — recreates the mount point empty and broadcasts a level-2 server-notice naming the path and items. `shadowedBy(p)` = the registered storage whose mount point contains p but is not mounted (gmail/credential records excluded; `isMounted(m, live)` accepts a pre-read /proc/mounts): files.js refuses every op under it with 503, task-groups skips its TASK.md and retries on every `mounts-updated` (the wiring tees the broadcast into `onMountsUpdated` + one boot resync after mounts exist — the constructor's boot regen runs before them). New server-side writers into user paths consult `shadowedBy` first. Gated by scripts/test-mount-stranded.mjs (26).**

### harnesses/ (the harness registry — S1/S4/S6, 2.369.18–22)

  src/plugin-manifest.js — PURE manifest validator + the consent vocabulary (Ph2 2.369.24, Ph4 2.369.30): validateManifest(raw, {hostVersion, folderName, homeDir, forbiddenRoots}) normalizes tiers (none|iframe|module; "trusted" = alias), clientEntry, contributes windows/agentTools/routes/settings(boolean|string|number|select)/themes, capabilities.server {fs.read/write (abs or ~, no .., never covering a forbidden root), childProcess (spawn⇒childProcess warned), net (declared only)}; needsConsent(m) = module tier, any declared server capability, OR any contributed agentTool (2.369.43 — a generated shim is a program on every session PATH, outside the plugin sandbox); capabilitySummary(m) = [{id,text,params}] the SAME items server (409 payload) and client (dialog via t(text, params)) render; capabilitiesHash(m) = key-order-independent fingerprint of the consent surface (tier/server/capabilities/agent tools/routes) — array order is significant by design. **2.369.43 hardening: cleanText() strips control chars + U+2028/U+2029 from EVERY free-text field (description/label/window title/tool description/setting+theme labels) — free text ends up in generated code, so it is sanitized ONCE here; normalizeFsPath COLLAPSES the path (own segment walk — the file has ZERO requires by design) before the forbidden-root prefix test, because node's permission model collapses `//`, `/./` and `/.` (`--allow-fs-read=/.` = the whole filesystem); the fs-write summary says "write AND read" (permissionArgs adds a read grant per write path).** **2.369.44: normalizeFsPath also MODELS node's `*`** — node reads a declared path only up to the FIRST `*` and grants every path whose STRING starts with what came before it (probed against node v24: `--allow-fs-read=/t/a/*` and `/t/a/*/ignored` both read `/t/a/b/c/f.txt`; `/t/a/z*` reads `/t/a/zz.txt` but not `/t/a/b/…`; `/*` reads /etc/hostname). Treating a pattern as a plain path let `["/*"]` and `["<parent-of-a-forbidden-root>/*"]` pass validation and grant the install/data dir. Now: at most ONE `*`, only as the LAST character (a mid-pattern `*` is refused LOUDLY — it grants everything before it and silently ignores the rest); the containment test runs on the literal prefix (`r.startsWith(prefix)` — a wildcard over a parent of a forbidden root is forbidden, and `…/dat*` catching `…/data` too); `/` and `/*` are refused with or without roots; `/dir/*` is never collapsed into `/dir*` (different grants — the latter also covers `/dirty`), and the accepted pattern is stored VERBATIM so the string the owner consented to is the string node gets. Invariant: no other module mutates or reinterprets a manifest.

skippable frames stepped over; cap ⇒ coded EZSTBIG; a truncated final frame yields its partial text)/`zstdDecompressHead(buf, plainBytes)`/`readHeadText(fp, plainBytes)` (plain or zstd, bounded on the COMPRESSED side too). **HEAD READS BISECT, THEY DO NOT GUESS A RATIO (2.369.x (codex-zst review batch)):** `zstdDecompressHead` decompresses progressively smaller compressed PREFIXES (a truncated prefix yields the plain text it covers) under a fixed output cap until ≥ plainBytes of plain text exist — the old one-shot `maxOutputLength: max(plainBytes*4, 1MiB)` threw EZSTBIG on every rollout compressing better than ~4× (7 of 40 REAL rollouts here) and readHeadText answered `''`, which read as "an empty transcript" all the way to a VANISHED session card. **A head that cannot be read THROWS** (`EZSTUNSUPPORTED`/`EZSTHEAD` — zero plain bytes out of non-empty compressed input is a failure, never emptiness) so every caller's catch can treat it as a failed extraction. interpretDiscoveryLines reads NC (name candidates, first that names wins) and CO (open rollout ⇒ 'remote-running') lines; synthesizeDiscoveryLines emits them from a daemon snapshot's `codexRollouts[].userLines` + `codexOpen[]`. **A .jsonl and its .zst twin list ONCE with the PLAIN twin's facts, MERGED (2.369.x (codex-zst review batch))** — "first in mtime order" meant the COMPRESSED twin won (both producers sort newest-first and compression happens after the last write), and the ssh scanner can only read a compressed head where the host has zstd(1), so the card silently lost the cwd/name the plain twin's HC/NC lines carried; mtime = the newer of the pair, CO on either twin marks the one thread running.

WHY ORCH, NOT SHARED (the deliberate deviation from the design's `descriptor.stream.parse` + daemon 共用): every consumer reaches the pool/quota engine, the machine handle (hosts.harvestUsage), the ledger, goal sync and the delivery ladder — none exist on a daemon, and test-architecture forbids a SHARED descriptor from reaching up — so it is descriptor-NAMES-it + registry-RESOLVES-it, and the device-side twin of the claude families stays `claudeSideEffects` in session-brain fed through `sbSeenFirst` (untouched; test-session-brain-dark). LOUD FAILURE at session start, both cases passing output through RAW (visible garbage beats silently mis-parsed claude; nothing ever defaults to stream-json): no `streamProtocol` → the pre-S5 `has no streamProtocol in src/backend-caps.js` console.error + `chat-backend-no-protocol` event (text unchanged); a declared protocol with no registered consumer → `registers no consumer for it` console.error + `chat-protocol-no-consumer` `backend/protocol` event (new — before S5 that case fell to the raw passthrough silently). STATE: the move is verbatim (body === HEAD range, indentation only); per-attach closure state stayed per-attach (`lineBuf`, codex's ANSI stripper, claude's startSubagentWatcher/stopSubagentWatcher — still bound onto `session._startSubagentWatcher` for boot-restore's re-arm); every other field is on the session (session-schema rows, owner 'stdout' — test-session-schema and test-attach-rebuild now scan src/server/stdout/ so those rows stay 'alive' and the feedLive-only rule still covers the consumers).   src/hosts.js — …S3: the ssh discovery script lists rollout-*.jsonl AND .jsonl.zst, emits `NC <path>\t<user record ≤2000B>` (first 3 user records of the 200KB head; a .zst head goes through zstd(1) when the host has it, else the thread lists nameless) and `CO <path>` (rollouts held open by a codex process: /proc fd scan, lsof fallback); `fetchTranscript(id, backend, sessionId)` runs the descriptor's remoteFind through _fetchRemoteByFind (codex predicate matches .jsonl and .jsonl.zst; the cache name stays .jsonl — readers detect zstd by magic).
  src/agentd/agentd.js — …S3: the discovery snapshot lists .zst rollouts, carries `userLines` (first 3 user records via readHeadText, 2000B each) and `codexOpen` (listOpenCodexRolloutPaths) — the same discovery-facts code the orchestrator interprets (device/ssh parity by construction).
  src/harnesses/acp.js + opencode.js — the GENERIC ACP v1 harness (S8, 2.369.29): `acpHarness({id,label,command,args,env,store,brand,terminal,caps})` returns a full descriptor (kind chat, caps row = BACKEND_CAPS[id] or ACP_DEFAULT_CAPS with streamProtocol 'acp-events', Adapter=AcpAdapter, Normalizer=AcpMessageManager, store.SessionMessages=AcpSessionMessages, quota=NULL_QUOTA, creds:null — the agent holds its own login, inject kind 'acp' = the wrapper prefixes /api/agent/prompt-context onto each prompt, no hooks). opencode.js = acpHarness for `opencode acp` (verified 1.18.29: loadSession, sessionCapabilities resume/list/fork, image prompts, config options model + mode build|plan). Invariants: capabilities beyond the caps row are read from the agent's `initialize` reply at spawn (never hardcoded per agent); a harness whose executable cli-env could not resolve reports adapter.installed=false → /api/home `harnesses[]` hides it in the picker and ws-create refuses the create loudly; `<ID>_CMD` env overrides the executable. Adding an ACP agent = one acpHarness() call + a BACKEND_META row + a hidden <option> in index.html + settings keys. S9: opencode.js's store gains discover/createReader/forkSession/forkChain over src/opencode-serve.js (next entry) — locate stays null (no file); ws-create mints the serve fork BEFORE the spawn (data.resumeId = the forked id, refused loudly on failure); capsOf('opencode').fork is a runtime verdict (setVerifiedCap from the serve OpenAPI), merged into the client's BACKEND_META from /api/home harnesses[].caps and the 'harness-caps-updated' broadcast.
  src/adapters/acp.js — AcpAdapter (S8): buildSessionArgs(chat) = resolved executable + acp args + extraArgs under data/bin/acp-wrapper.js with facts in ACP_WEBUI_* env (cwd/model/mode/resume id/session name/backend — never argv); terminal mode = the agent's own TUI (resume/model flags from the descriptor's `terminal`); formatPermissionResponse forwards requestId/approved/optionId/alwaysAllow/abort; formatChatInput returns a preview `user` record so the bubble renders before the wrapper's own record (dedup by msgId).
  data/bin/acp-wrapper.js — the generic ACP v1 CLIENT under dtach (S8): argv `<buf> <sidecar> <cmd> [args…]`; JSON-RPC 2.0 over the child's stdio; `initialize` advertises protocolVersion 1 and NO fs/terminal capabilities (fs/*, terminal/*, unknown agent requests get a JSON-RPC -32601 error — never a hang, never a crash); session/load when the agent adverts loadSession, session/resume when sessionCapabilities.resume, else a LOUD 'no-load' notice + new session; prompts queue locally while one runs (ACP has no queue verb); stdin verbs chat-input/interrupt(session/cancel — but ONLY for a turn already DISPATCHED — + every pending permission answered cancelled + the local queue ALWAYS dropped — the count in the 'queue-cleared' notice is what the USER queued (an internal bookkeeping nudge is not a message anyone can "send again"; the log line keeps the true total), a dropped peer message re-emitted as peer_result ok:false so the ladder re-stashes it, and a dropped NUDGE entry resets `nudgeTurnActive` because per-turn state must die with the turn it belongs to (only that nudge's own endPrompt clears the latch, which a queue entry that never runs never reaches))/permission-response(explicit optionId, else the option whose kind matches approved/alwaysAllow; a rejection with NO reject-kind option FAILS CLOSED to {outcome:'cancelled'} + a 'deny-cancelled' notice — never an allow option)/set-model/set-effort/set-mode|set-permission-mode (set_config_option by category, session/set_mode fallback; unsupported/rejected = loud notices)/peer-message ('Message from <name>:' prefix + peer_result honesty)/_frame_file/_stdin_ack. RECORD SHAPE (one JSON line per event, stdout + buffer file): {ts,type:'acp',kind} with kinds session/config/user/prompt_start/prompt_end/update(raw SessionUpdate, replay:true during load)/permission_request/permission_resolved/client_request/notification/notice/peer_result — documented in the file head; the buffer IS the history (no transcript API in ACP). Sidecar meta = caps {frameFile:true, peerMessage:false}, streaming, sessionId, model/agentMode/configOptions/modes/availableCommands/todos/usage/pendingRequests/acp{agentInfo,capabilities}. Program-use law: never a vendor call, never a secret. TURN INVARIANTS (review batch after 2.369.31, kb-bugfix-invariants "ACP WRAPPER: STOP DIDN'T STOP…"): the turn is claimed synchronously but `fetchContextPrefix()` is a real HTTP round trip, so `runPrompt` re-checks its OWN handle's `cancelled` after the await and a cancelled prompt ends as prompt_end 'cancelled' WITHOUT dispatching (a lone prompt_end is legal — prompt_start is a no-op in the normalizer and the consumer only toggles streaming); the boot-failure notice fires on ANY exit before the session is ready, exit code 0 included (finalizeExit exits synchronously, so boot()'s own catch never runs from the child-exit path — the gate on code!==0 left a blank window). Gate: scripts/test-acp-harness.mjs (91) — cancel-during-await, queued+interrupt, Stop-drops-the-queued-nudge, deny-with-no-reject-option, exit-0-before-initialize, each with a positive control. **THE INPUT QUEUE (2026-09-06):** ACP v1 has no queue verb, so `promptQueue` IS the queue — and therefore the wrapper is its PUBLISHER: `queue_changed {items:[{id,msgId,preview,ts,kind,from}], turn_id}` on every push/drain/drop/remove (plus an empty baseline right after the session is up), `queued_input {msg_id, turn_id}` where the old "Queued — runs after the current turn" NOTICE used to be (the state is now a chip on the bubble; codex parity, one client path), and the `queue-op` stdin verb for `remove` only. A steer that somehow reaches this wrapper answers `queue_op_result {ok:false, reason:'not-steerable'}` — `backend-caps.inputModes.steer=false` stops it at the ws layer and `AcpAdapter.formatQueueOp` refuses it with a reason, so this arm exists purely so nothing is ever dropped in silence. `remove` obeys the same two rules Stop already obeyed: a removed PEER entry goes back to the delivery ladder (`peer_result ok:false`, the consumer re-stashes), and a removed NUDGE entry clears `nudgeTurnActive` (per-turn state dies with the turn it belongs to). Removing an id that already drained answers `reason:'gone'` — never a fake success. **Stop reports its drop as removals**: each dropped entry gets its own `queue_op_result {op:'remove', ok:true, msg_id, reason:'stopped'}` BEFORE the emptied republish — the normalizer clears the chip of anything that leaves the queue with no result (= "it ran"), so a bare empty `queue_changed` told the user their message had RUN when Stop had thrown it away (round-1 review). Order is the fix; the result stamps `Removed` first and the republish then leaves those bubbles alone.
  src/acp-message-manager.js — AcpMessageManager (S8 normalizer, same duck contract as MessageManager/CodexMessageManager) + AcpSessionMessages (store reader over the buffer: raw/tail/slice/wrapperMeta/chatStatus/taskState/isStreaming). Mapping: tool_call kind read|search→Read/Search (fold read), edit|delete|move→write fold, execute→Bash, think→thinking, fetch|other→mcp, switch_mode visible; tool_call_update edits the card (diff content → Edit-style old/new input); agent_message_chunk/agent_thought_chunk stream per messageId (a kind switch closes the other stream so order is kept); user_message_chunk = load replay (the live echo of the wrapper's own `user` record is deduped by text); plan → todos meta; available_commands_update → slash commands on the init card; usage_update → context %; permission_request → the harness-neutral card {options:[{optionId,name,kind}], kind:'approval'} on the tool card, resolved by permission_resolved OR the ws-handler's echoed permission-response frame; prompt_end cancelled → Interrupted, error → error card, refusal/max_* → notice. The init card is a LIVE artefact (history rebuild makes none; attach status carries the facts). Ids are content-derived (stable across rebuilds).
  src/opencode-serve.js — OpenCode serve-mode STORE FACTS (S9 of design-harness-plugins §2.4, B-03f2; SHARED — facts about a machine). WHY: the ACP harness has no transcript files (OpenCode keeps sessions in ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db), so stopped OpenCode conversations never reached the sidebar. `opencode serve` (verified 1.18.29, OpenAPI at /doc) exposes that store on 127.0.0.1. THREE PIECES: (1) OpencodeServeClient — fetch over a base URL, 1.5s default timeout, every failure an OpencodeServeError {status, code: timeout|network|http|NotFoundError…}; listAllSessions = /project × `/session?scope=project&directory=<worktree>` deduped (VERIFIED 1.18.29 on the real store: a bare `directory=` is an EXACT-directory filter — `directory=/` lists NOTHING, which the first live run caught as "0 sessions"; `scope=project` lists the whole project; a serve's bare /session is scoped to ITS cwd's project, so the iteration is the safe shape); v1 `/session/:id/message?limit=` returns the NEWEST N, so the first user message comes from v2 `/api/session/:id/message?limit=3&order=asc`; fork = POST /session/:id/fork?directory= (body {messageID?}) → the new Session (title "<parent> (fork #n)", no parentID — parentID means a SUB-AGENT child). Basic auth only when OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD is in the spawn env. (2) createServeLocator — ONE instance per VibeSpace: reuse data/opencode-serve.json {port,pid,startedAt,command,cwd} when /global/health answers AND its own project is safe (a previous server's child that outlived a SIGKILL restart; never killed by stop() — only our own child is), else spawn `<cmd> serve --port <free> --hostname 127.0.0.1 --log-level WARN` detached + stdio ignored under agentEnv() **from its own empty data/opencode-serve/cwd** (2.369.50 — see WHERE IT RUNS below), wait ≤20s for health (1.2s locally), write the record atomically, probe /doc ONCE → onCaps({fork}) (the CLI version comes from /global/health — /doc's info.version is the API doc's own "1.0.0") — the ONLY writer of capsOf('opencode').fork (setVerifiedCap in backend-caps, PURE in-place row mutation) which cli-env then BROADCASTS as 'harness-caps-updated' (open clients learned fork:false from /api/home BEFORE the lazy start — the notify law). Keeper: exit → respawn after 1s·2^n (≤30s), crash counter resets after 60s of uptime, PARKED after 5 crashes (loud in user actions, silent in the poll), stop() on process exit (stopOnExit) — a SIGKILLed server leaves the serve running for the next boot to reuse. Started LAZILY by the first discovery, never at boot, only when cli-env resolved the CLI AND autostart is on: `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0` disables it explicitly, and the test-harness belt `VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS=1` disables it implicitly (every worktree smoke sets it and SIGKILLs its server — a spawned serve outlives that kill and would pile up per `npm run ci`; `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=1` wins over the belt); with autostart off a RECORDED live instance is still reused, otherwise discover is [] and user actions name the switch. **WHERE IT RUNS AND WHAT IT MAY TOUCH (2.369.50, the 2.369.42 runaway — full incident in kb-bugfix-invariants):** OpenCode boots an INSTANCE per DIRECTORY and each instance recursively indexes + inotify-watches that tree (`fff-*` fast-file-finder + `notify-rs` threads). Measured with /proc against a real 1.18.29 serve: the v1 routes (`/session`, `/session/:id`, `/session/:id/message`, `/project`) boot NOTHING; **every** `/api/session/{id}/…` v2 route boots an instance for that session's `location.directory` (+19 indexer threads, +180 MB, a full-tree watch). So: (a) the serve runs from `data/opencode-serve/cwd`, an empty directory made into a throwaway git repo by `ensureServeCwd` — OpenCode's project resolution walks UP for a git worktree, so a plain directory inside the checkout resolves the WHOLE VibeSpace checkout and a FAKE `.git` does not stop the walk, while with no repo above it the project is the `'/'` catch-all (a README next to it says why; `git init` failing only degrades the isolation, loudly). Accepted side effect: while our serve runs it pins the store's mutable `'global'` project row worktree to that empty dir — the field is last-writer-wins and every `opencode` invocation rewrites it, sessions carry their own `directory`, and our listing is worktree-independent. (b) NAMING uses v1 only, byte-capped. (c) `listAllSessions` takes the DIRECTORY-LESS listing first and only issues `scope=project&directory=` for worktrees passing `bootstrappableWorktree` (never `'/'`, `$HOME`, `os.tmpdir()`, nor the vcs-less `'global'` row — a real project row carries `vcs`, the API's own signal, so no fs call is needed); skipped worktrees surface in `facts.state().skippedWorktrees`. (d) `forkSession` — the one call handing a directory to a mutating endpoint — calls `POST /instance/dispose?directory=` afterwards (best effort; the forked session stays readable). **RUNAWAY GUARD:** the child's own `/proc` (`readProcUsage`) is sampled every 60s; >150% CPU sustained ≥5 min, or RSS >2 GB on a single sample, STOPS it, parks the locator `parkedKind:'runaway'` with the numbers in `lastError`, `console.error`s what to turn off, fires telemetry `opencode-serve-runaway`, pushes `harness-store-updated` and refuses to respawn for an hour (then exactly one retry). An unreadable /proc never parks anything; the locator never signals `process.pid`. **BOOT SELF-HEAL:** a recorded serve whose `GET /project/current` worktree is `'/'` or `$HOME` (a 2.369.42 leftover — its record also has no `cwd`) is SIGTERMed and replaced from the isolated cwd; a record written by this code (matching `cwd`) skips the probe, and a FAILED probe never churns a healthy instance. **AUTOSTART** is `agents.opencodeServeAutostart` (default ON, live-read through a function): `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0/1` wins, `VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS=1` forces off, else the setting.  (3) createFacts — the store members: discover({activeSessions}) = 10s list cache → 10s negative cache (returns the LAST GOOD list, [] before any) → refresh bounded by the 1.5s budget (client({budgetMs}) returns null while the serve boots; a HUNG serve costs ≤1.5s, pinned <2s) — NEVER throws, NEVER stalls the 5s /api/sessions poll; entries {backend:'opencode', backendSessionId, sessionId, sessionKey, cwd=directory, startedAt=time.updated, status live|stopped (live = an activeSessions opencode session holds the id; 'external' is undetectable — another process driving the same sqlite leaves no signal), name, agentKind primary|subagent (parentID), parentThreadId, webuiId/Name/Mode, opencode:{slug,agent,model,projectID}}; names = discovery-facts nameFromText over the FIRST user message read from the **v1** `/session/:id/message` list (the WHOLE conversation oldest-first — v1 `limit` is the NEWEST N so it cannot page from the front), capped at NAME_MAX_BYTES=1MiB by the client's streaming `maxBytes` (over the cap → code `too-large` → the title fallback; six unbounded whole-conversation fetches into this process is an OOM). THE v2 asc endpoint IS SMALLER AND MUST NEVER BE USED HERE — it boots an instance (2.369.42). ≤6 lookups per tick (progressive, no burst), cached per id, retried every 60s while a session has no user message, fallback = OpenCode's title unless it is the "New session - <date>" placeholder; readConversation(id) (8s, LOUD: not installed / parked / unreachable / 404 all name the cause) and forkSession(id,{cwd}) (refused with code unsupported when the OpenAPI lacked the fork path). RECORD SYNTHESIS messagesToAcpRecords(messages, session) → 'acp-events' so the unchanged AcpMessageManager renders a stopped conversation read-only: session{how:'serve', model '<providerID>/<modelID>', mode=agent} · user text parts (non-synthetic first, else synthetic; `ignored` dropped) + "[file: name]" lines → {kind:'user', msgId} · assistant text → agent_message_chunk (messageId = part id, one card per part) · reasoning → agent_thought_chunk · tool → tool_call (kind from the tool id: read→read, glob|grep|list|ls→search, edit|write|patch|multiedit|apply_patch→edit, bash|shell→execute, webfetch|websearch|codesearch→fetch, todowrite|todoread|plan→think, else other; rawInput {tool,…input}, locations from filePath|path|file) + tool_call_update (completed|failed, output/error text, edit oldString/newString as a diff block; running/pending = in_progress with no update) · subtask → completed tool_call "Task: <description>" · compaction/retry → notice · a modelID/mode change between assistant messages → config · assistant end → prompt_end (MessageAbortedError→cancelled, other error→error, completed→end_turn; an open assistant message gets none) · step-start/step-finish/snapshot/patch/agent skipped. OpencodeServeSessionMessages extends AcpSessionMessages: live (the activeSessions membership fact from createSessionMessages, or a non-empty buffer) = the wrapper journal as before — a just-spawned live session with an empty journal is NOT read from the serve (the wrapper's session/load replay would double); stopped = records on prepare() (transcript-service awaits it at its three reader sites; a caller that does not gets an empty reader, never a wrong one). The installed singleton (install() by cli-env; facts() = NULL_FACTS when unwired: discover [] silently, user actions say "not configured"). NOT wired (seen in the 1.18.29 OpenAPI): POST /session/{sessionID}/revert, GET /question + POST /question/{requestID}/reply, /pty*, SSE /event + /global/event (could replace the 10s list poll), /session/{sessionID}/todo. Gate: scripts/test-opencode-serve.mjs (131 — mock serve in scripts/dev/mock-opencode-serve.mjs, which MODELS the instance boot: any v2 per-session route adds `state.instances`, so 'a whole discovery tick bootstraps ZERO instances' is a real assert, plus a spawn-cwd spy, the '/'-worktree boot self-heal, the runaway guard driven by a fake /proc reader + fake clock, dispose-after-fork and the byte cap); manual: `node scripts/dev/opencode-serve-live.mjs --measure [seconds]` runs the REAL loop against the REAL store and prints CPU/RSS/threads/indexer-threads/inotify-watches (healthy = single-digit CPU%, flat RSS, 0 indexer threads, 0 watches).
  src/harnesses/index.js — ONE descriptor per agent harness (claude.js, codex.js, shell.js, opencode.js via acp.js) = identity, `caps` (the backend-caps row), `Adapter` + `adapterConfig(cfg)`, `Normalizer`, `wrapper` file, `store` (locateTranscript/transcriptDirs/conversationIdField…), `quota` (S4 QuotaSignalSource: normalize/signalFromStream/probe/classifyAuthFailure — claude-quota.js/codex-quota.js/null-quota.js; the pool engine's probe dispatcher routes on `quota.probe`), `inject` (S6: kind hooks|wrapper|acp, hookFile, hookEvents, sessionStartHonoured — agent-tool-generators builds HOOK_FILES/HOOK_EVENTS_FOR from it; agent-routes' seen-gates consult sessionStartHonoured), `creds` (S2, 2.369.26-27: subsDirName/authFile/spawnEnvVar/loginLabel/defaultIdField/keychainSensitive/parseAuth(dir) + files/bumpFile/hostFactsKey/longLivedToken/supportsApiKeys/remoteSymlinks/ensureTargets/probe/seedDir(dir) [codex: sharedHome()] — accounts.js resolves dirs/auth/labels/default-account/export files/import seeding/delete/verdict host-facts/pool-swap bump/spawn env var/remoteCreds ship shape through `_credsOf/_acctDir/_readAuthFor/_remoteCreds`; readSubCreds/readCodexSubAuth/_seedConfigDir/_seedCodexDir/_codexSharedHome are shims over it; createPool validates capsOf().pool + registry. Deliberately still dispatched: resolveForSpawn (claude oat/api-key ladder vs codex CODEX_HOME) and the claude-only hostSubs held-check), `settingsPrefix`. API: `HARNESSES` (built-ins), `get/has/list/ids` (unknown id THROWS — never a claude fallback), `register/unregister` (plugin tier-5; built-ins immutable), `assertQuotaContract`. adapters/index.js and normalizers.js are built from it. Conformance: scripts/test-harness-contract.mjs runs the same assertions over every registered harness (descriptor keys, adapter base methods + buildSessionArgs, normalizer duck contract incl. injectPeerCard/convertHistoryAsync, wrapper file + caps advert, store, settings keys, client BACKEND_META row, inject shape, wiring pins). S7 (2.369.23) client META caps + settingsPrefix parity pinned here (2.369.26 — the pins missed their 2.369.23 commit: a patch batch aborted before the suite). S3 (store, 2.369.31) and S5 (stdout pipeline per descriptor — see the HARNESS STDOUT entry below) are landed. S5 decision: the descriptor names its live-stdout consumer ONLY through `caps.streamProtocol` (the backend-caps row it already carries); there is deliberately no `stdout:{protocol}` field — a second copy is a twin waiting to drift, and the contract suite pins that none exists. **store.warmTranscript (B-21e4 item 5):** the OPTIONAL pre-read hook `(id, cwd, {remote}) → Promise` every history consumer awaits through the descriptor before constructing a reader — ws-handler attach + view-only and transcript-service.view (the claude-only `warmSessionJsonlAsync` ternaries are gone): claude = the worker parse-cache warm (unchanged), codex = the thread/read fallback for a missing rollout, a harness without it skips.

### gmail-sync.js

  gmail-sync.js        — GmailSync (2.134.0, backlog B-64db): Gmail-as-a-folder — a 'gmail' mount = local dir of .eml files synced READ-ONLY from the Gmail API (GYB-style; NOT FUSE). Own loopback OAuth for gmail.readonly (rclone authorize is drive-only; same guided UX incl. remote paste-back); seed = STREAMING + CHECKPOINTED (2.136.1-4): each page downloaded then a CROSS-RESTART cursor persisted — a DATE (`state.seedOldestMs` → `before:<sec>`), NOT the messages.list pageToken (pageToken is only stable within one run; new mail between runs shifts/expires it and silently skips OLD mail the incremental never back-fills — real user catch). historyId anchored at seed START (covers mail arriving during a long seed). Total for the N/total bar comes cheaply from users.getProfile `messagesTotal` (whole mailbox) or labels.get `messagesTotal` (label filter), persisted as `state.seedTotal` (free-text query → indeterminate). Progress broadcasts throttle (indeterminate 2.5s — a fast cadence rebuilt the card and restarted the bar animation, stuttering it 'in the first third'; the bar is a symmetric pulse so a mid-anim rebuild is invisible). Incremental via history.list (404 = expired → reseed; dir tree IS the dedup index — msg id in filename tail, _seenIds recurses one date/label dir level), messages.get?format=raw → base64url → .eml (atomic tmp+rename, RFC2047-decoded subject slug in name). Client id/secret from VIBESPACE_GDRIVE_CLIENTS presets (they carry gmail scope) or custom — NO built-in fallback. mounts.js integration: isMounted=worker alive, health sweep skips fuse probing (dead worker → auto-restart), unmount stops worker but keeps files (it's the archive), mode forced ro. Viewer: src/lib/eml.js (dependency-free browser-safe MIME parser, 41-case test) + file-viewer _renderEml (header card, text/html toggle, html in sandbox="" iframe, attachment blob downloads). **2.151.0 incremental-freeze fix (real 3h hang):** history.list lists ADDED ids that can be deleted by fetch time (spam auto-purge; guaranteed with includeSpamTrash) — the per-message 404 failed the whole pass and the cursor only advances after a COMPLETE pass ⇒ every 120s retry re-listed + re-404'd the same dead id forever. _writeGone404 skips gone messages (both paths); all API/token fetches bounded (AbortSignal.timeout 60s/30s) so a wedged socket errors into the backoff. Tests: scripts/test-gmail-sync.mjs (mock API e2e incl the dead-id regression), scripts/test-eml.mjs

### transcript-worker.js

  transcript-worker.js — heavy JSONL transcript work OFF the main thread (2.235.0, the userL degradation follow-up): a persistent SafeFs-hosted worker pool (SafeFs gained opts.workerPath/inlineRun generalization + in-flight ref()) running the EXISTING sync implementations by requiring the same modules — worker-side mtime+size caches, zero duplication. Async variants jsonlGapInfoAsync/readJsonlLineRangeAsync/scanJsonlUserTurnsAsync/readJsonlBoundedParsedAsync serve the gap/turnmap/slab endpoints; session-store.warmSessionJsonlAsync pre-warms the parse cache before the ws attach rebuild + /api/session-messages (sync machinery then cache-hits). Worker down ⇒ sync inline fallback. RULE: new heavy transcript scans go through this worker, never inline on the loop (measured: 1ms max main-thread block vs 78ms inline on a 39MB fixture, 0.6-1s on real files). Test: scripts/test-transcript-worker.mjs. Related: sysinfo.js samples event-loop lag (500ms cadence, 3-min median; ≥300ms amber / ≥800ms red → sysinfo-alert kind:'evloop' toast "sessions are NOT dead" + history field `e`)

### agentd/

  agentd/               — CS refactor (B-5052) machine agent — ALL milestones' protocol+device side COMPLETE and acceptance-tested (2.140.0–2.146.0; docs/design-remote-cs.md + M0 addendum + milestone record). THREE flags, DEFAULT ON since 2.158.0 (CS graduation — local=device#0; automatic legacy fallback preserved). **ONE MACHINE MODEL since 2.160.0 (B-f3e8, resolves the user's 2026-07-15 insight that row inconsistencies = two underlying concepts): a machine is a host record, transport ∈ {ssh(absent), dial}** — the pairing credential lives ON the dial record (`dialTokenHash`, redacted from list(), migrated ONCE from dial-tokens.json via hosts.migrateDialTokenFile → `.migrated`; LOSSLESS is load-bearing: in-field daemons hold the raw tokens); roster/test/unpair = /api/hosts + POST /api/hosts/:id/test (hosts.test dial branch = mux runCmd probe) + DELETE /api/hosts/:id (server unpairDialDevice: mounts+token-file+live-stream teardown); /api/agentd/devices is GONE; `hosts-updated` broadcasts on pair/unpair/dial-in/out; hosts.dirComplete rides the device link (dial machines get real remote autocomplete); sidebar has ONE _buildHostRow for all transports + a 'This machine' local row (local=device#0 made visible). The flags: `agentd.sessions` (M1: local sessions run in the daemon — dtach attach inside agentd, survives server restarts), `agentd.remoteSessions` (M2: remote chat = persistent pipe session in the remote daemon via the vibespace-agentd-attach bridge, keeper-run-identical contract so chat-wrapper is untouched; auto install/refresh at spawn), `agentd.dataPlane` (M3 switchovers: RemoteFs fs ops / discovery raw-facts synthesized into the ssh-script line format→UNCHANGED parser / transcript INCREMENTAL slab sync (append-only delta via read-range — no more whole-file remote-jsonl pulls) / usage harvest via run-stream; per-path ssh fallback; hosts.device(id) = per-host DeviceManager registry). mux.js = length-prefixed binary mux (chan0 JSON control + credit-flow-controlled byte channels; data() SPLITS at the window — all-or-nothing starved big writes; zero deps, shared by daemon+server = invariant #3). **CONTROL-OVERTAKE INVARIANT (2.187.0, real incident): chan0 control is credit-EXEMPT, so a completion marker (fs-done/stream-exit) OVERTAKES data still queued behind the 256KB window — any "resolve on the done marker" consumer truncates big transfers to exactly INITIAL_WINDOW bytes.** fsReadRange/runStream are COUNT-GATED (resolve when the counted bytes landed; daemon reports `sent`, run-stream exits on 'close' not 'exit', both pace under window pressure via onWritable); the incident: a 45MB remote transcript cached as a 256KB prefix stamped complete → permanently ancient chat history (fetchSessionJsonl now also refuses to stamp meta for bytes not received + caps on FETCHED delta not total size; **2.188.1: cache-valid additionally requires the cached FILE to hold meta.size bytes — a pre-fix stump over a STOPPED transcript passed size/mtime forever since the self-heal only fired on remote-file change**). Any NEW byte-channel consumer must gate on a byte count, never on a control-channel done. Test: scripts/test-agentd-bigread.mjs. agentd.js = daemon (flock singleton, setsid, 0700 unix socket, multi-server, vsht_ hello auth, self-upgrade→versioned dir→re-exec (2.185.2: the re-exec PRESERVES the original argv via reExecArgv — dropping `--dial/--dial-token` re-exec'd a dial device into LISTEN mode and wedged the link behind the singleton, the userW-class 'device goes offline after updates' root cause); pipe-sessions = keeper semantics in-daemon (setsid child + buffer/fifo + offset reattach + sentinel + drain-only-never-respawn); **pipe-child identity is exec-PROOF startTime, never cmdline (2.184.1, real userL outage: chat children spawn as `sh -lc '… exec env … claude'` so cmdline loses argv0 — every daemon upgrade re-exec then misjudged the live claude as a recycled pid, synthesized a crash sentinel (session died, claude orphaned) AND made kill-pipe-session a no-op; adopted children additionally get a liveness watcher because a non-child can't be wait()ed — no watcher meant no exit sentinel ever; test-agentd-adopt.mjs guards both)**; fs-op incl read-range byte-chan slab; discovery snapshot w/ tailIds/headCwd/userLines enrichment + fs.watch dirty push; run-cmd (argv-only bounded); run-stream (unbounded stdout on byte chan); tcp-connect (loopback only = VNC bridge shape); **serve-folder (2.150.0; WEBDAV since 2.151.0, device-folder-mount): a minimal in-daemon read-only WebDAV subset (OPTIONS/PROPFIND Depth 0-1/HEAD/GET+Range; zero deps, mirrors src/webdav.js) bound to 127.0.0.1 that rclone `webdav`-mounts via tcp-connect (src/device-mount.js) — the plain-`http` backend was the 2.150.0 stall: it requests a fixed 128MB range per read then waits ~6s on the keep-alive connection after the clamped 206; webdav requests sane ranges and reads in ~7ms (verified). GOTCHAS: clamp ranged end to size-1 (rclone asks 128M ranges → clamp or truncated EIO); the SERVER-side bridge net.createServer MUST be `{allowHalfOpen:true}` + attach the data listener BEFORE `await tcpForward` (curl/rclone half-close a GET's write side + write-then-read, both lose bytes otherwise); NEVER do sync fs/exec on a device mountpoint from the process hosting the bridge — the blocked event loop can't pump the tunnel, the FUSE read never completes, SELF-DEADLOCK (the acceptance test hung itself this way for a whole debug cycle; a dir `ls` still 'works' off rclone's dir-cache, which hides it; test IO now runs in async children w/ watchdog — production safe by construction: bridge in the server, reads from other processes/safe-fs workers). Still not a MountManager UI type (next step). **SSH-BRIDGE STDIN EPIPE (2.241.1, userN's exit-code-1 crash): the client.js ssh-transport wrapper writes to child.stdin — pipe errors arrive ASYNC as 'error' events on the stdin socket itself (the wrapper's on('error') only covered the child process; the write try/catch only stops sync throws), so a dying ssh child during the heartbeat PING was an UNCAUGHT write EPIPE that killed the whole server 28s after an update restart. stdin/stdout error events are swallowed; 'close' drives mux teardown+reconnect. Any new pipe-wrapped transport must attach error listeners on the PIPE STREAMS, not just the process.** dial-in device consumption = DeviceManager `stream` transport + server deviceForDial(id) wrapping a dialed-in ws stream — **deviceForDial NEVER reuses a stop()ed DeviceManager (2.169.0, real userW outage: hours of phantom-offline against a healthy dialed-in Mac): stop() is terminal (_connectLoop throws 'stopped' forever), so a stopped dm is evicted like a stale stream and a failed connect() never stays cached; any new dm.stop() caller must not leave the object in agentdDialDevices**. Dial chat TERMINATE kills the device-side claude via the daemon's `kill-pipe-session` (client killPipeSession) + rm's the session token over the device link — the ssh-only teardown threw for dial and silently orphaned claude (double-JSONL-writer class). Pairing commands render `/api/device-dial`; `/api/agentd-dial` is a PERMANENT alias (in-field daemons hold it in dial.json); env `VIBESPACE_DEVICE_ROOT` preferred, `VIBESPACE_AGENTD_ROOT` honored forever. **RE-PAIR / IDENTITY ROTATION (2.170.0, the userW-incident class): the daemon re-reads dial.json on EVERY dial attempt and the host-token file on EVERY hello — a re-pair heals a RUNNING daemon within one retry; never cache either at startup (the cached identity dialed a dead pairing forever while the singleton blocked its replacement + launchd respawn-spammed). The installer replaces a running same-root daemon (cmdline-verified kill); macOS singleton verifies the lock pid via `ps` (no /proc — recycled pids read as "already running" forever). Multi-instance = one root/daemon per instance (`~/.vibespace/device@<dialhost>`), re-pair replaces within a root.** **REVERSE forward tcp-listen/tcp-accept/tcp-unlisten (2.148.0, the NAT-traversal tunnel)** — daemon binds 127.0.0.1:<port> ON THE DEVICE, accepts push back over the mux as new byte channels (chan ids from 0x40000000 — never collides with server-allocated 2..N; the tcp-accept control MUST precede channel data), server pipes them into its own loopback port; on link death the listener is DISOWNED not closed (port stays bound → reconnecting server re-owns same-port via connect-loop re-registration → remote mounts heal in place); both tcp paths backpressured (credit-after-drain + mux.onWritable resume); --stdio ssh bridge mode; --dial ws dial-out (Transport B; ws-min.js = hand-rolled RFC6455 client — the `head` bytes-with-the-101 upgrade trap is handled, dropping them ate first frames on fast reconnects)). client.js = DeviceManager (transports local/ssh, openSession/openPipeSession/fs*/runCmd/runStream/tcpForward/reverseForward/discovery). Server: /api/agentd-dial upgrade branch (dial-token gated, verifies hosts.dialTokenHash since 2.160.0) + dial-pair mint (writes the hash onto the dial host record; /api/agentd/devices + /api/device-mounts RETIRED in 2.160.0 — roster/test/unpair = /api/hosts, mounts = /api/machine-mounts, see machine-mounts.js). Pairing dialog has per-OS commands (mac/linux bash; WINDOWS EXPERIMENTAL via /agentd-install.ps1, PS 5.1-compatible + win32 named-pipe SOCK in the daemon; the ps1 now also persists state\dial.json + registers a logon SCHEDULED TASK, so a Windows device survives a reboot). **EVERY URL in those commands must come from `httpBase` (= `r.relayUrl || location.origin`), NEVER location.origin directly (2.246.0 bug): a relay-paired device correctly dialed the relay but was handed origin-based installer/bundle URLs its network cannot reach — it failed before the daemon ever started.** MULTI-INSTANCE pairing: dial-out installs root at ~/.vibespace/agentd@<dialhost> (per-instance daemon/tokens/bundle; installer exports VIBESPACE_AGENTD_ROOT — forget that and the daemon reads the default root's token). INSTALLER GOTCHA (2.152.1, real Mac report): macOS has NO setsid(1) — the installer's unconditional `setsid node …` started NOTHING and printed ✓ anyway (stderr redirected into agentd.out); now setsid-or-nohup + kill-0 verification before claiming success. **NODE-FREE PAIRING (2.246.0): the installers (bash AND ps1) no longer REQUIRE node — resolution order is `--node` override → OUR private `$ROOT/node/bin/node` → PATH → newest nvm → common absolute paths → PROVISION (pinned official tarball from nodejs.org/dist, verified against that release's SHASUMS256.txt, smoke-run, then atomically `mv`d into `$ROOT/node`; `--node-only` = resolve/print/exit, the support probe). Private-BEFORE-PATH is deliberate (a launchd plist pointing at an nvm path dies at the user's next nvm upgrade); nvm-before-common-paths is the `curl|bash`-is-non-login lesson (nvm.sh never sourced ⇒ the #1 false 'no node'). Everything lands INSIDE the root, so `rm -rf $ROOT` uninstalls the runtime too — and because agentd's `spawnEnv()` prepends `path.dirname(process.execPath)`, the `#!/usr/bin/env node` agent tools resolve on a machine that never had node (2.244.4 chicken-and-egg, solved structurally). CONSEQUENCE: a provisioned device is PINNED to that node forever (no OS security updates) until the pin is bumped — documented in docs/device-agent.md § Node runtime. RULE: every later node/npm invocation in the installers uses the resolved absolute `$NODE_BIN`/`$NODE_DIR/npm`, NEVER bare `node` (launchd/systemd get no PATH of ours). set -e footguns in that block: `[ -e x ] && mv` and bare `x=$(cmd|pipe)` under pipefail both exit the script — keep new predicates inside `if`/`|| true`. musl + 32-bit Windows refuse honestly with fix instructions (official builds are glibc/64-bit only). Instance-side fallback mirror `GET /vibespace-node/:version/:file` (server.js, auth-exempt, fixed upstream + strict filename allowlist + disk cache in data/node-cache/) for devices that reach this instance but not nodejs.org. Tests: scripts/test-node-bootstrap.mjs (19 asserts, local HTTP fixture + a bwrap node-free sandbox; covers tamper/unlisted-checksum refusal, re-use, nvm discovery, override).** E2E: scripts/dbg-pair-smoke.mjs (17 asserts, REAL daemon dial + mount chain + UI rows). Build: npm run build:agentd → data/bin/vibespace-agentd.js + vibespace-agentd-attach.js (both gitignored artifacts). Tests (all green, 3 environments incl the devbox real-ssh + fleet test pod): test-mux/-agentd/-agentd-session/-agentd-remote/-agentd-robustness (bidi/jitter/latency/concurrency)/-agentd-wired/-agentd-dial/-agentd-m3m4 (18 scenarios)/-agentd-switchover (real-host legacy-vs-device cross-checks). u-vstest soaks with ALL flags on. **2.369.12 push阶梯(inc-mtl78uhs)**: mountpoint的~/$HOME按机器home展开+去尾斜杠(liveness探针比裸路径); rclone daemon子进程--log-file写入dialog tail的同一日志(真实原因不再被--daemon吞掉); macOS阶梯=rclone nfsmount(≥1.66, 免macFUSE)→FUSE mount(有macFUSE时)→mount_webdav, 每级失败都保留展示; 设备rclone pin v1.69.3, 自有旧安装force重装, 系统rclone不动; method 'rclone-nfs'与umount家族同处理.

### machine-mounts.js

  machine-mounts.js    — MachineMounts (B-f3e8 2.160.0 — HostMounts 2.147.0 + DeviceMounts 2.153.0 collapsed into ONE manager; records carry dir:'push'|'pull', keyed by hostId, store data/machine-mounts.json, both legacy stores constructor-migrated → .migrated; routes /api/machine-mounts* (POST :hostId {dir,…}), broadcast machine-mounts-updated). PUSH = mount THIS instance's folder ON the machine, OS-aware (Linux rclone/FUSE; macOS rclone/macFUSE else native mount_webdav — macFUSE probed ON the Mac since 2.160.0, the old check read the SERVER's /dev/fuse; Windows rclone/WinFsp else net use; rclone auto-installed remote-side w/ unzip→busybox→python3 fallbacks). Transport: _davBase prefers the agentd REVERSE-FORWARD TUNNEL (remote mounts http://127.0.0.1:<port>/dav over the device link — NAT-proof; dial machines tunnel-ONLY, ssh falls back to agentd.publicUrl); tunnelPort per record. PULL = the machine's folder mounted INTO this workspace over hosts.device(hostId) (serve-folder→tcp-forward→rclone webdav ro — works for ssh AND dial identically); live chain in-memory, restore()/onMachineLinked heal recorded pulls + re-own push tunnel ports (a re-dial means the daemon RE-EXECED → stale handles torn down first, real Mac report 2.158.1) + 90s child-process-ls health sweep (never node fs on a fuse mountpoint). unmount = best-effort remote teardown but ALWAYS token-revoke + record-drop (dead machine can't hold a credential); host removal → onMachineUnpaired drops all its mounts. GOTCHAS (real e2e catches): mint() returns {raw, rec} not {token,id}; NEVER execFileSync in anything sharing the process with /dav (event-loop block = FUSE IO hang); detached remote rclone needs FULL fd redirect (</dev/null >/dev/null 2>>log) or the ssh ControlMaster never frees; setsid is LINUX-ONLY — conditionally prefixed (macOS died at command-not-found with the error swallowed into the remote log, now tail'd back into the dialog); **TRAILING SLASH on a pull's shared path 403'd EVERY file (2.168.0, real userW Mac→VibeSpace report): the daemon serve-folder confines with `root + path.sep`, so a root ending in `/` (e.g. `/Users/me/Downloads/`) double-slashes the prefix (`…Downloads//`) — the root listing passes but every subpath 403s → rclone "couldn't list files: 403 Forbidden", mount never comes up. PUSH was immune (mountPush runs path.resolve). Fix strips the trailing slash in THREE places: mountPull (intake), _up (heals an existing record without a device-daemon update), serveFolder (daemon defense) — dbg-pair-smoke now pulls a trailing-slash path so its file-read assertion guards it.** Tests: test-machine-migrate.mjs (migration guard) + test-host-mounts(-tunnel).mjs (push e2e) + dbg-pair-smoke.mjs (pull e2e over a real dialed daemon)

### exit-proxy.js

  exit-proxy.js        — ExitProxyManager (task #164, 2.186.0): ON-DEMAND egress — an agent borrows a paired machine's network for a SINGLE command (a region / internal-VPN / fixed source IP), NOT the whole session. Two tiers via the `vibespace-exit` CLI (data/bin, static tracked, in AGENT_TOOLS): `use <machine>`/`url` = the device daemon serves a zero-dep in-daemon SOCKS5 (serve-socks/unserve-socks ops, CONNECT-only, DNS-on-exit) on its loopback, the server reaches it via the existing tcpForward and binds local `socks5h://127.0.0.1:<port>` (tool stays local, egress = the machine; proxy-aware TCP only); `run <machine> -- cmd` = execute natively on the machine via runCmd (universal fallback: ICMP/UDP/proxy-unaware/its DNS). Opt-in per machine (hosts.allowExit, default off — SSRF into its LAN; Remote-tab ↗ toggle). Routes: /api/exits + /api/hosts/:id/allow-exit (cookie UI) + /api/agent/exit[/use|/run] (vsst_).
