The loopback-only tunnel boundary holds (tcp-connect stays never-a-general-proxy; the SOCKS server is the one sanctioned egress, inside the machine owner's network). Tests: test-agentd-socks.mjs + test-exit-proxy.mjs

### port-forward.js

  port-forward.js      — PortForwardManager (B-0b60 tunnel path, 2.165.0): vscode-style port forwarding over the agentd data plane — NO frps/public exposure. detect(hostId) = `ss -tlnH || lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN` over dm.runCmd (lsof matched FIRST — its `(LISTEN)` suffix; an ss IP:port pattern also matches an lsof line; 2.177.0: ss runs with -p for process names, the /proc/net fallback resolves socket inodes→proc via a bounded /proc/*/fd scan, and _classify flags known NON-web daemons `hidden` (both ports UIs fold them behind '+N system listeners', the watch never toasts them; DBs deliberately stay visible); 2.180.0 (B-16d9): scans carry the pid and _classifyOrphan flags LOCAL listeners whose /proc/<pid>/cwd readlink ends ' (deleted)' (zombie dev server from a removed worktree — userL's 2×6GB next-servers) — watch announces each pid once incl. the baseline sweep, ports UIs show an 'orphan' tag + Kill (POST /api/ports/kill-orphan re-verifies deleted-cwd at kill time, never kills a healthy process)); 2.185.0: PROTO chips + override — detect({probe:true}, the UI route only) tags visible ports http/https/tcp via probeHostPort (local direct, remote through a fresh dm.tcpForward duplex; 5-min cache, cache-first + ≤12 uncached probes/scan, 3.5s budget); forwards carry proto/protoDetected/protoOverride (effective = override||detected), `POST /api/port-forward/:id/proto` sets the override and transparently RE-publishes a published forward in the new mode (publish passes the override as frpPublish's proto hint; restore passes override||publicProto so restarts keep the URL shape); forward(hostId, remotePort) binds a local 127.0.0.1:0 server piping each conn into dm.tcpForward(remotePort) (same primitive VNC/device-mounts use; loopback-only on the device — **the device is resolved PER CONNECTION via hosts.device(), never captured at _start (2.169.0): a dial re-dial stop()s the old DeviceManager, so a captured one bricked the forward forever while onMachineLinked skipped the "already live" record**), returns {localPort, url}; 2.186.3: a forward may target `targetHost:port` — another machine on the device's LAN (jump host); daemon tcp-connect gained an optional host (loopback default), the proto probe + record key follow the target, UI manual box accepts ip:port. persists data/port-forwards.json {id:`pf-<host>-<port>`, hostId, remotePort, label}, restore()/onMachineLinked re-establish (fresh local port each time; **re-publish passes preferSub/preferPort so shared public URLs survive restarts**), onMachineUnpaired drops a machine's forwards (**and frpUnpublishes its public URLs first — orphaned relay proxies had no remaining UI handle**; ssh host removal also drops forwards via the DELETE /api/hosts hook). Routes: GET /api/hosts/:id/ports, POST /api/hosts/:id/port-forward, GET /api/port-forwards, DELETE /api/port-forward/:id; broadcast `port-forwards-updated`. UI = sidebar-mounts `_showPortsDialog` (🔌 on a machine row; detected list + manual box + active forwards). **ARCHITECTURE GOTCHA: the forward binds the SERVER's loopback — the user's browser can't reach it directly**, so "Open" routes through the embedded browser's PROXY (`openBrowser(url, {proxy:true})` → node-unblocker on the server → server loopback → tunnel). frps PUBLIC exposure = a separate future path needing the reverse-proxy server infra (B-0b60 frps half). 2.352.0-2.353.0 scan-detail ladder (owner report: docker-host rows had zero detail — `ss -p` only names sockets YOUR user owns): `_enrichDocker(ports, run)` is ONE implementation fed the local execFile runner or the remote dm.runCmd runner — rung 1 = docker's own port table (`docker ps --format`, no root needed) → `docker:<container>`; rung 2 (2.353.0) = `/proc/net/tcp{,6}` uid column + `/etc/passwd` → `user:<name>` for whatever is STILL anonymous (root/other-user listeners; the owner is the best truth available when the process is invisible) — a docker-rung failure must NOT skip the uid rung. 2.353.0 WATCH INVARIANT (owner report: our reverse-tunnel port re-toasted as "new" per session lifecycle): `_watchSweep`'s per-host seen-set ACCUMULATES across sweeps (bounded 4000, clear = one silent re-baseline) — never reset it to the current snapshot; novelty is computed against the set BEFORE recording (seen === prev by reference). Regression-pinned (churn scenario: vanish → reappear stays silent). 2.358.0: publish(id,{sub}) = user-chosen FIXED frp subdomain (persisted publicSub); setPathMount/pathMountTarget = main-domain /svc/<name>/ mounts (unique names, live-port resolution per request — full essay in kb-api.md under POST /api/port-forward/:id/path; proxy = src/server/path-mounts.js, suite scripts/test-path-mounts.mjs in the gate). Tests: test-port-forward.mjs (unit, detect parsing + watch churn + real piping) + dbg-dial-session-e2e.mjs (5, real dialed daemon)

### plugins.js

  plugins.js           — PluginManager (2.140.0, B-2d44): generic host-capability plugins — install + PERSISTENT state (~/.vibespace/plugins/<id>/, home=PVC in fleet) + boot replay (enabled+desiredUp → restart with server) + guided setup + status. First plugin TAILSCALE: dual mode (kernel via /dev/net/tun+NET_ADMIN(sudo) / userspace --tun=userspace-networking + SOCKS5 localhost:1055), system tailscaled detected-never-managed (own --socket/--statedir coexist), login = capture `tailscale up`'s auth URL (Drive-OAuth pattern), node key in statedir ⇒ pod rebuild reconnects sans re-login. Routes /api/plugins* (server.js); UI src/lib/plugins-ui.js (⚙ → Plugins…); helm optional tun device (tun.enabled → NET_ADMIN + device-plugin resource). Second plugin FRP (2.166.0, B-0b60 public-URL half): runs frpc on the instance → a shared frps RELAY, so a forwarded machine port can be PUBLISHED to the public internet as a shareable link. Relay addr/port/token INJECTED via env `VIBESPACE_FRPS_ADDR/_PORT/_TOKEN` (fleet infra, NEVER in the repo — absent ⇒ `configured:false`, plugin no-ops). frpc runs with a base config + a local admin API (127.0.0.1:7400) + `includes` proxies dir; `frpPublish(name, localPort)` writes a per-port proxy toml, `frpc reload`s, polls the admin `/api/status` for `running` (retries a new public port in `VIBESPACE_FRP_PORT_MIN..MAX` on collision — the relay is fleet-shared), returns `http://<relay>:<port>/`. PortForwardManager.publish/unpublish wire it to a forward (persisted, re-published on restore). The relay itself (frps) is set up per the PRIVATE deploy dir runbook (the PRIVATE deploy repo, frp/), NOT this repo. **SUBDOMAIN mode + double-NAT pairing (2.183.0, B-5c1e): with `VIBESPACE_FRPS_SUBDOMAIN_HOST` set (or the plugin's subDomainHost config), frpPublish emits `type:https`+`https2http` proxies → `https://<sub>.<host>/` (persisted preferSub survives restarts). The Pair-a-device dialog's `viaRelay` option self-publishes the instance's own PORT and uses the relay subdomain as the dial base (bundle fetch + device-dial WS both route through it) — so a NAT'd instance can pair a NAT'd device (relay bridges both). Persisted selfDialSub keeps the URL stable across re-pairs. dial-pair route: `viaRelay` branch in server.js; plugin: setSelfDialSub/selfDialSub in status. PROTOCOL-AWARE publish (2.184.0): frpPublish `_probeProto`s the backend — HTTP→routed `type=http` subdomain (relay terminates TLS server-side w/ a wildcard cert via a Caddy front → browser-trusted, ZERO cert on instances), HTTPS→`https://ip:port` passthrough (backend's own cert), raw-TCP→`tcp://ip:port` (a DB/VNC has no Host/SNI). Never blindly https2http. `proto` rides the publish result + `publicProto` on the forward record. Relay side (private runbook): Caddy :443 wildcard-cert termination → frps vhostHTTP :8080; acme.sh Cloudflare DNS-01 auto-renew.** Test: scripts/test-frp-plugin.mjs (live, against the real relay)

### accounts.js

  accounts.js          — AccountManager (per-session billing-identity switching, backend-scoped via a `backend` field on each account — legacy records = 'claude'. THREE account types: (1) Claude API key — AES-256-GCM in data/accounts.json + .accounts-key, injected as ANTHROPIC_API_KEY; (2) Claude SUBSCRIPTION (2.56.0) — a dir data/subs/<id>/ holding ONLY that account's .credentials.json, read by the CLI via CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR (relocates the SECRET store ONLY; projects/sessions/settings stay in ~/.claude so transcripts+discovery stay shared — verified vs claude 2.1.205: Wde()=env??sn(). Empty dir→loggedIn:false, dir-with-creds→that login, ~/.claude untouched. **CONSEQUENCE (2.244.2, forensically verified on 2.1.222): claude's /status Organization/Email reads the NON-relocated ~/.claude.json — a relocated/host-held spawn DISPLAYS the machine login's identity while the TOKEN (= billing) is the dir account's (proof: the spawn refreshes the dir's token, machine store untouched). Never treat /status identity as billing proof for relocated logins; the host-held Test toast explains this to users**); (3) Codex/ChatGPT SUBSCRIPTION (2.59.0) — a dir data/codex-subs/cxs-<id>/ = an isolated CODEX_HOME whose sessions/+config.toml SYMLINK the shared ~/.codex (threads/settings shared, unified discovery — VERIFIED codex 0.142.5: symlinks survive a run, rollout lands in the shared dir), auth.json real per-account; createCodexSubscription/readCodexSubAuth(parses auth_mode + id_token JWT→email/plan)/finalizeCodexSubscription; login = `codex login --device-auth`. resolveForSpawn(requested, backend) returns {kind, localEnv:{VAR:val}, secret?, remoteCreds?} — claude-api→ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, claude-sub→CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR, codex-sub→CODEX_HOME; all PROCESS-ENV only, never argv. Separate defaults per backend (defaultAccountId / defaultCodexAccountId). REMOTE (P3, 2.59.0): subscriptions ship their creds dir to a host via `remoteCreds` — ws-handler tars the whitelisted files (.credentials.json/.claude.json | auth.json), streams over ssh-stdin into 0700 ~/.vibespace/<subs|codex-subs>/<id>, symlinks shared subdirs, prefixes the env var; 2.59.1: NO rm -rf (concurrent same-account sessions), tar --keep-newer-files newest-wins (host-side refresh rotates the live token — don't clobber it) + poison-heal probe (remote creds file missing its validity marker is rm'd pre-extract). list() returns tail-8 for keys, email/plan/loggedIn for subs (read-only). importFromCli() captures a Console primaryApiKey. **remove() POOL HYGIENE (2.335.0, owner report: deleting the account a pool sat on broke the whole pool): deleting a claude subscription strips it from every pool's explicit member list, re-points the default symlink to a live member (or unlinks it — absent = honestly signed-out, a dangling link is a phantom login), and re-points/drops per-session links that billed to it (`_healPoolsAfterRemoval`); gated by test-pool-signed-out (17).** **Re-login identity guard (2.333.0, owner directive): `reloginResolve(id)` finalizes an on-this-machine re-login by COMPARING the fresh login's identity to the record — same ⇒ in-place; matches ANOTHER record ⇒ creds move there ('moved'); unknown ⇒ auto-SPLIT to a new entry named by the email while the original stays signed-out with history intact (a friend logging the wrong account can never rebrand/pollute a record); route POST /api/accounts/:id/relogin-finalize, watcher in manage-agents toasts all outcomes; matrix scripts/test-account-relogin.mjs (13).** Lets the user's OWN subscription logins coexist per backend, switchable per session — the CLI's own /login only keeps one active at a time, so VibeSpace remembers each separately (like signing in/out without losing the others). **§ban-safety (2.59.1, after a real Max ban+refund — see [[reference_multi_subscription]]):** NO background /api/oauth/usage polling with sub tokens (that off-CLI fixed-cadence automated call on idle accounts is the #1 bot-flag: ToS §3.7 + 2026-02-20 OAuth-clarification) — usage is now captured PASSIVELY by data/bin/vibespace-usage (STATIC file), a statusLine hook injected via --settings into LOCAL TERMINAL sessions only that harvests the CLI's OWN rate_limits into data/usage-cache/<key>.json (zero API calls; chat/stream-json has no statusline → last-known; server ingestPassiveUsage reads the dir, no network). Subscription creds shipping to REMOTE is OFF by default (setting accounts.shipSubscriptionToRemote; ws-handler remoteAccountEnv gate throws with guidance) — a sub token from a datacenter IP is an impossible-travel/abuse signal; default = "log in on the host"; API keys always ship (sanctioned programmatic path)) **list() POOLED-FIRST (2.369.18, P1 codex pool UI): the pooled branch is evaluated BEFORE the per-backend branches for every backend — the codex branch used to win, so a codex pool listed as a bare ChatGPT login with no `pooled/current/currentName/memberOptions/auto/hot` (no client surface could operate it AND the engine's `type==='pooled' && a.auto` tick enumeration never saw it). The pool row reads identity THROUGH the symlink with the member's own auth reader (readCodexSubAuth / readSubCreds) and carries `hotSupported` = capsOf(backend).hotSwitch==='verified' (src/backend-caps.js) so the UI hides the hot toggle where the engine ignores it (codex). Pinned in test-codex-pool.**

### accounts.js — LONG-LIVED TOKENS continuation

  (LONG-LIVED TOKENS, B-211a 2.253.0: a claude subscription can hold a `claude setup-token` oat (sk-ant-oat01-, 1yr, NO refresh token, inference-only — mechanism verified vs the 2.1.225 binary: CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN has TOP precedence in the credential getter, adapted as refreshToken:null/expiresAt:null, client never touches the token endpoint). AES-GCM at rest (a.oatEnc); setOat/clearOat/getOat + routes POST|DELETE /api/accounts/:id/oat; resolveForSpawn returns secret:{var:'CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN'} so REMOTE spawns reuse the API-key 0600-file+$(cat) channel (ssh AND dial — the sub ship-gate/dial-no-ship never fire for oat accounts) while loggedIn keeps the local DIR spawn (hot-swap/pool intact); oat-ONLY accounts spawn locally via env token. evaluateOnHost ranks held > linked > 'oat' > ship. EXPIRED oat: create refuses with re-mint guidance (a long-lived 401 has NO self-heal — the string in the binary says re-mint, no fallback to stored creds), logged-in+expired just drops the secret; boot serverNotice ≤21d. AMBIENT STRIP: CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN(+_FILE_DESCRIPTOR) joined AGENT_ENV_DROP + the local spawn env deletes — top-precedence means an inherited copy silently re-bills EVERY spawn; deliberate oat spawns re-add via localEnv AFTER the strip. Quota ⟳ can't ride an oat (no user:profile scope); passive statusline capture unaffected. UI: manage-agents _oatDialog (mint terminal + paste-back; the BROWSER login decides the account — the dialog warns in bold), row tags `· long-lived token` amber<30d red-expired, `· via long-lived token` on host views; New Session + billing switcher offer oat accounts (subBlock's oat short-circuit covers the LOCAL/no-verdict path — locally there ARE no verdicts, the legacy fallback used to grey oat-only accounts as never-signed-in). REVIEW-HARDENED INVARIANTS (17-finding adversarial pass, do not regress): _billingHow has an 'oat' rung BEFORE the remoteCreds 'ship' rung (a logged-in+oat account carries both — 'ship' misreported the exact action oat avoids); the ws-handler explicit-pick probe runs for oatOnly too (oatOnly doesn't throw, so the not-logged-in rescue never arms — without the probe a host-HELD account with an oat silently bypassed held precedence AND the identity-mismatch refusal; held wins, the probe grafts remoteCreds onto the oatOnly shape); hasOat in evaluateOnHost counts only VALID tokens + expired ⇒ reason 'oat-expired' everywhere (a usable expired verdict made the switcher kill-then-fail and let a DEFAULT account silently flip billing to the host login at expiry); export/import/merge all carry oatEnc (oat-only accounts otherwise migrate as dead records); the dial deviceAgentSetup catch throws only when `secret && !_hostSubReady` (held billing rides dialAcctAssign and cannot fail — an oat on the same account must not make degradable tool errors fatal); the expiry sweep is boot+6h-interval; helper terminals must NEVER open while a createModalShell overlay is up (z≈99998 vs window z≈9000 — the mint dialog closes FIRST; general rule for any dialog that opens a terminal); a locally-logged-in account with an expired remote-only oat is NOT blocked locally (subBlock: expired blocks only where the oat is the actual channel). ACCEPTED residuals: setOat cannot distinguish a pasted short-lived OAuth ACCESS token (same sk-ant-oat01 prefix — the dialog copy directs to the setup-token output); dial devices' 0600 .key sweep is ssh-only (same limit as account delete). Matrix: scripts/test-account-verdicts.mjs 45 asserts (incl. the 2.269.0 local-only-mac rung). **INTENDED-USAGE (official docs code.claude.com/docs/en/github-actions, 2.253.1): setup-token/CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN is Anthropic's DOCUMENTED path to run your subscription on remote/CI machines (generate locally → use as a secret on the runner) — cross-machine use is BY DESIGN. But the docs steer to an API key "for a secret shared across repositories … an OAuth token is tied to the subscription of the person who ran claude setup-token", so broad multi-context sharing is OUTSIDE the intended lane — the UI copy says "tied to your subscription, safer than shipping the login, use an API key for broadly-shared credentials", NOT "zero-risk anywhere". §ban-safety reality: oat removes refresh-rotation + shipping the interactive login, NOT the "subscription inference from a datacenter IP" signal (identical to shipping creds) — but it IS the sanctioned mechanism so the ToS posture is better. LIVE E2E scripts/dbg-oat-pool-live.mjs (throwaway server + REAL subscriptions + REAL inference turns, 21 asserts): real claude runs THROUGH the pool symlink, a target switch is picked up by a fresh spawn, a normal + a pooled session on one account resolve to the SAME real dir (shared refresh lock, verified via /proc), an oat-only account completes a real turn via the env token, ambient CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN is stripped from a normal spawn (/proc-verified). SAFETY: copies of subs with >1.5h token life ONLY, so a short test can never trigger a refresh that would rotate production's single-use token; production instance never restarted, its accounts.json/creds only READ.)

### account-pool-auto.js

  account-pool-auto.js — Pooled pseudo-account (B-6217, 2.251.0-2.252.0): account type 'pooled' — ONE switchable billing identity over the logged-in Claude subscriptions. The pool's creds dir data/subs/<poolId> IS a DIRECTORY SYMLINK to the current target's dir (the shape the whole design rests on — see the hot-swap SOLVED note in the billing section); switching = accounts.setPoolTarget (symlink-to-temp + rename + utimes on the target's creds so the CLI's mtime-gated cache invalidates). remove() UNLINKS only (rmSync on a symlinked dir THROWS, probed — never reaches the target). Members default = ALL logged-in claude subscriptions (explicit list narrowable; narrowing away the current target re-points). LOCAL-ONLY (evaluateOnHost verdict pool-local-only; macOS keychains key on the env STRING so the sharing breaks there). Attribution: passive statusline key + ledger by-time attribution both resolve pool→real target (recordUsageAttribution + the spawn env). **USAGE ACCOUNTING (2.256.0, user #4): the pool id is ALSO baked as a separate `pool` tag on each event** while `acct` stays the real target — so per-account $ and the global sum stay exactly correct with NO double-count, AND the Usage window gains a "By pool" breakdown (classic view) + a `pool` dashboard dimension showing the total that flowed THROUGH each pool (a legitimately-overlapping SEPARATE lens, never added into totals). recordUsageAttribution captures pool separately + dedups by `acct|pool`; recordAttribution writes it to attribution.ndjson; `_poolAt(sid,ts,attrib)` (by-time walk mirroring `_acctAt`) bakes `pool` onto Claude + Codex events at scan; aggregate() adds a sparse `pool` dim (SKIPS the null bucket so non-pooled events don't collect). Historical pre-2.256.0 events carry no pool tag (fine — they just don't appear in the pool lens). v2: `auto` = at claude turn end (maybePoolAutoSwitch in server.js), current target <5% remaining (MIN across 5h/7d/scoped-weekly; account-pool-auto.js pure decisions — reset-passed bucket = full, unknown member = 50, unknown CURRENT = never switch, 60s throttle) → re-point, re-record ledger attribution, and (cold) ask exactly ONE connected client to restart affected conversations via `pool-auto-switched` (headless = de-facto hot until a client appears). **v3 EDF RANKING (2.259.0, user-designed): quota is PERISHABLE — the target choice is earliest-WEEKLY-deadline-first, not most-remaining** (KEY FACT, user-confirmed + cache-verified ±1s: scoped weeklies like Fable are COMPONENTS of the same 7d window — same resetsAt — so each account has ONE weekly deadline and per-account EDF is optimal; the coupled-bucket objection collapses). 5h is EXCLUDED from ranking (burst limiter refilling ~33×/week — else every account's soonest reset is its 5h and the sort is noise) but stays in the <5% usability GATE. Same deadline ±60s → more remaining first (equal expiry ⇒ order can't change utilization; fewer switches); known-deadline members outrank unknown; fresh-after-reset (deadline unknowable from stale cache) ranks last. **HOT pools get a PROACTIVE tier** (`proactive: !!a.hot`): switch toward a strictly-sooner deadline (≥1h margin, never onto unknown data) BEFORE exhaustion — hot re-points are free; cold pools stay exhaustion-only (each switch restarts conversations). Test: scripts/test-pool-auto.mjs (29). **v4 ESTIMATED VIEWS (2.263.0, B-fcff): maybePoolAutoSwitch's readCache overlays dead-reckoned utilizations (usage-estimator estimateFor→overlayCache) so decisions see NOW, not the last stale reading; hot pools pass exhaustPct 10 (est<10% 提前切 before a limit interrupts a long task), cold keep 5; candidate gate stays 5.** `hot` = re-point only, no restart — the CLI re-reads creds on its next request (2.1.222-verified). ZERO API calls anywhere in the auto path (§ban-safety — decisions read data/usage-cache/*.json only). KNOWN SEAM: hot re-point leaves a terminal session's VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_KEY on the old target until restart (ledger correct either way). UI: Manage Agents + Add account… → pooled; row `→ target · email`; ⋯ Switch target / Members… / Auto / Hot. **Members dialog (2.257.0, user request): checkbox per claude subscription + an 'All subscriptions' option = the store's DYNAMIC default (members:null — includes accounts added LATER, the user's 全选 semantics), explicit selection = fixed narrowed set; an empty explicit selection is REFUSED in the dialog (updatePool maps [] → null = All, a silent surprise otherwise).** **v6 PER-KIND THRESHOLDS (2.268.2, user-designed: ABSOLUTE headroom decides — 1% of 7d ≈ \$17 vs 1% of 5h ≈ \$2-5): THRESH = 5h{hot 10, hard 5} / weekly{hot 5, hard 3} (7d + scoped share 'weekly'); exhaustion = ANY bucket under its kind's threshold, candidate gate = every bucket ≥ its hard floor, settle bar = every bucket ≥ kind hot + MIN_GAIN. decidePoolSwitch takes `hot` (exhaustPct param retired); a weekly-88% member is a healthy EDF target (~\$200 headroom) — the immediate effect that pulled Personal back as a target. Test suite 38 rewritten per-kind.** **v5 OSCILLATION GUARD (2.266.1, real incident: Personal<10% ⇄ Fish every tick — soft-exhaustion one direction, EDF-soonest the other, each hot re-point cold-starting BOTH sides' prompt caches): every VOLUNTARY move (soft-exhaustion at the raised hot threshold OR proactive EDF) must land on a member clearing the SETTLE BAR (exhaustPct+MIN_GAIN, i.e. the target won't itself trip exhaustion) — applied at candidate SELECTION (a below-bar EDF-first member is skipped for a later qualifying one); hard-dead (<5%) still takes scraps (best.eff > cur+MIN_GAIN). Engine belt in server.js: `_poolSwitchAt` — 180s dwell after any actual switch before the next voluntary move (hard-death exempt).** **v7 LIVENESS (2.312.0, real incident): an optimality ranking must never be the only escape hatch.** The hard-dead branch took `ranked[0]` — the EDF pick — but a member whose weekly window just ROLLED has no known deadline and sorts LAST by design, so a fully-fresh account was invisible and a pool at 0% with a 100%-on-every-bucket member present returned null for hot AND cold (reproduced from the reporter's own usage-cache shape). Now: `bestSettle || max MEASURED remaining` — the headroom scan excludes unknown members because their fabricated `UNKNOWN_REMAINING_PCT` beats every real reading and turns escape into a jump onto ignorance (caught by the existing anti-flap assert = built-in negative control). Paired no-silent-failure: `decidePoolSwitch({explain:true})` returns `{to:null, reason:'stuck'|'no-members'|'no-settleable'|'healthy'|…}` (the historical null contract is unchanged without the flag) and the engine notices on EVERY blocked reason — 2.312.0 surfaced only `stuck`, and the candidate gate dropping every member returns through `no-members`, i.e. the same incident down a different branch (2.313.0). TWO rules for any new notice here: the key must carry an hour bucket (`_sentNotices` is a per-BOOT permanent Set, so an unbucketed key reports a recurrence never again), and it must NAME THE BUCKET — buckets carry a reporting `label` (`5h`/`7d`/scoped name) because "every member is out of quota" is wrong under the nested model and points at the opposite user action from the truth ("Fable 0% spent, 7d still 39%" means run a different model, not wait a week). **PER-SESSION LINKS (plan C, 2.315.0 — B-a612 SHIPPED, user-decided): a local pooled session gets its OWN directory symlink `data/pool-links/<poolId>/<sessKey>` → the chosen member; the default `data/subs/<poolId>` link stays the state for display + identity-less sessions. Model identity = newest(last-assistant served model, set-model pick) else spawn model — persisted (spawnModel/pickedModel in session meta, restored in BOTH restore blocks). Placement + per-session auto-switch decide on the engine's SAME estimator-overlaid readCache PROJECTED to the session's family (src/model-family.js — the family vocabulary's shared home; unknown family ⇒ NO projection, never relax on ignorance); the switch re-points ONLY that session's link (per-session dwell keys; cold restarts only the moved session; pool-level `affected` skips own-link sessions). Attribution + resolveUsageKey + the billing badge resolve pool→poolCurrentFor(pool, webuiId) = the SESSION's target. Lifecycle: create at spawn / drop at kill / sweepSessionPoolLinks at boot; pool remove clears the links dir (symlinks only). Sealed orders carry `linkPaths`; the daemon matches a banner to the LONGEST-matching link and re-points only it (old daemons ignore the field = reduced coverage, never a wrong re-point). Invariant pinned in test-account-pool (31): N links → M accounts still leave ONE real dir per account = one refresh lock.** **MANUAL HOT-SWITCH SWEEPS LIVE LINKS (2.355.0, userW's inc-msz495u6 "热切换死了"): plan C silently demoted the manual target change to new-sessions-only — poolCurrentFor prefers the session's own link, and setPoolTarget only moved the DEFAULT symlink (verified live: default → new member, 16 live links → old member). setPoolTarget(id, subId, {sweepSessionLinks}) now repoints every live per-session link when the USER route passes it (gated on pool.hot; logged '[pool] manual target → X: repointed N live session link(s)'); the ENGINE's calls never pass it — its per-session moves ARE the model-family projections a blanket sweep would clobber. Both semantics pinned (test-account-pool 33).** **MODEL BLINDNESS (the pre-2.315.0 state — kept for the exhaustion-test half that remains):  every model-scoped weekly is flattened to kind 'weekly' and `accountRemaining` is min-across-all, so one spent cap for a model NOBODY IS USING declares the account unusable and gates it out as a candidate; and one pool = ONE creds dir (`resolveForSpawn` returns `subDir(poolId)`), so concurrent conversations on different models always bill the same account. Adversarially verified: buckets are NESTED (5h + 7d + the family's cap when the account exposes one — `seven_day_opus:null` is real), and the server can NOT reliably know a live session's model (`_servedModel` is written only in the chat branch, never persisted, stale between set-model and the next assistant record) — so any model-aware gating must fall back to today's behaviour on ANY unknown. Design + real-data evidence: SharedContext/vibespace-pool-quota-model.md.** Tests: test-account-pool.mjs (16) + test-pool-auto.mjs (51, incl. the oscillation pair, the liveness set + its negative control, and the model-scoped semantics) + test-creds-symlink-swap.mjs (8) **codex池(2.368.20, P2冷切v1): createPool({backend}), 池symlink在data/codex-subs/<poolId>, 决策核心原样复用(codex读数同桶形), 热切结构性禁用等P3 symlink-swap验证; 信号=wrapper转发的rate_limits_updated+typed codex_error_info→recordCodexQuotaSignal(读数落当前成员cache, 耗尽先池切后arm auto-resume); sealed orders/plan-C保持claude-only。test-codex-pool(17)。** **2.368.21: codex撞限逃生梯=reset credit(codex.limitResetCredit默认off, wrapper动词codex-reset-credit/codex-read-limits经live app-server RPC)→池切→auto-resume; 引擎门控走src/backend-caps.js能力注册表(hot只在hotSwitch=verified——codex实验判定impossible: CODEX_HOME启动canonicalize+auth内容换垃圾turn照常完成即token驻内存)。test-codex-pool(28)。** **codex池UI(2.369.18, P1): accounts.list()池分支对所有backend先于codex分支求值(此前codex池行无pool字段, 客户端无法操作+引擎auto tick枚举不到); manage-agents两个名册共用ONE池菜单块`_poolMenuItems`(Switch target/Members…/Auto-switch/Hot——hot项按服务器`hotSupported`能力判定, codex显示禁用行说明结构性冷切)+`_createPoolDialog`(api()而非fetchJson: createPool 400曾被吞成'已创建')+`ROSTER_ICONS`/`rosterSort`共享(codex名册此前只按名排序); Members对话框按池的backend筛成员; 三处错标修正: server.js sessionAuth codex分支对池返回source:'pooled'+poolTarget(状态栏芯片/标题栏徽章/会话属性此前把codex池当普通ChatGPT账号), usage-meter codex芯片排除池(池不是配额持有者), session-card ⚙账号列表池行'name → target'(曾是'— API …undefined'), session-props计费行pooled/codex-subscription/codex-cli不再'Unknown'。**

**AUTH-FAILURE EVICTION (2.335.0, owner report: 封号/欠费/过期 never triggered a switch — quota was the engine's only signal and a dead-auth account often still SHOWS rich quota):** pure `classifyAuthFailure({status,message,attempt})` (403 immediate; 401 needs attempt≥2 — a lone first 401 is the mid-refresh-race shape; ban/credit/oauth-expired messages immediate; 5xx/529 never). Engine side (usage-pool-engine): `notePoolAuthFailure(session, sid, info)` fed from session-stdout's api_retry + error-result records — marks the member (10min TTL, cleared early when the creds file is REWRITTEN i.e. re-login), re-points the session's link + the pool default off it via the same rankPoolMembers EDF, notices the user, cold pools get the restart ask; quota eval + chooser exclude marked members (`healthyPoolMembers`, falling back to the full list when EVERY member is marked). Runs regardless of `auto` — routing around a dead account is the 2.330.2 heal's live-session sibling, not quota optimization. **2.335.1 review hardening: evict throttle per (member,session) not per member (else sibling conversations stay pinned 60s each); default-move collects ALL default-billing sessions into the cold-pool restart ask; auth-mark clears on TOKEN-MATERIAL change never mtime (repointPoolSymlink utimes-bumps creds on every re-point); message-only classification needs API-layer context (`API_CTX_RE`) so agent tool output can't evict; removing a pool's LAST explicit member keeps the stale list (null = ALL subs — silent widening re-pointed billing to excluded accounts). ALSO: the restore smoke now overlays the WORKING TREE (pre-commit runs used to test HEAD — how 2.335.0's lost engine binding passed a green smoke).**

**WALL = GROUND TRUTH + OBSERVED-ORG AWARE EVALUATION (B-2c9b, owner-approved plan A+B 2026-09-05; usage-pool-engine).** Three same-day production occurrences: a walled turn's `onWalledTurn` read the POOL scope and logged "usable via <linked member>" off the linked member's healthy cache, while `usage-limit-banner-marked` had landed on a DIFFERENT account — the org the CLI was actually running on (193 `usage-reading-reattributed` events had moved the session's readings there); the pool only moved 1.5-3 min later when an edf/exhaustion reading arrived, and in-flight subagents died with "session limit" in the gap. **Invariant: a wall is ground truth for the account it lands on; readings only confirm.** ① **Plan A — demotion on BLOCKED entry.** Every wall signal carries the org-verified cache key its mark landed on (`noteWallSignal(session, {resetsAtMs, bucket, scopedName, key})` — claude rejected event, banner boolean (bucket = the name parseLimitBanner gave the cache mark; still NO time from text), all three codex sites) and a per-account ring of walled-turn timestamps (`_wallRing`, one entry per turn per key — a turn's banner + rejected event are ONE wall; 120s window, `wallCount` sums the identity group). `demoteWalledAccount(session, sigs)` resolves the wall's member (the last signal's key, else `orgVerifiedKey(session, usageCacheKeyFor(session), 'wall')`; pool members or the linked member only — non-pooled sessions return `not-pooled`, their own cache mark already is the truth) and marks the affected bucket exhausted NOW through `captureRateLimitEvent(…, source:'wall')` — the SAME write path readings use (utilization 1, status limited, resetsAt = the signal's future reset else the cached future reset else the bounded 5h/24h guess; fetchedAt now, so anchors / the estimator (re-anchors on the newer rawCache) / verdicts see it in this tick). **Misattribution guard (the 2.368.34 class — the link is never the org a running CLI is on):** demote only when the account saw ≥2 walled turns inside 120s (any sessions) OR it IS the session's OTel-observed org (≤10min); a single wall on an account the session is not verifiably on HOLDS (journal `[wall] <id>: single wall on <name> (session not observed there) — holding the demotion`, event `wall-demote-held`) — the near-fire/pre-fire probe or the next wall inside 120s settles it. Journal `[wall] demoted <name> <bucket> until <iso> (n walls / observed-org)`, event `wall-demote`. ② **Plan B — observed-org aware evaluation.** `observedMemberFor(session, poolId)` = the OTel truth stream's latest org for the session (`OBSERVED_ORG_RECENT_MS` = 10min) resolved to a pool member; `sessionCurrentMember` = observed else link ({id, linkedId, observedId, divergent}), logging the divergence once per session per 10min (`[pool] session <id> observed on <X> while linked to <Y>`, event `pool-observed-divergence`). It drives: `resolveUsageKey` for pooled sessions (the live odometer feeds, probe-session matching and every derived cache key now attribute to the observed member — pooled sessions with a fresh observation therefore resolve straight to it and `usage-reading-reattributed` counts drop for them, by design); `quotaVerdictFor(scope, {model, session})` (the current member is judged FIRST so `via` names the account the session is on; the verdict carries `on/linked/divergent` + a reason note); the per-session auto-switch pass (decides FROM the observed member — a session walled on it moves even when its link reads healthy; when the chosen target is the link itself the re-point is journal-only `(re-point, same target)` + the creds-mtime bump the CLI re-reads on, no user notice); both probe targets (wall ladder + pre-fire gate). The hot-switch symlink still targets the CHOSEN member — this changes what the engine BELIEVES the session is burning, never the material. ③ **Order on BLOCKED entry** (`noteTurnEnd` → `onWalledTurn`): demote → session-aware verdict → arm → pool evaluation in `finally` (AFTER the arm, so a hot switch's `fireNow()` finds the session armed and continues it in the same tick; the normal-turn branch keeps its own `maybePoolAutoSwitch`). Gate: test-auto-resume §11 (functional — a REAL AccountManager pool with real per-session links + the real engine + the real auto-resume module + a fake OTel source, fixtures shaped from the journal: one wall holds, two walls inside 120s demote with the signal's reset and switch+continue in ONE noteTurnEnd, observed-org divergence names the observed member and moves the link, stale observations fall back, named non-demotions, the ring counts turns not records, order-of-operations pins) + test-rate-limit-capture ⑧ (the `source` option).

### harnesses/ (the harness registry — S4 QuotaSignalSource)

  harnesses/index.js — THE harness registry (docs/design-harness-plugins.md §2.2 "src/harnesses/index.js 是唯一注册表"; S4 landed it with the QUOTA contract first, later slices add theirs beside it). `get(id)` is LOUD on an unknown or empty id — core never falls through to claude (the gemini-as-claude class); `has/list/ids`; `register(descriptor, {replace})` for contributed harnesses (built-ins can never be replaced/unregistered; a duplicate contributed id needs the explicit flag — silent shadowing is how twins are born); `assertQuotaContract` validates every registration (normalize/signalFromStream/classifyAuthFailure functions + `probe ∈ QUOTA_PROBE_RUNGS` = 'cli-usage'|'rpc-rate-limits'|null); `NULL_QUOTA` (null-quota.js, frozen) is the shell descriptor's source AND the engine's degrade target for an unknown id. Descriptors: claude.js / codex.js / shell.js = `{id, label, quota}`. SHARED tier (test-architecture): pure hooks over SHARED parsers, never reaching up into ORCH — the daemon may bundle them; the S5 stream CONSUMERS themselves stayed ORCH (src/server/stdout/, below) because they need the engine/hosts/ledger, so a descriptor only names the protocol.
  harnesses/claude-quota.js — the claude QuotaSignalSource. `normalize(raw, nowMs)` dispatches by SHAPE over the three claude reading channels: a string = the `claude -p /usage` panel text (parseCliUsageText, MOVED here verbatim from usage-routes — the MONTHS/zonedEpoch/_parseCliResetTime helpers with it), an object with `rate_limits` = the get_usage control payload (ClaudeCodeAdapter.parseGetUsageResponse, unchanged), an object with five_hour/seven_day/limits[] = the GET /api/oauth/usage REST JSON (parseOAuthUsage, MOVED verbatim = usage-routes' former `_parseUsage`; the route + the device-op consumer bind the same function). `signalFromStream(record)`: `rate_limit_event` → `{source:'rate_limit_event', kind: rejected⇒'exhausted' | utilization⇒'reading' | else 'meta', bucket, scopedName, resetsAtMs, ev}` (the parse is rate-limit-capture's parseRateLimitEvent — reached through the registry, no twin); an assistant text matching the LIMIT BANNER regex → `{source:'limit-banner', kind:'exhausted', bucket, scopedName, resetsAtMs:0}` (a BOOLEAN wall signal — design-wall-machine §1: text is never a data source; parseLimitBanner only names the bucket for the passive cache mark). `probe = capsOf('claude').quotaProbe = 'cli-usage'`. `classifyAuthFailure` = account-pool-auto's Anthropic-wording classifier, the SAME function object (pinned).
  harnesses/codex-quota.js — the codex QuotaSignalSource, PURE. `normalize` = the former usage-routes normalizeCodexRateLimit MOVED verbatim (2.368.18 P0 semantics: windows classify by LENGTH ≤480min⇒fiveHour else sevenDay, never by primary/secondary position; every exhaustion marker survives: rateLimitReachedType/spendControlReached/credits; the tripped window reads dead) — usage-routes re-exports it under the old name and the engine reads it through the registry. Owns the typed exhaustion vocabulary `EXHAUSTION_RE` (usage_limit_reached|quota_exceeded|usage_not_included|workspace_owner_usage_limit_reached|workspace_member_usage_limit_reached|workspace_member_credits_depleted) and `trippedWindow(snap)` (primary→burst window when present else weekly; secondary→weekly else burst — the engine's 2.368.20 rule). `signalFromStream(record)` accepts the server frame `{type:'event_msg', payload}` OR the bare payload: `rate_limits_updated` → kind 'exhausted' (with `tripped` + resetsAtMs) | 'reading' | 'probe-failed' (the on-demand `{error}` reply — never silent), snapshot carries the on-demand resetCredits count; `task_failed` → 'exhausted' (errorInfo, resetsAtSec/Ms, snapshot when one rode along) | 'auth-failure' (the typed `unauthorized` enum only — a plain error text is not a quota signal) | null; `reset_credit_result` → 'recovered' | 'reset-credit-failed'. `probe = 'rpc-rate-limits'`. `classifyAuthFailure` in OpenAI/codex wording (typed unauthorized; "401 Unauthorized"/invalid_grant/token expired|revoked/not logged in/org|account deactivated|suspended; 403 immediate; 401 needs attempt≥2 — the same refresh-race rule as claude; 5xx/429 never). Codex auth-failure EVICTION is still v1 surfaced-only (`codex-auth-failure` event): the engine's member mark keys on the claude creds path (credsTokenSig) — the S2 accounts strategy object owns that arm.
  **THE CAPS-ROUTED QUOTA PROBE (S4, usage-pool-engine `probeQuotaForKey(target, {session, timeoutMs=20000})`)** — ONE dispatcher for every "refresh this identity's quota now" need: the wall machine's BLOCKED-entry verification probe, the wall probe ladder (0→30m→1h→2h), and the auto-resume pre-fire gate (`beforeAutoResumeFire`). `quotaBackendFor(key, session)` names the identity's harness (a named account's `backend` — legacy records = claude, same rule as accounts._acctBackend/capsOf; `__global_codex__` = codex; identity-less keys `__global__`/`host-*` take the asking session's own backend; else claude) and `quotaSourceFor(backend).probe` names the rung: **'cli-usage'** = `getQuotaProbe()` = usage-routes refreshViaCliPanel (`claude -p /usage`, the owner-approved auto-cli channel — server.js wiring unchanged, still the ONE spawn site); **'rpc-rate-limits'** = pick a LIVE local codex chat session on the identity (the asking session first — it sits idle-walled on exactly that identity with its app-server up; else any session whose `codexQuotaKeyFor` = account→pool current member→`__global_codex__` lands in the target's identity group), write the wrapper verb `{type:'codex-read-limits'}` (account/rateLimits/read on the session's own app-server — the official client makes the fetch, §ban-safety class unchanged) and await the next `rate_limits_updated` through `session._codexLimitsWaiters` (registered in session-schema; `recordCodexQuotaSignal` settles the waiters AFTER the cache write so the verdict taken next already reads the fresh file; a failed on-demand read settles `ok:false` with the wrapper's error; a bounded timeout drops the waiter); **null** (shell / unknown harness → NULL source) = nothing runs, the verdict stands. Before S4 every probe spawned `claude -p /usage` whatever the backend — a codex identity burned a pointless claude process and never got a fresh reading (design-harness-plugins.md §1 P2). Result `{ok, rung, backend, reason}` — every miss is named. Unknown harness id ⇒ one console.warn + `quota-harness-unknown` event + the NULL source, never a claude parse. Gate: scripts/test-quota-source.mjs (functional: real engine.create() with fake sessions — codex identity rides the live session + never spawns claude; claude identity keeps cli-usage; shell/unknown run nothing; timeout + failed read honest; beforeAutoResumeFire routed; + registry contract, every harness's normalize/signalFromStream/classifyAuthFailure on real shapes, wiring pins, and a vendor-whitelist sub-run).

### rate-limit-capture.js

  rate-limit-capture.js — PASSIVE quota capture from the CLI's own `rate_limit_event` stream-json records (B-e5c9, 2.289.0; discovered by 2.1.226 binary forensics — a first-class stdout record "emitted when rate limit info changes" VibeSpace dropped as unknown forever). Rides real API responses ⇒ ZERO extra calls (§ban-safety: purely passive; the banned auto-get_usage and a billable `-p` probe are both worse AND `-p` must carry a prompt + returns no 5h/7d data). Closes the chat/stream-json gap (no statusline ⇒ last-known quota only). `parseRateLimitEvent(msg)` (both key casings, 2.227.6; utilization integer-%→0..1; all 16 rate_limit_info fields) + `captureRateLimitEvent({cacheDir,key,identityIds,ev})` — ONE impl for local AND remote (remote chat stdout relays through the same server parse at server.js:1616; `usageCacheKeyFor` routes a host-login remote session to `host-<id>` not `__global__`, also fixing remote banner attribution). `status:'rejected'`⇒bucket dead+pool re-eval (banner parity); `utilization`⇒usage-cache write that becomes a dead-reckoning ANCHOR next sweep (estimator self-calibrates from live chat). Carries the 2.267.0 identity-group anti-poison discipline + ONE addition: **`fetchedAt` bumps ONLY for a real READING** (utilization or rejected) — a resetsAt/overage-only event updates fields in place WITHOUT promoting a stale file to 'freshest' (false-anchor poison). Server: recordRateLimitEvent in the claude stdout dispatch. Test: scripts/test-rate-limit-capture.mjs (20). **B-2c9b: `captureRateLimitEvent({…, source})` labels the reading write (default 'rate-limit-event'; the wall machine's ground-truth demotion passes 'wall') — ONE write path for readings AND walls, never a twin writer (suite ⑧).**

### usage-anchors.js

  usage-anchors.js     — Quota dead-reckoning DATA FOUNDATION (2.261.0, user-designed 惯性导航 step 1): every ground-truth usage reading (statusline/⟳/get_usage/limit-banner — any usage-cache write) becomes an ANCHOR paired with the local ledger cost since the previous anchor (split by model family so Fable-bucket rates stay derivable) in data/usage-anchors/anchors-<identity>.ndjson; 60s local sweep (sweepUsageAnchors in server.js), zero API calls. **PRIMARY KEY = identity (orgUuid > lowercased email > account id), NOT the minted sub-<hex> id** — a sub's history survives remove+re-add (re-add mints a fresh id; same-login re-add WITHOUT remove already merges). Raw pairs are kept forever (never just an EWMA) so prediction models re-derive offline. **2.263.0: the sweep GROUPS BY IDENTITY** (an org-merged login — machine login + named sub — double-anchored one identity from two cache files, each record's costSince missing the sibling's spend; real data bug from the ProblemFactory analysis): one identity = one anchor stream, freshest cache wins as source, cost sums across ALL the identity's account ids via `costBetweenMulti` (union incl. ids from the anchor file — removed/re-added subs). Anchors also carry `calib` (predicted-vs-actual per bucket, from usage-estimator.predictCalib) + `accountIds`. Empirical constraint (recorded in SharedContext): a real account's LOCAL ledger saw only ~1/7 of its consumption (multi-device) — fixed token→% rates are impossible, per-identity learning is mandatory; conversely a POD-EXCLUSIVE account (ProblemFactory) measured rate≈1.0 and the odometer method (cost(window)/u) recovered its quotas to ±3% (5h≈$500, 7d≈$1730, Fable≈$875 — Max 20x). Test: scripts/test-usage-anchors.mjs. **2.340.0 calibration batch (owner audit: bursts under-estimated 20-45%): pairs require SAME-SOURCE endpoints; ≥5pt jumps need cost corroboration (≥20% of prior-implied) or taint; org-merged identities union '__global__' cost under a 14d stream-recency gate (deliberate 2.263 reversal, both semantics tested); estimator extrapolates the ~20s un-scanned ledger tail (lagS knob); seven_day_<model> rate_limit_events write scopedWeekly readings — the scoped buckets' only passive channel.** 2.369.36: costBetweenMulti is interval-memoized (key ids|from|to|events-at-or-before-to, 20k entries) — anchor pairs are immutable history; only a backfill INTO the interval recomputes it. **2.369.43 (review batch) closed the key's two holes: the PRICE TABLE is part of the key** (`usageHistory.pricingToken()`, a sha1 of {tiers,accounts} recomputed only when `_pricing` is replaced) — a tier edit or a per-account discount changes every historical cost while the event count is untouched, so the memo served pre-edit dollars into the learned rates forever (repro: 10x tier edit and a 50% discount both froze at the old total); a ledger that cannot version its prices is NOT memoized. **Eviction picks a RANDOM victim, not FIFO**: learnRates re-walks one identity's pairs in the same order each recompute = a cyclic reference string, where FIFO/LRU cliff (measured: 20000 keys 100% hits, 21000 keys 0.0% — the inc-mtox23xw per-pair walk returns) while random decays as cap/working-set (53% at 1.3x cap). The working set is real and grows: production carries 5124 qualifying pairs over 28 days across 8 identities (~180/day, biggest identity 1126) and anchors are never pruned. The memo hangs off the UsageHistory instance (WeakMap), never the module.

### usage-estimator.js

  usage-estimator.js   — Quota dead-reckoning ESTIMATOR (B-fcff v2, 2.263.0): est_u = anchor_u + learned_rate × ledger_cost_since_anchor, per identity per bucket. Rates = WLS through origin over ALL anchor pairs (Σ Δu / Σ cost — integer-% quantization noise averages out; NEVER use a single pair, ±3×) blended with the measured Max-20x priors (CLAUDE_MAX_PRIOR_FULL_USD 5h $500 / 7d $1730 / scoped:fable $875; pseudo-pair weight PRIOR_WEIGHT_DU=0.15 so real data dominates by ~15% observed movement; Pro accounts start off and self-correct — banner is the hard backstop). Pair guards (2.265.0 calib-driven additions FIRST: **at-cap readings u≥0.995 censor the pair** (banner marks + exhausted ⟳ carry no linear information — the 9%→100%-over-$50 pair taught full≈$139 vs true $500) and **pair costs RECOMPUTE from the live ledger at learn time** (frozen costSince lags fast burns; 10-min rate-cache TTL; zero-live-total falls back to the snapshot)); reset moved >120s inside a pair = void; u-decrease >0.02 without reset change = anomaly skip; small negatives clamp to 0 with cost KEPT. Window-roll estimation: 7d/scoped re-base from 0 at the passed reset boundary (+7d steps — fixed cadence); **5h ABSTAINS after its reset (window opens at first-request-after-idle, start unknowable)**. UsageEstimator class: mtime-cached anchor reads, rate memo invalidated per new anchor, 30s estimate memo (taskbar polls /api/usage at 8s), rates.json snapshot for offline inspection; **usageHistory is passed as a LAZY () => ref — the estimator is constructed ~2900 lines above usageHistory's declaration (TDZ boot crash, smoke-caught)**. **MEMO STALENESS (review major, do not regress): the 30s estimate memo records baseFetchedAt and BUSTS the moment rawCache.fetchedAt is newer — a frozen estimate otherwise falsely trips the est<reading roll display AND masks a limit-banner mark (utilization 1) from the pool's immediate switch.** More review-hardened guards: resetsAt-less buckets (statusline defensive `resetsAt:0`) dead-reckon only within ONE window length then abstain (a 3-day anchor + $600 cost rendered a 120% 5h arc, verifier-repro'd); `status:'unknown'` fabricated buckets never anchor (maybeRecord nulls them — a u:0 fabrication + next real reading forged ~6× rate inflation); legacy pre-2.263 interleaved records (org-merge under-counted cost) excluded from learning via the accountIds grouped-record signature (mixed-id files learn from marked pairs only); scoped pairs with identical asOf carry no information (skipped); MIN_GAIN_PCT=3 anti-flap on the pool exhaustion tier. **2.268.0/2.268.1 5h TOKEN-CLASS REGRESSION (B-536b; 3-class final): the 5h bucket ≈ INPUT-CLASS token count with ~ZERO weight on cache READS — real-data 3-var LS across 3 identities in tight agreement: cr-full $2660-2782, fresh $179-192, cw $190-261 (cw's premium over fresh = exactly our 1.25-2× cache-write pricing; the interim 2-class 'cw burns half' was an artifact of cr hiding in both groups). learnRates fits du = rateCw×cw$ + rateCr×cr$ + rateFresh×fresh$ per identity (pure-class priors $500/$3000/$200, PRIOR_WEIGHT_DU each; per-class sanity bounds + positive coefficients withhold absurd fits → blended fallback), costBetweenMulti emits byClass{cw,cr,other} (historical pairs get splits RETROACTIVELY via the learn-time recompute), the live ring carries cwUsd+crUsd, estimateBuckets predicts per component when the source has the split. 7d/scoped stay blended (long windows average the mix; calib error already 0-3pts). Incremental caching makes conversation windows cr-DOMINATED — that's why the 2-class version still overestimated (60-vs-51; 3-class re-predicts +2.5 vs actual +3).** **2.267.3 EST-2× (live-odometer double count, arithmetic-confirmed: 2×$34.73/$256.5 = the exact 27% the user saw vs ⟳ 9%): STDOUT records carry NO requestId — ring entries key on msg.id while ledger events key on requestId, so `known.has(rid)` NEVER excluded a scanned entry and every streamed request counted twice while a conversation was active. Ledger events now bake `mid` (message.id), _loadEvents keeps a `mids` set, _liveDelta excludes against BOTH id spaces; a re-noted rid upgrades to max-usd (stdout emits the same msg.id up to 3× with growing usage). JOIN-FIELD RULE: any dedup between the stream and the ledger must join on an id BOTH transports carry.** **2.267.0 CALIBRATION PASS (user report 高估; the reset logic was sound — the poison was upstream): ①BOUNCE-BACK TAINT — a u-decrease anomaly pair voids itself AND the next pair off that anchor (a stale sibling cache file promoted to freshest anchored a week-old reading; the recovery pair du+32%/$1.68 taught a ~30%-hot fable rate = the user-visible overestimate; real-data re-derive: fable implied-full $556→$724). ②markLimitBanner writes the dead bucket into EVERY identity-group cache file, basing the promoted write on the group's FRESHEST file and never bumping a sibling's fetchedAt (the flap's source: one-file marks promoted week-stale sibling buckets). ③predictCalib honesty guards (at-cap/one-sided-resetsAt/window-length span skipped — p100/a0 noise rows drowned the calib signal).** Consumers: maybePoolAutoSwitch overlays estimates onto readCache (hot pools pass exhaustPct 10 = est<10% 提前切; cold keep 5; candidate gate stays 5; **readCache reads through the IDENTITY GROUP's freshest cache file** — org-merged accounts keep TWO files and ⟳ truth lands in whichever was targeted, exhaustion-#2 root #2) + **EVENT-DRIVEN evaluation (2.266.0, user-designed after exhaustion #2: Fable 51%→100% in ~4min between polling ticks): every streamed usage record (main + sidechain) feeds `usageEstimator.noteLive` (in-memory ring, rid-deduped against the ledger's own rid set — never double-counts once the scan absorbs; noteLive busts the estimate memo) and `kickPoolEval` (5s throttle; per-pool gate 10s; anti-flap = MIN_GAIN margin, never cadence); workflow agents are file-only (no stdout, IN-PROCESS API calls — no process to wrap) so the launch ack "Run ID: wf_…" arms the workflow tailer (src/workflow-usage-tailer.js since 2.270.0): run-dir fs.watch + 5s belt poll tails agent-*.jsonl growth (per-file byte offsets, complete-lines-only) into noteLive within ~1-6s — the file-level equivalent of wrapping them; teardown 30min idle/session death; rid-dedup makes re-reads harmless. **THE ACK-BEATS-MKDIR RACE (2.270.0, user caught est 30-vs-65 live): the ack lands ~17ms BEFORE the harness creates the run dir — the original one-shot existsSync precondition lost EVERY run since 2.266.0 (zero armed events in a month of telemetry, proven against healthy sibling event names), so workflow bursts only reached the estimator via the 180s ledger scan. The tailer now RETRIES until the dir appears (2s cadence, 3min give-up); RULE: anything waiting on a path the harness creates asynchronously must retry, never probe once. Observability so it can't die silently again: `wf-usage-noted` metric per drain + the armed event fires on FIRST REAL DRAIN (an arm-time event proves nothing). Test: scripts/test-workflow-usage-tailer.mjs (9, incl. the race).**** + a 30s TIMER re-evaluates every auto pool (banner wordings: `reached|hit`, model-name ⇒ scoped even without 'weekly', task-notification blobs scanned) + /api/usage `estimates` field. UI (user-specified 虚线/浅色): popup bars = light diagonal hatch reading→est with dashed right edge + `est N%` stat; taskbar pies + Agents mini-donuts = light arc for the est delta + dashed outer ring; **ROLL DISPLAY RULE: est < reading ⇒ window reset ⇒ dark collapses to 0, whole arc light (est ≥ reading within one window, so est<reading is the clean discriminator — utils.estDisplayPair, shared client-wide)**. Zero API calls anywhere. Tests: scripts/test-usage-estimator.mjs (35) + dbg-est-shot.mjs (screenshot smoke incl. rolled case + pool row) **准确度度量=delta相对误差(2.368.13, owner纠正): 绝对|pred−act|随刷新频率变小——每分钟刷新任何模型都完美; calib行带u0/predDu/actDu/rel(|actΔ|<2pt时rel:null), 引擎指标usage-est-rel-err-pct只在窗口真动过时发; 诚实口径下14天同源≥2pt配对: 5h捕捉~85%/Fable周~78%/7d仅~61%真实移动——系统性低估待查(外部消费份额漂移/学习滞后)。test-usage-estimator(83)自此进CI gate(此前在gate外=silent-stale类)。**

### usage-walker.js

  usage-walker.js      — THE ledger walk, ONE module (2.297.0 twin-killer): UsageHistory.scan() consumes it IN-PROCESS (cursors injected, onEvent enrichment orchestrator-side), the daemon runs it as the usage-scan op child, and data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan mirrors it for checkout-less ssh hosts (parity-pinned). **usage-events PUSH (2.299.0, R4 complete): the daemon watches transcript dirs → incremental walk → chan-0 batches → server acks after durable ingest → ONLY THEN the device commits its cursor (two-phase, roles inverted; unacked batches re-emit, rid dedup absorbs). Remote ledger = seconds-fresh; covers external sessions + workflow file-only usage the stdout relay never sees; 60s-floor dirty-kick harvest stays as the fallback rung. E2E scripts/test-usage-events-push.mjs (7) incl. the unacked-batch-does-not-advance-cursor proof.** Event fields: {rid, mid, ts, sid, be, model, cwd, i, cw5, cw1, cr, o, tier} + codex-only `effort` (v3, 2026-09-06). Codex block v3: `mid` = the preceding `token_usage_record.response_id` (0.153; cursor-parked `pendMid`/`pendTotal`, total-checked, consumed by the next token_count), `effort` from turn_context; the `cx:<sid>:<cumulative>` rid is the ONE key the codex normalizer must derive identically (its meta.requestId): sid = the rollout FILE's uuid (never a session_meta inside it — a sub-agent rollout also carries the PARENT's at line 1), and an identical re-emitted token_count is dropped on `rid === cur.lastRid` — the normalizer mirrors both (round 3: per-RECORD file provenance `__threadId` tagged by the merged reader, the reader's id only a default for provenance-less records, wrapper_meta re-points it; `_lastRidKeys` per thread = this cursor per file). test-usage-walk-parity + test-codex-history assert the equality on the same rollout, and test-codex-history's corpus smoke asserts minted-key-set equality against this walker over ≤30 real local rollouts (skips with a reason on CI).

### conversation-index.js

  conversation-index.js — Conversation-location index (2.297.0, R3 tail): data/conversation-index.json records which machine owns each conversation from BOTH discovery listings and transcript fetches — resume host-inference locates conversations the raw remote-jsonl cache never saw; two live claims = honestly ambiguous (null), a week-fresher claim is decisive, removed-host claims ignored. hosts.conversationOwner(sid); ws-handler inference consults it FIRST and forces ambiguity on disagreement with the cache scan. Test: scripts/test-conversation-index.mjs (7).

## src/server/wrapper-files.js (2.339.2 resolver + 2.364.1 capability reader)

Two files belong to a local chat session and they have DIFFERENT writers: `data/session-meta/<sock>.json` is the SERVER's record (webuiSessionId, claudeSessionId, account, task…; written by writeSessionMeta) and `data/session-buffers/<id>.json` is the wrapper's SIDECAR (pid, startedAt, mode, streaming, tasks, todos, goal, `caps` — written by data/bin/chat-wrapper.js itself). `resolveWrapperFiles(BUFFERS_DIR, id, sockPath)` (2.339.2) finds the sidecar even for counter-collision sessions whose wrapper argv carries a different id (reads the dtach master's argv from /proc). `wrapperCaps(BUFFERS_DIR, id, sockPath)` (2.364.1) is the ONE reader of the wrapper's advertised capabilities: `{frameFile, peerMessage, inputQueue, caps, reason:'ok'|'no-caps'|'no-sidecar', startedAt, pid}`, stateless (`inputQueue` since 2026-09-06: the wrapper publishes `queue_changed` and serves the `queue-op` verb — the ws `queue-op` case and the client's `queueSupported` both hang off it, because backend-caps only says what a KIND of agent can do). The 2.361.1 gate read caps from the session-meta file instead — which never has them — so every wrapper tested old and the frame-file bypass was dead for two releases (kb-bugfix-invariants: CAPABILITY GATE READ THE WRONG FILE). Rules: new wrapper capabilities are added to the `caps` object in chat-wrapper.js AND read through wrapperCaps only; callers cache only positive verdicts; test-chat-frame-guard §2 spawns the real wrapper and asserts wrapperCaps sees its sidecar (parity). **Both wrappers speak the same contract (design-harness-plugins §1 P1)**: data/bin/codex-chat-wrapper.js writes `caps: { peerMessage, frameFile }` into the same sidecar shape and resolves `_frame_file` pointers on its stdin path (read → unlink always → must be ONE JSON object, nested pointers refused → dispatched through `handleInput` exactly as a stdin line; every failure logs AND emits a `task_failed` event, as does an unparseable raw stdin line — the old `if (!msg) continue;` was a silent message loss; and it acks every received stdin line with `_stdin_ack` on stdout like chat-wrapper, so ws-handler's 5s broken-pty re-send never replays a pointer whose file is already consumed). The chat-input bypass in ws-handler is therefore TRANSPORT + CAPABILITY only (`>64KB && !host && socketPath` → wrapperCaps decides pointer / raw ≤1MB / evidenced refusal) — it must never regain a backend-id branch; resolveWrapperFiles' /proc fallback already matches `codex-chat-wrapper.js` (substring of the argv marker). test-chat-frame-guard §2b drives the REAL codex wrapper against a stub app-server (pointer → intact turn/start input; shredded/missing/nested files + unparseable lines → loud) and §4 pins the gate shape.

## src/server/auto-resume.js (2.368.0 — continue after a usage limit resets) 2.369.32: the continue prompt carries originKind 'auto-resume' (server.js sendToSession tags the user record: claude `originKind`, codex `payload.webui_origin`) → both normalizers set msg.originKind='auto-resume' → chat-renderers shows a labelled VibeSpace card, never an anonymous user bubble. The four "who said continue" lanes: auto-resume continue (server, user-role, labelled) / Stop-hook block reason (the CLI renders it as a user turn: "Stop hook feedback: …") / UserPromptSubmit additionalContext (<system-reminder>, VibeSpace's prompt-context) / "The user hasn't heard from you in a while" (Claude Code's own idle nudge — not VibeSpace).

The CLI ships this feature but only in the interactive REPL: `/rate-limit-options` is **not** in a stream-json session's `slash_commands` (verified against a real init record) and the timer is a TUI `useInterval`, so a VibeSpace chat session hits the limit and stops dead. This module is ours, with the same shape and one deliberate difference: **the wait is persisted and survives a restart** — the CLI's own says "Automatic continue cancelled · Claude Code relaunched during the wait", and a multi-hour wait that a deploy cancels silently is worse than no feature.

**Order of preference (do not invert):** exhaustion first tries `maybePoolAutoSwitch` (another account = seconds, not hours); only then does `armIfEnabled` run. The module disarms itself on any proof of recovery — a fresh non-rejected `rate_limit_event`, the user's own prompt (ws chat-input), a dead session — because firing on a recovered session is a billed turn for nothing. **Gate is tri-state**: `session._autoResume` (spawn value or the live ws toggle) wins over the `claude.autoResumeOnLimit` default, so a per-session OFF survives the default being ON; default is OFF because firing spends quota unattended. Fires at `resetsAt + 15s`, never early, never twice, never while `_isStreaming`, refuses waits >26h out or with no reset time, retries on delivery failure, and announces both arming and firing in the conversation. The continue wording is the CLI's own, verbatim. Gated by scripts/test-auto-resume.mjs (48). **2.368.27 nearest-reset arming (c1206711 incident 2026-08-26)**: the rejection may carry a FAR bucket's resetsAt (seven-day ~100h, org overage off) while a nearer reset that will actually free the account (five-hour, rolling-window recovery) sits in the usage cache — the old flow refused the far wait journal-only and left NOTHING watching. Now `pickArmReset` (PURE, exported) picks the nearest in-range future reset from event + cache buckets (fiveHour/sevenDay/opus/scoped); the engine's `armBestReset` is the ONLY way exhaustion arms (all three sites: claude dead-branch + both codex — wiring pinned, 2.355.0 lesson); a still-dead account simply re-runs the ladder on its next rejection (self-limiting at bucket cadence). A too-far refusal now NOTIFIES the session (1/h floor) — a refusal the user can't see is the incident. Suite now 71. **2.368.28 (the owner's correction — the real c1206711 story)**: the pool had hot-moved the session onto a member whose 7d then died; the 5h-exhausted sibling (7d fine) was the way back, and the pool even switched back on its own — but a hot re-point never moves an idle blocked session. ① armBestReset merges POOL SIBLING members' bucket resets (poolReadCache × poolMembers — the freeing reset lives in the sibling's cache); ② HOT switches (per-session + pool-level) `fireNow()` ARMED sessions — continue delivered the moment a healthy account lands, armed-only, cold keeps client restarts; ③ the timed fire runs `beforeFire` (maybePoolAutoSwitch) before spending. Suite 79. **2.368.32 (owner caught the premature fire — "7d重置没和5h对齐"): the wait target is the MAX over the identity's DEAD buckets** (a session unblocks only when ALL dead buckets reset; a healthy bucket's nearer reset is no candidate), min across identities (self + pool siblings) unchanged; dead = utilization≥0.999 / status limited|rejected / usedPercent≥99.5 with a future resetsAt. The limit-banner path now (re-)arms too — it used to mark the cache dead and leave nothing waiting. Suite 81. **2.368.33 false-arm suppression (owner: '没被打断任何对话'): a dead event races the hot switch that just fixed it — armBestReset skips when the CURRENT pool member has no dead bucket (shared isDeadBucket; unknown never guessed), and ANY main-thread assistant record disarms (`noteSessionProduced`, engine→session-stdout) — the readings-based disarm misses accounts that emit no events. Suite 86.** **2.368.34 — that pre-check ATE A REAL WALL (9h dark) and is REMOVED: a live CLI holds its OLD token, the org-verified banner marks the OLD member dead while the link points at a healthy one — the link is never the org a running CLI is on. Replacements that cannot eat a wall: arm unconditionally; announcement DELAYED 90s (disarm inside the window ⇒ silent); noteWorked age-gates the produced-work disarm to >30s post-arm (the banner's own trailing records land within seconds). parseBannerResetMs: the banner's "resets 12:40pm (TZ)" is timezone-parsed into the precise arm target (was a now+5h guess). Suite 91.**

**2.369.0 — THE WALL MACHINE (owner-designed, docs/design-wall-machine.md; supersedes every arm/disarm mechanism above where they conflict).** auto-resume.js is now the EXECUTOR only (persisted armed waits, tick fire, delayed announcement, veto-capable beforeFire); detection + recovery live in the engine's turn-granular machine: wall signals (rejected events / banner as a BOOLEAN — text is never a data source; parseBannerResetMs deleted) accumulate on the current turn via `noteWallSignal`, the `result` record (codex: task_complete/task_failed) classifies via `noteTurnEnd` — walled (signals with ≤1 assistant record after the last one) → BLOCKED entry: pool eval, then `quotaVerdictFor` (the account system's ONE usability answer — account-pool-auto.quotaVerdict pure: 5h<10%/weekly<5% THRESH hot tier, blockedUntil=max over dead buckets, pool=any-usable/min-over-members, model-projected + estimator-overlaid) → usable ⇒ arm now+45s (announce delay 90s keeps a quick success silent), blocked ⇒ arm blockedUntil, unknown ⇒ PROBE auto-cli /usage 0→30m→1h→2h then loud give-up. **2.369.6: blocked-with-target ALSO fires one throttled verification probe (10min/scope, _wallVerifyAt) — a confidently-lying cache armed a 2.6-day wait on an alive account (inc-mtdsoj5f); confident ≠ correct, every BLOCKED entry verifies.** A normally-completed turn unconditionally disarms (sufficient proof — kills noteWorked/30s-age; the pool-rescued mid-turn case classifies NORMAL by construction). Pre-fire: probe + re-verdict, false VETOES the spend and re-arms. Blocking identity = orgVerifiedKey (OTel-observed org over the link — the .33/.34 root cause). Session fields _turnWallSigs/_turnWorkAfterSig/_wallProbeTimer/_arFiring registered. Gate: test-auto-resume §8-10 (80). **B-2c9b (2026-09-05): BLOCKED entry is now demote → session-aware verdict → arm → pool eval (`finally`)** — the walled branch of `noteTurnEnd` no longer runs `maybePoolAutoSwitch` BEFORE `onWalledTurn`, so the wall's account is demoted (ground truth) before the verdict reads the cache and the hot switch's `fireNow()` finds the session ARMED and continues it in the same tick; `quotaVerdictFor` takes `{model, session}` and judges the member the session is OBSERVED on first. Full essay under account-pool-auto.js ("WALL = GROUND TRUTH"); gate test-auto-resume §11.

**Output style (same release, same suite).** The CLI's Concise/Explanatory/Learning/Proactive are a SETTINGS key with no flag, and stream-json never gets `/output-style` — so it is spawn-only: `buildSessionArgs({outputStyle})` merges it into the ONE `--settings` flag (alongside `ultracode`/`switchModelsOnFlag`; never a second flag), ws-create defaults it from `claude.outputStyle`, the status-bar chip persists it as per-session config, and every surface says "applies on the next resume" instead of pretending otherwise.

## src/server/instance-url.js (2.367.0 — this instance's own public address)

**One resolver.** "The URL a machine outside this box uses to reach this VibeSpace" is needed by reverse mounts (machine-mounts), remote agent installs (mounts-plugins-wiring), the agentd auto-graduation gate and published-page share links; all four read `agentd.publicUrl` inline before this. `url()` is the single reader and resolves **explicit frp mapping → `agentd.publicUrl` → `VIBESPACE_PUBLIC_URL` → null** (null = callers fall back to the asking browser's origin, 2.366.1). The mapping is a LAYER: publishing never writes the setting, so unmapping lands exactly back on it — the owner's requirement ("不要覆盖原来的设置, incase取消了映射直接回归原来的设置").

**One publisher.** `plugins.frpPublish('vibespace-instance', PORT, …)` predates this module but ran as an invisible side effect of installing a remote agent over the relay — two callers writing one relay proxy name, with nothing recording the URL. This module owns that proxy; the install path calls `ensurePublished()`. **Explicit vs auto is load-bearing**: a user pressing Map sets `desired` and outranks the setting; an install-driven publish is `auto` — recorded and shown in the Ports panel, but never repointing share links (a side effect must not move everyone's URLs). `publish()` is REFUSED while auth is off (an open instance on the internet is RCE as the owner). `restore()` re-asserts a desired mapping at boot without clearing the persisted URL (frpc is its own process and usually outlives a restart); a failed re-assert keeps the intent and surfaces the error. UI: `_renderInstanceUrlRow` in sidebar-rail.js (Ports panel), which also states what is kept underneath. Gated by scripts/test-instance-url.mjs (39).

## src/server/design-kit.js + data/bin/vibespace-page (2.366.0 — design canvases from chat, published HERE) **2.369.14 zstd阶梯**: CLI≥2.1.257把skill/helper/payload打成bun内zstd帧(SKILL-<hash>.md.zst / seed-canvas.mjs-<hash>.txt.zst / payload), 模板字面量锚点消失; extractFromZstd扫全部zstd magic候选按头部分类(frontmatter name: design / '// Design-canvas seeding helper' / doctype+appifact-doc), 220MB仅176ms, 旧CLI仍走模板字面量路径, Node<22.15无zlib zstd时明说.

**Problem.** Claude Code's bundled `/design` skill (design canvas, early preview) is only reachable from the TUI: the init `slash_commands` list carries ONE `design` (plus `design-consent`/`-revoke`/`-sync`); the terminal picker pairs the skill with the consent command, stream-json has no picker and resolves to consent, and the Skill tool refuses `design` ("built-in CLI command, not a skill"). The skill = `seed-canvas.mjs` (helper) + `payload.template.html` (~2 MiB editor) + ~55 KB instructions; the CLI extracts the first two to `/tmp/claude-<uid>/bundled-skills/<ver>/<hash>/design/` when the slash command runs, and injects the instructions as a user message (they live nowhere on disk).

**design-kit.js** (`create({dataDir, claudeCmd, log})`): `ensure()` builds `data/design-kit/<cliVersion>/` once per installed CLI version — helper + payload from the CLI-extracted dir when present (the CLI's own bytes; parity-tested) else extracted from the binary (`payload.template.html.asset` name → the one occurrence followed by `<!doctype html`, up to the first NUL; the helper and the skill text are backtick template literals with `\uXXXX`/`\xHH` escapes → `unescapeTemplate`; all scans stream in 8 MB chunks — pods are small), `SKILL.orig.md` (verbatim) and `SKILL.md` = `adaptSkill()`: step 4 (Artifact publish) and "Updating an existing canvas" (artifact read-back) replaced by the VibeSpace publish step (`vibespace-page publish`), banner after the frontmatter; anchors missing ⇒ `{error}` and the kit is NOT ready — never a half-adapted document. Every build is VALIDATED by seeding a sample artboard with the kit's own helper and running its `--check` (`ok:`). `status()` feeds `GET /api/design-kit/status` (cookie; `?refresh=1` rebuilds) — shown in the chat status bar's design popover, so "not ready" always carries its reason (no CLI binary / layout changed in a new CLI / check failed). Nothing of Anthropic's is vendored in the repo: extraction is per-user, per-version, at runtime; only the adaptation text is ours. Warmed 15 s after boot, off the critical path.

**The flow.** Chat status bar design chip (`chat-status-bar.js` `_renderDesignPopover`; SVG `UI_ICONS.design`; view-only windows have no chip) → brief + "public link" → `chat-view._sendDesignRequest` sends a VISIBLE user message (`[VibeSpace design request] …` — no hidden injection; the transcript shows what the agent was asked): `vibespace-page kit` → read `<dir>/SKILL.md` → `vibespace-page publish <file> --title "…" [--public]` → reply with the link. `vibespace-page` (STATIC, in AGENT_TOOLS): `publish` uploads the file body to `POST /api/agent/pages/publish` (vsst_ or jbt_; raw ≤25 MB; `?title&path&host&public`), `kit` asks `GET /api/agent/design-kit` and, on the hub machine, prints the server's kit dir, else mirrors `seed-canvas.mjs`/`payload.template.html`/`SKILL.md` from `GET /api/agent/design-kit/file/:name` into `~/.vibespace/design-kit/<ver>/` (remote hosts need no CLI extraction), `list` shows this session's pages. Publishing attributes the page to the session; `onPublished` → `page-published` broadcast → the chip count/list update live. Hosted canvases are view + PNG/PDF export (CSP sandbox, no online Save) — SKILL.md tells the agent to say so at handover. Invariants: the kit is per CLI version and validated before use; adaptation is all-or-nothing; the request is visible, never injected; remote hosts mirror the hub's kit (one kit, one adaptation); the CLI never receives tokens on argv.

## src/server/published-pages.js (2.364.0 — Published pages, "接管artifact"; 2.366.0 content publish)

Instance-hosted shareable HTML. Owner incident: the /design canvas skill publishes to claude.ai artifacts, which could not be shared outward; the canvas is a self-contained local HTML file, so the instance hosts it itself. Store data/published-pages.json + snapshots data/published-pages/<id>.html (publish COPIES; re-publish of the same srcPath UPSERTS the same id — stable share URL across design iterations). Serving: GET /p/:id, middleware-exempt in auth.js with the gate INSIDE the module (private ⇒ requestAuthed cookie; per-page `public` flag = anyone with the link) — the /svc doctrine verbatim. **CSP `sandbox allow-scripts …` on every /p response is LOAD-BEARING**: pages are arbitrary user HTML with scripts served from the APP ORIGIN; the sandbox directive gives them an opaque origin (no cookies, no same-origin /api) — removing it turns the feature into stored XSS against the owner. Never add `allow-same-origin`. UI = the explorer right-click dialog (create+manage in one; Open uses a browser window). Gate: scripts/test-published-pages.mjs (60, real HTTP + CSP assertions + wiring pins).

## src/msg-acl.js + src/server/conversation-deliver.js + data/bin/vibespace-msg (2.362.0 — Communication Channels v1)

**What.** Agent-to-agent messaging with Task-Group-scoped reach, built as the first channel of the owner's Communication Channels vision (external sources — Gmail/Lark/Slack — later feed the same ladder with their own `source` tags; every stash envelope already carries one).

**ACL (msg-acl.js, PURE).** Same group = mutual see+message (the default collaboration boundary). Outside: group `externalVisibility` (`none` default / `visible` / `messageable`, task-groups field + task-detail select) and a per-session `_msgReachability` override (schema-registered, meta-persisted, `POST /api/sessions/:id/msg-reachability`, widening ONLY). Levels rank none<visible<messageable; effective = max of grants; multi-group targets = union; ungrouped sessions are closed singletons. HONESTY: this is a coordination/attention boundary, NOT security — same-OS-user agents can always read transcripts and raw sockets; the native ListAgents/SendMessage tools still see the whole machine. Uniform not-found errors (no existence oracle).

**2.366.1 (published-pages.js) — the hosting layer.** `/p/<id>` is a SHELL (our own HTML on the real origin, no user content) that frames `/p/<id>/raw`, where the published HTML runs under the sandbox CSP (opaque origin; **never** `allow-same-origin`). Both routes share one `gate()`; the shell escapes the agent-chosen name. `/raw` injects a COMPAT PRELUDE (`COMPAT_PRELUDE`/`injectShim`, cached per snapshot): `crypto.randomUUID` polyfilled from `getRandomValues` — it is SECURE-CONTEXT-ONLY and VibeSpace is normally plain http on a hostname, which hung every canvas artboard forever (kb-bugfix-invariants: HOSTED PAGE NEEDED A SECURE CONTEXT) — plus in-memory `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` for the opaque origin; each only when genuinely missing.
