Share URLs: `urlFor(id, req)` = `agentd.publicUrl` → the ASKING request's Host → relative `/p/<id>`; nothing is remembered (the server cannot know about proxies/tunnels), agents get the path, and chat-renderers linkifies it against `location.origin`. **2.369.7 geo/storage bridge (B-74de, owner-directed via 生活方式助手's verified spec)**: the shell ships a per-request-nonce'd postMessage bridge (`script-src 'nonce-…'` added to the SHELL CSP only) forwarding a geolocation watch stream + `vp_<pageId>_`-namespaced localStorage into the sandboxed iframe. Protocol FROZEN (consumer pages ship it): `vibeBridge:'ready'{geo,store}` on iframe load / `vibeGeo:'start'|'stop'` → `'pos'{lat,lng,accuracy,heading,speed,ts}|'err'` / `vibeStore:'set'|'del'{k,v}`. Source verified (`e.source===iframe.contentWindow`), pages cannot read each other's keys, NO user content enters the shell (interpolations = ID_RE id + own nonce), and the red line stands verbatim — iframe never gains allow-same-origin, raw CSP untouched. Accepted trade-off: any published page may surface a geolocation PROMPT on this origin (browser still asks the user; per-page gating is the escalation path if that ever bites). Pins: test-published-pages (bridge presence, source-check+namespace, per-request nonce). **2.369.11 offline + blobs**: /p/sw.js service worker (location-capped scope /p/ — structurally cannot touch the app; network-first + 4s cache fallback, NOT cache-first — an online logged-out browser must never read a private page from cache) + vibeBlob protocol (IndexedDB vibespace-pages/blobs, `<pageId>:` keys, on-demand get — 10-30MB tile caches; vibeStore/localStorage stays for small values). Shell shows a 6s 'offline ready' badge when a cached copy exists.

**2.366.0 additions (published-pages.js).** `publishContent({html, name, srcKey, makePublic, sessionId, conversationId})` = the upload path (agent CLI / remote hosts; body in the request); upsert identity is `srcKey` (`local:<abs>` for file publishes, `<host>:<abs>` for uploads — legacy records get `local:`+srcPath on load); records carry `sessionId`/`conversationId` (`list({sessionId})`, `GET /api/pages?sessionId=`); `onPublished(page, {replaced})` is the ONE notify point (server.js broadcasts `page-published` to the session's clients) for dialog AND agent publishes; `noteBrowserOrigin(req)` (every management call) teaches the server the browser's origin (`x-forwarded-*` first) so `url` is absolute when `agentd.publicUrl` is unset — relative `/p/<id>` stays the honest fallback and the client joins it with `location.origin`.

**Delivery (conversation-deliver.js).** ONE ladder for jobs notifications AND agent messages (jobs-wiring's inline twin deleted — the 2.361.5 lesson: delivery topology must have one home): ① channel socket (experimental) ② local CLI inbox (peer-messaging findPeer/postToPeer — peer-messaging is now a SHARED-tier module, the daemon bundles it) ③ the OWNING machine's daemon via the `peer-post` agentd op (capability-gated three-touch; conversation-index.ownerHost names the machine — raw conversation uuids are the ONLY address, agents never learn topology) ④ durable stash `data/msg-stash.json` (cap 30/conversation, debounced atomic write) drained at the conversation's next context injection — machine-agnostic because remote sessions' hooks call back to the hub anyway. **2.368.26 rung ①.5 — registry lane `rpc-queue`** (between ② and ③, local only): when `capsOf(backend).peerDelivery === 'rpc-queue'` (codex; NEVER a backend-id branch — src/backend-caps.js is the registry) AND the wrapper's OWN sidecar adverts `caps.peerMessage` (2.361.1 law: gate on what the process wrote; negative verdicts never cached — stateless read per attempt), deliver writes a `peer-message` stdin frame to the live wrapper. The wrapper owns the app-server RPC connection: idle ⇒ `turn/start` (billed turn + reply, claude-inbox parity), busy ⇒ `thread/queue/add` (runs right after the current turn — upstream-test-pinned, codex 0.149 source). The frame carries `fromName`+`cardText` (P1, design-harness-plugins §1); the wrapper records the peer user message itself WITH a `webui_peer:{name,body}` marker (item notifications never carry `userMessage` ⇒ no double render; the codex normalizer renders that record as the LABELLED peer card live and on rebuild — marker-blind twin dedup, see codex-message-manager.js) so this rung does NOT emitPeerCard (an in-memory card on top would double-render live, and a stdin write is not yet a delivery); it reports `peer_message_result` both ways (ok:false echoes text+fromName) and ok:false makes session-stdout re-stash the echoed text with its label — a promised message is never lost. Old wrapper / claude session / no sidecar = honest miss falling to later rungs. Gate: scripts/test-peer-delivery.mjs (19 — functional real create() + sidecar + negative controls + the 2.355.0-class WIRING pins, incl. the recordCodexQuotaSignal dead-wiring fix this work uncovered).

**Routes/tool.** `/api/agent/msg/peers` + `/api/agent/msg/send` (vsst_; integration master switch gates; 30s per-pair floor + 10min dup dedupe + 16KB cap — a delivered message opens a BILLED turn on an idle receiver, the intro/manual/CLI all teach this). CLI `vibespace-msg list/send`; manual `docs/agent/msg-manual.md` (`vibespace-docs msg`). Incoming messages render via the existing peer-message card (origin.name → 2.361.6).

**Delivery-site card rendering (2.363.0).** Server-posted injections reach the CLI as an UNREGISTERED poster → the CLI records origin {kind:'peer',from:'unknown'} with no name/msg_id/body, so neither the JSONL rebuild nor result-mining can attribute or even display them. The ladder therefore renders the receiving chat window's peer card ITSELF on every ok lane (`emitPeerCard` dep from server.js → session normalizer `injectPeerCard`; opts.fromName/opts.cardText on deliverToConversation carry the label + raw body). Stash drains (agent-msg ×2 + job-notifications ×2 sites in agent-routes) emit the same card at injection time. injectPeerCard is deliberately containment-free — the delivery site posts once per fire, and same-body repeats are legitimate (the 2.362.2 review lesson).

**Invariants.** Delivery topology stays in ONE module — any new notification-shaped feature consumes conversation-deliver, never scans registries itself; ACL changes stay in the PURE module with matrix tests (scripts/test-agent-msg.mjs, 41); the per-session override only WIDENS; stash envelopes always carry `source`; message VISIBILITY is owned by the party that has the information — when the transport strips attribution, the POSTER renders the card.

## src/otel-truth.js + src/server/otel-ingest.js (2.361.0, B-345b 终案 — the per-request billing-TRUTH stack)

**Why it exists.** Pool hot-switches do NOT take effect inside a RUNNING claude process — repointPoolSymlink's utimes trick invalidates the CLI's mtime-gated credential cache only for NEW processes / token expiry; a live CLI kept billing the OLD org ≥25 minutes after being switched away (forensic case: 79928a2b, 6 auto-switches in 35min, old org climbed 35%→95% and rate-limited FROM that session while attribution said three other orgs; 558 mid-session switches / $7.9k post-switch spend in 12 days). Link-intent attribution therefore mis-books storm spend in both directions, poisoning per-org odometers AND rate learning (the ≥10pt burst calibration residual of -30~-49% — six alternative mechanisms tested and REFUTED: walker gaps ($0 diff vs raw transcripts), reading lag (storm-level residual doesn't cancel), training censoring (73 big pairs in-set), output share, family mix, cw5/cw1 mix, per-request overhead, unresolved-host exclusion).

**The channel.** Transcript records, statusline payloads and rate_limit_event all carry quota VALUES but never identity (verified field inventory, CLI 2.1.235). The CLI's built-in OpenTelemetry export is the ONLY per-request source that NAMES the org: every `claude_code.api_request` log record carries organization.id + user.account_uuid/email + request_id (the ledger's rid) + model + all four token classes + cost_usd; prompt content is redacted upstream. Base endpoint + `/v1/logs` path semantics verified empirically.

**Data flow.** ws-create's r6Env IIFE injects OTEL_* env into LOCAL claude spawns (gate `!session.host && backend==='claude' && otelEnv()`; a user-set OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT in the session spec wins; process-env channel — the PERSISTED token (data/usage-history/otel-token, 0600; per-boot would silently 403 sessions surviving a restart) rides OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_HEADERS, never argv). The CLI POSTs to `/otel/v1/logs` on THIS instance (cookie middleware exempts `/otel/`; the module's loopback-remoteAddress + token gate is the ONLY door — /svc precedent). otel-ingest then: ① `truthLookup(rid)` → consumed by `UsageHistory.scan` at BAKE time (`setTruthLookup`; undefined = fall back to the attribution walk, null = machine global login); ② corrective `recordAttribution({sid, trueAcct, pool-preserved, ts})` when observed ≠ current walk (dedup per sid on last truth acct; org flips re-write; agreeing truth just arms the marker); ③ append-only stash `data/usage-history/otel-truth.ndjson` (acctKnown honesty for unknown orgs, boot-replayed into the rid map — bake overrides survive restarts, 30d/12MB trim).

**Invariants (do not regress).** Zero vendor calls — the CLI pushes to us (§ban-safety compatible by construction; test-vendor-whitelist unchanged). Unknown orgs are COUNTED and logged once, never silently dropped (api_retry lesson) — an org resolves via usage-cache orgUuid (identityGroups 'org:<uuid>') else roster email; teach a missing org by clicking ⟳ once. envFor() returns null when `usage.otelTruth` is off (kill switch, default ON). OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER stays 'none' (logs carry the events; metrics/traces routes 200 tolerantly). Gated by scripts/test-otel-truth.mjs (28: sanitized REAL-payload fixtures, real-HTTP ingest, REAL bake-path scan, wiring pins) + a chat-E2E assert that proves env→receiver→parser on every push's live haiku turn. Known residue: events scanned BEFORE their truth arrived keep the link-intent acct (scan races OTel by ≤15s throttle vs 5s flush — rare; a truth-aware shard repair mirroring the existing rebake precedent is the follow-up if soak metrics show material counts — watch otel-truth-correction / otel-truth-req in Diagnostics).

### usage-history.js

  usage-history.js     — UsageHistory (2.61.0, codex 2.63.0): PERMANENT per-request token LEDGER for the Usage window. Mines Claude's OWN JSONL transcripts (terminal AND chat — mode-independent; **2.265.0: ALSO `<sid>/subagents/**` agent + workflow transcripts — workflow agents' usage exists ONLY there (~$205/run measured invisible), events attribute to the PARENT sid, journal files excluded, rid-dedup absorbs parent-sidechain overlap; the top-level-only scan under-reported every workflow run AND poisoned dead-reckoning rates**) AND Codex rollouts (~/.codex/sessions/**/rollout-*-<threadId>.jsonl: `event_msg`/`token_count` → `info.last_token_usage` = one request {input⊇cached, output⊇reasoning}; info:null = rate-limit heartbeat, skip; NO requestId → synthetic rid = `cx:<sid>:<cumulative total_tokens>` (strictly monotonic per thread — replays dedup); model/cwd from the preceding `turn_context` record PERSISTED IN THE CURSOR since incremental scans start mid-file; cw5/cw1 always 0 — rollouts don't report cache-write counts) into append-only monthly shards data/usage-history/events-YYYY-MM.ndjson; incremental per-file {offset,lastRid[,model,cwd]} byte cursor (offset is BYTES not string length — CJK bug, do not regress), reads CHUNKED via _scanFileLines (32MB slabs, complete-lines-only — a 1.9GB rollout exceeds Node's max string; first 2.3GB pass ≈8s, then incremental). **PERF (2.70.0):** /api/usage-stats used to re-read+re-parse EVERY shard per request — seconds of CPU at 218k events even on fast storage, and observed at 18s while a concurrent full-disk scan saturated IO (the workspace is a FUSE NFS mount; its IDLE baseline is fast — the '40ms/read' measured that day was contention, per the user's correction). Now: in-memory event cache (`_loadEvents` — shards are append-only, so after one full load each call reads only appended bytes, last-newline-aligned BYTE offsets; rid-dedup at load), scan() throttled 15s (force flag for the 3-min timer path), `_metaMap` TTL-cached 60s (66 tiny files = 2.7s over NFS), `_tier` memoized per model, `warm()` at boot (+8s). 18s→0.3s hot. DEDUP BY requestId TWICE (scan-time contiguous + read-time Set in _events — one request = 2-3 identical/sometimes-non-adjacent records). Account attribution BAKED per event (acct+atype: subscription/api/codex-subscription/global) PER-REQUEST-BY-TIME via attribution.ndjson (**2.84.0 ATTRIBUTION FIX, real data bug**: requests predating a session's FIRST attribution entry are GLOBAL (10-min grace for spawn skew) — the old `list[0].acct` fallback billed a week of pre-registration history to a newly added subscription during the initial 2.61.0 backfill (19.7k of Fish Max's 20.9k events were pre-registration); one-time `_maybeRebakeAttribution` (.attrib-rebake-v1 marker) rewrote 20,304 baked events) (appended from writeSessionMeta→recordUsageAttribution; sid = claudeSessionId||backendSessionId so codex threads attribute too) so sub $ and API $ NEVER mix. aggregate({from,to,backend,accounts}) → totals + series + groups(day/model/account/billing/project/mode/hour/weekday/session) (seq dims day/hour/weekday sort by KEY, not cost — cost-sorting scrambled the dashboard's hour axis, 2.180.2; the dashboard also self-sorts + gap-fills the closed hour/weekday scales + names weekdays); accounts = Set of bucket keys (account ids / '__global__' claude-global / '__global_codex__' codex-global — the two CLIs' machine logins are SEPARATE buckets since 2.64.0, billing keys 'cli-global-login'/'codex-cli-login') for the window's account filter. Pricing v2 pricing.json {tiers,accounts:{id:{discount}|{tiers}}} — account-aware _cost; _tier is DATA-DRIVEN (longest tiers-key substring match — new model = new pricing.json key, no code); official rates verified 2026-07-09 (Fable $10/$50, Opus 4.5-4.8 $5/$25, GPT-5.6 Sol $5/$30 Terra $2.5/$15 Luna $1/$6, GPT-5.5 $5/$30; missing default tiers back-fill into an existing pricing.json without clobbering edits). See [[project_usage_ledger]] 2.369.36 (inc-mtox23xw): `_events(from,to)` walks a lazily sorted view with binary search (O(log n + k)); `_evCountUpTo(t)` is the memo version for interval costs; `_loadEvents` re-checks shards at most once/s (scan()'s own appends reset the throttle). The estimator called `_events` once per anchor PAIR — with the permanent ledger that was a full scan + readdir/stat per pair and blocked the loop 10-59s (caught by Debugger.pause; the CPU profiler mislabelled it idle). **Codex join fields (2026-09-06):** scan() and ingestRemoteEvents pass the walker's `mid` (codex = the 0.153 `resp_…` response id) and new `effort` through unchanged (undefined for claude ⇒ not serialized); `eventForRid` matches the `cx:` key exactly / `h:<host>:cx:…` by suffix, `eventForMid` matches `ev.mid` — the rid-info route's two lookups resolve codex events without any new index; the `mids` cache set now also holds `resp_…` ids (harmless to the live-odometer exclusion join, which keys codex live entries on the same cx rid). Pricing v2 pricing.json {tiers,accounts:{id:{discount}|{tiers}}} — account-aware _cost; `pricingToken()` (2.369.43) is the table's memo VERSION for anything that caches computed costs (the anchors' interval memo is the one consumer) — recomputed only when setPricing REPLACES `_pricing`; _tier is DATA-DRIVEN (longest tiers-key substring match — new model = new pricing.json key, no code); official rates verified 2026-07-09 (Fable $10/$50, Opus 4.5-4.8 $5/$25, GPT-5.6 Sol $5/$30 Terra $2.5/$15 Luna $1/$6, GPT-5.5 $5/$30; missing default tiers back-fill into an existing pricing.json without clobbering edits). See [[project_usage_ledger]] 2.369.36 (inc-mtox23xw): `_events(from,to)` walks a lazily sorted view with binary search (O(log n + k)); `_evCountUpTo(t)` is the memo version for interval costs; `_loadEvents` re-checks shards at most once/s (scan()'s own appends reset the throttle). The estimator called `_events` once per anchor PAIR — with the permanent ledger that was a full scan + readdir/stat per pair and blocked the loop 10-59s (caught by Debugger.pause; the CPU profiler mislabelled it idle).

### user-todos.js

  user-todos.js        — UserTodoManager (2.65.0, the GLOBAL user-facing TODO list / "For you" inbox): discrete items an AGENT filed that need the USER (decision/input/review) — the inverse of the agent's own TodoWrite. One item = one owning session (sessionKey like session-status, webui:→backend rekey in the agent route); data/user-todos.json (session-status persistence pattern: debounced 500ms + content-compared + exit flush); add() idempotent BY TEXT across ALL statuses (open → refresh; resolved/dismissed → REOPEN the SAME id — review finding: fresh-id minting let an add→resolve loop or a dismissed-text re-file toast-spam every client despite the open cap); caps: 20 open/session (add throws with guidance), 1000 total (oldest RESOLVED pruned, open never); resolveByAgent by id or unique text substring (own session only); snapshot() = {open: urgency-then-newest sorted, resolved: last 15}; 'user-todos-updated' broadcast on every change. Client = src/lib/user-todos-panel.js. KNOWN LIMIT: webui:→backend rekey happens on the agent's NEXT call only (same as session-status) — in practice unreachable since vibespace-ask runs mid-turn when the backend id already exists. Client review fixes (do not regress): jump falls back to attachSession for a live session with NO open window (goToWindow is a no-op then); resync fetch on ws reconnect (items filed while offline stayed invisible); setStatus checks r.success (fetchJson never throws); knownIds includes the resolved tail (reopen must not self-toast). Panel detail+origin (2.69.1): item detail renders behind a collapsed `.ut-detail-exp` expander (open AND resolved rows — inline 2000-char details swamped the list); resolved rows carry the source session name (`.ut-sess`) and row-click jumps like open rows; clicks inside the expander are guarded from the row's jump-and-close

### vnc.js

  vnc.js               — VncManager (2.104.0, in-container desktop): LAZY lifecycle — POST /api/vnc/start spawns Xtigervnc (-localhost -SecurityTypes None, safe: the cookie-authed /api/vnc WS bridge is the ONLY route in) + XFCE session, both DETACHED (app restart keeps the desktop; boot ADOPTS an already-listening port — also the KasmVNC/bring-your-own path and the Xvfb+x11vnc test path). Env: VIBESPACE_VNC_PORT/DISPLAY/GEOMETRY. Client = src/lib/desktop-window.js ('desktop' window type, ⚙ menu entry gated on /api/vnc/status.available probed once at startup; noVNC is a SEPARATE ESM bundle public/novnc.js dynamic-imported on first use — top-level await can't live in the IIFE main bundle) **VNC BLACKLIST TRAP (2.111.1, real report 'Too many security failures'):** Xtigervnc launches with **`-UseBlacklist 0`** — MANDATORY, not optional. TigerVNC blacklists a source host after N unauthenticated connect-then-drop attempts (default 5, timeout doubles per strike); ALL desktop traffic is 127.0.0.1 (the bridge) AND `portListening()` connects+immediately destroys the socket on every status poll → TigerVNC counts each probe as a failure and locks localhost out. The cookie-authed bridge is the only route in, so the blacklist protects nothing. An already-tripped blacklist is in-memory: kill the stale Xtigervnc (or redeploy) to clear it.

### ssh-key-format.js

  ssh-key-format.js    — Private-key FORMAT classifier, PURE + shared server/browser (CJS pulled into the bundle like task-color-seq.js). **WHY a decoder and not a regex (2.246.0): an `openssh-key-v1` key's PEM armor is BYTE-IDENTICAL with or without a passphrase — the word ENCRYPTED appears NOWHERE** (only classic PEM's `Proc-Type: 4,ENCRYPTED` and PKCS#8's `BEGIN ENCRYPTED PRIVATE KEY` announce themselves). The old first-3-lines /ENCRYPTED/ check therefore missed effectively every key produced since OpenSSH 7.8: they imported "fine" and failed at connect with a bare `Permission denied (publickey)` that never mentions a passphrase at ANY -v level. Encryption state lives in the DECODED body (`magic | ciphername | kdfname`; none/none = plain). classifyPrivateKey → {format: empty|openssh-v1|pkcs8|pem|ppk|ssh2|unknown, encrypted, cipher?, usable, malformed?}; TOTAL (runs on every keystroke — bounded to the first 256 base64 chars, length words capped at 64, hostile input returns a verdict and NEVER throws). ssh.com armor is FOUR dashes with spaces (`---- BEGIN SSH2 …----`), not the PEM five

### ssh-key.js

  ssh-key.js           — Server-side private-key UNLOCK at import (2.246.0). prepareImportedKey(text, passphrase) → {body, wasEncrypted, format, fingerprint, type}, KeyError codes key-encrypted/key-bad-passphrase/key-ppk/key-unsupported/key-not-a-key/key-too-large/key-no-ssh-keygen/key-unlock-unavailable/key-unlock-failed (the CLIENT renders localized text off the code). **PASSPHRASE TRANSPORT IS ENV, NEVER ARGV**: ssh-keygen reads a passphrase only from a tty or SSH_ASKPASS (never stdin), so a throwaway 0700 helper (`printf %s "$VIBESPACE_KEY_PASS"`) in a 0700 mkdtemp dir is exec'd with the secret in the CHILD ENV (/proc/<pid>/environ is 0400) + SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=force; argv is only `-p -N '' -f <tmpfile>` (/proc/<pid>/cmdline is world-readable — the 2.126.0 rule) and HOME points at the temp dir so ssh-keygen can never touch the server's ~/.ssh. Temp dir removed on EVERY path incl. throws. **NON-NEGOTIABLE ERROR ORDER: an askpass helper that can't be exec'd (noexec tmpdir) makes ssh-keygen exit 255 saying "incorrect passphrase supplied" for a CORRECT passphrase — the only discriminator is the `ssh_askpass:` marker in stderr, which MUST be checked BEFORE the passphrase branch** or a hardened container lies to users forever. Fingerprint is computed in JS from the `-y` blob (byte-identical to `ssh-keygen -l`, verified — no extra spawn). node crypto is NOT an option: it cannot parse openssh-key-v1 at all, so a crypto-based impl passes RSA tests and fails every real ed25519 key. **The STORED key is UNLOCKED — this removes the passphrase, it does not preserve it** (every ssh here is BatchMode=yes and can never prompt); disclosed in docs/device-agent.md. Tests: scripts/test-ssh-key.mjs (60) + scripts/test-ssh-key-dialog.mjs (24, real headless browser)

### ctx-sync.js

  ctx-sync.js          — **THE ctx-folder sync, ONE implementation for every machine (2.277.0).** The old split — ssh=bidirectional rsync NO caps, dial=hashed sync with a SILENT ≤400-files/≤2MB cap — meant a 3MB context file reached every ssh host and never reached a dial device, invisibly. Hashed newer-wins sync (sha-equal ⇒ untouched, echo can't ping-pong; traversal-guarded pulls) over the device link on EVERY transport; ssh degrades to its legacy rsync pair only when the link is down; caps 1000 files/16MB and **every capped skip is REPORTED** (deduped serverNotice — a bounded sync may refuse a file, never lose one silently). Local sessions read the contextDir directly (TRANSPORT-ONLY, not a divergence). Test: scripts/test-ctx-sync.mjs (13).

### discovery-facts.js

  discovery-facts.js   — **Shared discovery INTERPRETATION (2.278.0)** — deliberately tiny so the esbuild agentd bundle carries it (unlike the ssh one-file scanner, the daemon CAN share code). One naming rule (was DRIFTED: local first-line vs remote whole-message-collapsed = one session, two names; + unterminated-truncated-string fallback the old regex missed), one extractTailIds (was 3 implementations feeding claimJsonls with different mention windows; session-store delegates, null-on-unreadable contract kept), and pidLooksClaude — the PID-reuse guard the daemon snapshot never had (liveness-only = phantom running session on a recycled pid). **R5 steps 2-3 (2.290.0/2.291.0): + `interpretDiscoveryLines(out, {hostId, hostName, claimJsonls})` AND `synthesizeDiscoveryLines(snap)`** (snapshot→fact-lines, also extracted out of hosts) — with both halves shared, the daemon runs the WHOLE chain as the `discovery-claims` op — **SWITCHED LIVE in 2.292.0** (hosts.discoverSessions asks the device first; ladder: claims op → server-side snapshot+synthesize+interpret → legacy ssh script → stale cache) (capability-gated; device returns finished session cards, orchestrator's remaining job = cross-machine merge). Parity in test-usage-scan-op: device-computed claims BYTE-IDENTICAL to the server-side interpretation of the same snapshot. Original note: — the ~120-line lock-first CLAIM algorithm (exact-id → tail-id → mtime; keeper adoption; codex rollout cards; brand-new-lock case) extracted verbatim out of hosts.discoverSessions so the interpretation can run WHERE the facts live (the future dark `discovery.v2` op — device computes its own claims, orchestrator only merges). `claimJsonls` INJECTED (session-store must not be pulled into this tiny module). Golden-fixture parity: scripts/test-discovery-interpret.mjs (9, all the tricky claim cases). Fixture trap recorded in the m3m4 suite: /bin/sleep can be a uutils MULTI-CALL binary that dispatches on argv0 — a symlink named 'claude' dies; use a script. Test: scripts/test-discovery-facts.mjs (18).

### writer-sweep.js

  writer-sweep.js      — **THE pre-resume writer sweep, ONE implementation for every machine (CS separation, 2.276.0).** The invariant: before resuming, no OTHER process may still be writing that conversation's transcript (two writers on one JSONL = the B-4058 corruption class). It used to exist THREE times — ssh, dial, and NOT AT ALL for local; the local gap was never a decision, it is what happens when `hostId` is a BRANCH instead of a PARAMETER (whoever fixes the remote bug never exercises the local twin). `sweepWriters(hosts, hostId, rid, …)` — falsy hostId = this machine via device #0; ssh keeps its per-op channel as fallback, dial correctly has none, local throws rather than pretending. Every kill leg echoes `SWEPT:<pid>` and the created reply carries them (a destructive op must never be silent). **FORKS NEVER SWEEP (2.284.4, real incident: forking a LIVE conversation SIGTERMed the parent's claude mid-turn the day the local sweep went live)** — a fork resumes the PARENT's rid but only READS that transcript and writes a NEW id's JSONL, so a live parent writer is legitimate; the resume-already-live guard exempts forks by design, which voids the "live sessions were filtered upstream" assumption the sweeps were written under. All three ws-handler gates carry `!data.fork`; the test's drift guard fails any new call site without it. Test: scripts/test-writer-sweep.mjs. **CODEX LEGS (P1 codex double-writer, 2026-09).** `writerSweepScript(rid, shq, {backend, protectSids})` / `sweepWriters(…, {backend, protectSids})`: the claude script is byte-for-byte what it was (default backend); `backend:'codex'` swaps the holder legs for ① a /proc fd scan (lsof on macOS/BSD) for an open `rollout-*-<threadId>.jsonl` — the pattern also matches `.jsonl.zst` (codex ≥0.153 may compress) — and ② an argv leg for `codex resume <threadId>` (a TUI in an external terminal) / `CODEX_WEBUI_RESUME_ID=<threadId>` (an orphaned VibeSpace wrapper or its dtach master); the pipe-session-meta + keeper legs are shared. Both codex legs kill only `*codex*` cmdlines and report `SWEPT:`. **THE PROTECT LIST is load-bearing**: a codex app-server keeps EVERY rollout of its thread tree open for its whole lifetime — measured on the dev box, one app-server (`…/bin/codex app-server`) held the parent + three finished sub-agent rollouts two days later — so a plain "kill the holder" would SIGTERM a healthy LIVE session's app-server whenever a sub-agent or forked-away thread is resumed (the 2.284.4 class). ws-create's `sweepOpts(hostId)` passes the webui ids of the live codex sessions on the TARGET machine; the script resolves a candidate's `CLAUDE_WEBUI_SESSION_ID` from its argv (dtach/wrapper carry the env pairs as args) or `/proc/<pid>/environ` (`ps -E` on macOS) and skips protected holders — live sessions are the resume-already-live guard's business, the sweep reaches EXTERNAL/orphaned writers only. The argv leg scans `ps -eo pid=,args=`, and the sweep's own `sh -c <script>` carries RID in its argv: the literal `VS_WRITER_SWEEP` sentinel in the script text is what skips the sweep shell and its subshells — keep it. Protect ids are validated `^[\w-]+$` before they reach the shell. Parity: local and remote receive a byte-identical codex script (test §8); test §9 drives the real script against real holder processes (temp HOME, real rollout path shape, SIGTERM observed; protect-list positive/negative; argv leg; .zst; non-codex holder untouched; the claude script never touches a codex holder); test §10 pins the ws-create guard functionally (real `createWsCreateHandler`) + every sweep site passing `sweepOpts`. **THE SUITE'S OWN ARGV IS NOT A SAFE HOLDER (test-only fix, review batch after 2.369.31):** the cmdline guard substring-matches `*claude*` over the whole `ps -o args=` (deliberate — the real CLI runs as `node …/claude/cli.js`), so when the suite held the transcript fd in ITS OWN process and ran from a git worktree under `~/.claude/worktrees/` (where the worktree-only-smokes law puts every agent) it matched its own guard and SIGTERMed itself: exit 143, `npm run ci` red, on code that was fine. The live fd-scan leg now spawns TWO holder processes with controlled argv — a neutral `node -e` (must survive) and a `claude-cli.js` script (must be swept, the positive control that proves the scan reached the holder at all).

### remote-shell.js

  remote-shell.js      — **ALSO buildRemoteExec (2.279.0): the five remote spawn command builders (ssh terminal/ssh keeper chat/ssh agentd pipe/dial pty/dial pipe) are ONE composition** — `cd → pre → resolve → AMBIENT_OAT_UNSET → tokenAssign/acctEnv → exec env parts/tail`, every difference a named parameter; the ambient-oat strip is STRUCTURAL (2.267.0 hand-added it to five sites) and provably ordered before the deliberate token assign; two builders' redundant double-REMOTE_PRELUDE gone. Drift guard in test-remote-shell (20 asserts) fails anyone hand-assembling an `exec env` line in ws-handler. **ONE definition of the remote shell prelude + POSIX node finder (2.274.0, CS unification).** Every remote command (spawn builders ×4, capability probes, agentd stdio bootstrap, tool install/uninstall, usage harvest) must import `REMOTE_PRELUDE`/`nodeFinder()`/`buildRemoteShellPrelude()` — the string was copy-pasted into 11 sites and the copies DRIFTED (spawn builders running a stale variant is exactly how a bug becomes remote-only; two node-finder copies were the weaker no-PATH-export variant, leaving `#!/usr/bin/env node` tools dead on dash hosts). scripts/test-remote-shell.mjs FAILS if any file re-inlines either string.

### hosts.js

  hosts.js             — HostManager (ssh host registry, connectivity test, keygen, remote discovery + first-user-message names, bootstrap, backend-status, dir-complete, fetchSessionJsonl = remote transcript → data/remote-jsonl cache; **add() stays SYNCHRONOUS (setDialToken calls it then findByDeviceId on the result — an async add returns a Promise there and silently breaks device pairing), so it can NEVER run the ssh-keygen unlock: the /api/hosts route decrypts first (src/ssh-key.js) and hands add() plaintext; add()'s own classifyPrivateKey check is the BELT for callers that skip the route and REFUSES a still-encrypted key (code 'key-encrypted') rather than storing one that only fails at connect time. 2.246.0 also moved the duplicate-NAME check BEFORE the key write (the old order wrote data/ssh/<id>.key then threw, orphaning a file no record referenced) and importBundle SKIPS a passphrase-protected key returning {warnings} — nothing can prompt during an unattended import, and persistence.js surfaces it in the `applied` array;** 2.129.0 remote-integration transparency: static AGENT_TOOLS list (ws-handler's per-spawn distribution reuses it), agentToolsStatus(id) = one read-only ssh round trip (per-tool sha256 — content compare, NOT mtime: hook/status are regenerated every local boot — + remote hook registration grep + node + keeper .sock count), installAgentTools (tar-over-stdin + hook-register), uninstallAgentTools (register --uninstall BEFORE rm; only our 6 files, .tok-* untouched). **`_hostShell(h, script)` (2.188.0) = the shared read-only probe channel: ssh hosts via _ssh, dial devices via dm.runCmd — backendStatus/accountsStatus/agentToolsStatus/cliPrimaryKey/readRemoteOAuth all route through it (they were ssh-only, so a selected dial device showed everything not-installed/unreachable; install/uninstall stay ssh-only — dial tools ship per-spawn). accountsStatus knows every claude auth shape (OAuth/primaryApiKey/apiKeyHelper — keep in sync with backendStatus's loginMethod chain, the 2.186.7/2.188.0 same-dialog-contradiction class) + returns host identity (claude config email; codex auth.json JWT email/plan decoded server-side). ⚠ CLI AUTH PRECEDENCE (2.191.0, verified by disassembling the 2.1.211 binary — CW-H200 incident): a configured `apiKeyHelper` in the MERGED settings UNCONDITIONALLY disables claude.ai OAuth (HS() gate), valid creds or not — so backendStatus/accountsStatus report `keyHelper` as an INDEPENDENT flag (never an elif rung) and Manage Agents warns "apiKeyHelper overrides this login" when both exist; the billing switcher explains why no pick can change it. `--settings '{"apiKeyHelper":null}'` is DROPPED by the CLI's field-level schema .catch (null fails string validation); **`""` VERIFIED working (2.236.0 controlled A/B): an inline empty-string helper overrides the file's helper — apiKeySource flips apiKeyHelper→none (subscription OAuth) and the turn succeeds; buildSessionArgs merges it (`neutralizeKeyHelper`) for every EXPLICIT subscription pick (the bare 'subscription' CLI-login sentinel deliberately keeps machine behavior)**; forceLoginMethod only affects the login FLOW, not runtime precedence. 2.191.0 also: `fetchWorkflowState` (remote View Workflow — one compound probe, nonce-delimited payload, GNU-else-BSD stat, 2s TTL, ≤1MB for dial's runCmd stdout slice), `fetchAgentJsonl` + the generalized `_fetchRemoteByFind` core (fetchSessionJsonl's cache/invariants, parameterized find), and discovery sessions carry `pid` (Terminate on remote EXTERNAL cards reaches killRemotePid — it silently no-op'd without it).**) **ONE CACHE SLOT, MANY REMOTE FILES (2.369.x (codex-zst review batch)):** a backend's `remoteFind` predicate may resolve DIFFERENT files across polls — codex matches `rollout-*<tid>.jsonl` OR `.jsonl.zst` and the host compresses a finished rollout — while `data/remote-jsonl/<host>/<backend>/<id>.jsonl` is keyed by conversation id alone. The `.meta` therefore records `remotePath` + `compressed` next to {size,mtime}: a changed path invalidates the slot (full refetch, no cache-valid short-circuit), and a compressed remote — or an already-compressed cache file (magic-byte check, so pre-fix metas are covered too) — is NEVER delta-appended, because recompression rewrites the file rather than appending plain bytes. Without it the append-only slab path concatenated the compressed twin's bytes onto the cached plain prefix and stamped it complete; a stopped thread never changes again, so the corrupt transcript was served forever. The locate pipeline is `find … | sort | head -1` so the pick is deterministic and prefers the plain twin. **AND THE ALREADY-CORRUPTED SLOTS HEAL THEMSELVES (review follow-up, same batch):** every slot in the field carries a PRE-provenance meta, and reading “no remotePath” as “same file” is vacuously true — the hybrid a stopped thread’s slot already held kept passing the short-circuit forever. So `sameRemote` is strict (`meta.remotePath === remotePath`), a meta without provenance is NOT valid (refetch whole ONCE, rewrite it, never delta onto it), and the cache FILE is checked independently of its meta: head magic must agree with the resolved remote, and a plain cache whose last 4KB carry a NUL byte or a zstd frame header is spliced (an append lands foreign bytes at the TAIL). Single exception: a remote already past `maxBytes` cannot be refetched whole at all (the delta path is how the slot grew there), so a byte-verified cache adopts the provenance instead of failing “too large”. Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥/⑥b (stub device switching remote files under one slot; a pre-written hybrid + old-shape meta healing with the remote standing still, negative-controlled against the pre-fix predicate).

### clerk-auth.js

  clerk-auth.js        — ClerkAuth (2.103.0, env-gated: VIBESPACE_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY = on-switch, frontend-API host + JWKS URL DERIVED from the key's base64 payload; VIBESPACE_CLERK_ALLOWED_EMAILS comma list, @domain = whole domain, EMPTY = reject all). verifyToken = pure node:crypto RS256 vs cached JWKS (1h TTL, unknown-kid rotation refetch, exp/nbf ±60s skew, iss check, alg pinned to RS256). REQUIRES an email claim in the token — Clerk dashboard: session-token custom claims {"email":"{{user.primary_email_address}}"} or a JWT template named 'vibespace' (login page tries template first). No Clerk SECRET key anywhere — the backend only reads JWKS

### agent-routes.js

  agent-routes.js      — setupAgentRoutes() (2.93.0 split): every agent-facing endpoint — vibespace-ask/status/task routes, task-context/prompt-context injection (incl. user preamble + per-turn extras), stop-check nudge arbiter. Injection ORDER + SIZE are load-bearing — read the renderContext notes before touching payloads. TEACHING ARCHITECTURE (2.111.22-25, user-driven): the injected tools section is a DISCOVERY layer — per tool one trigger sentence + one fenced COPY-READY complete invocation (status sample carries --reason+--detail+--urgency; progress/ask carry --detail) so the first copied call is already valid; syntax/edge cases live in each CLI's own no-args output; hard rules enforce at point of use (waiting-state reason+detail rejection, success-output reminders). Don't grow the upfront block back into a rules dump (2.68.0 oversize incident is the other guardrail). **GROUP UPDATES DELIVER AS DIFFS (2.113.0, user request):** a mid-session group change no longer re-injects the whole group context — the session keeps a per-group SNAPSHOT of what it last saw (`s._groupSnap`, set at every delivery next to `_groupSeenAt`/`_ctxSig`; in-memory, restart ⇒ full re-delivery like the seen markers) and prompt-context injects a `<vibespace-task-update>` DELTA block (tasks.renderContextDiff: renamed / objective / backlog PARKED-RESOLVED-DROPPED-CLAIMED-UNCLAIMED-reworded-REMOVED one-liners (2.122.0; since 2.123.0 matched by the stable item id — occurrence-indexed text pairing retired — and TARGETED: `sessionKey` filters events to items the session created or claimed, everyone else keeps only the count pointer) / contextDir files updated-new-removed via old-vs-new `_ctxSig` / new activity entries LAST; ~5KB hard cap with tail-first truncation + `show --full` pointers; EMPTY diff = a no-op edit ⇒ nothing injected, markers still advance — the old code re-sent everything for those too; checklist deltas REMOVED with the feature in 2.121.0). No snapshot / setting `agents.contextUpdateDiffs` off ⇒ the old full "was UPDATED" payload. Also: FIRST-TIME groups on one prompt get ONE layered renderMultiContext (the codex first-prompt path) instead of N full payloads each repeating the ~2.3KB tools section — but ONLY when they cover the WHOLE membership (renderMultiContext states absolute membership; a subset call told a 3-group session "belongs to 2" — partial sets render per-group, count-free). REVIEW-HARDENED invariants (10-finding adversarial pass, do not regress; the checklist occurrence-indexed-diff one retired with the feature in 2.121.0): contextDir set/changed/cleared ⇒ diff returns null ⇒ FULL fallback (structural — the file index + folder conventions must be taught, a one-line path left pre-existing files invisible); the TASK.md pointer renders for LOCAL sessions only (remote ctx rsync excludes `.vibespace/`); contextDirSignature escapes `%`/`|` in paths (raw `|` sheared parse entries → wrong-path reports); **N CHANGED GROUPS COLLAPSE INTO ONE COMBINED BLOCK (2.113.1, user directive twice-clarified — 穿插/layered, the 2.68.0 class):** stacked per-group `<vibespace-task-update>` blocks + the ~2KB persisted-preview truncation could hide the very FACT that a 2nd group changed — `renderContextDiffMulti` (fed by `diffChanges`, which returns {lines, bits} where bits = phrase summaries like '3 new activity') renders ONE block whose HEADER line enumerates EVERY changed group + its summary, then per-group `##` sections SMALLEST-FIRST, 6.5KB cap with tail-first truncation (details die, the enumeration never does); ANY multi-block delivery (diff block + full re-deliveries + new-group fulls) is manifest-headed — `<vibespace-delivery-note>` naming EVERY block by group + the persisted-output rescue, diff block first, fulls last; combined parts >8KB with no rescue line get one prepended; markers/snapshot advance at RENDER, so a harness-dropped delivery (hook 3s timeout) stays lost until `show --full`/restart — ACCEPTED, same class as the seen-bump loss window. Tests: scripts/test-context-diff.mjs (store) + scripts/test-prompt-context.mjs (route, fake express)

### usage-routes.js

  usage-routes.js      — setupUsage() (2.92.0 split): the ENTIRE usage/rate-limit cluster from server.js — passive statusline ingest, read-only OAuth token, opt-in active poll, on-demand quota refresh, codex rollout-tail summarizer (§ban-safety rules apply — see kb-design-lessons §9). **Account-identity truth (2.114.1, real mixup report):** ~/.claude.json's oauthAccount goes STALE after a /login switch (config said fish.audio, token was actually gmail — popup looked like two subs swapped quotas). The on-demand ⟳ ALSO fetches `/api/oauth/claude_cli/roles` (same human-gated click, never scheduled) and bakes orgUuid/orgName/orgEmail into the usage cache; the global↔named link prefers org-uuid equality over config-email match (proven-different org BREAKS an email match); `globalLogin.{actualEmail,identityMismatch}` drive the popup's corrected label + amber warning; data/bin/vibespace-usage preserve-merges the org fields like scopedWeekly. Smoke: scripts/test-usage-link.mjs. **2.260.0 chat-mode freshness (B-7edc/B-292b): ⟳ PREFERS the CLI's own `get_usage` control channel** — a live LOCAL claude chat session billed to the refreshed account answers `get_usage` ITSELF (first-party fetch, = typing /usage there; probeUsageForAccountKey in server.js → vsu- request_id interception in the stdout parse → usage-cache write), human-gated + 60s throttle + silent fallback to the bare read-only call. **AUTO-firing get_usage is REJECTED (user decision 2026-08-09): a machine-initiated quota check is the banned automated-access pattern regardless of who makes the HTTP call.** The chat-mode PASSIVE signal is the LIMIT BANNER instead: the CLI's own "You've reached your … limit" chat record → ClaudeCodeAdapter.parseLimitBanner (5h/weekly/scoped-by-name; unknown→fiveHour = shortest self-heal) → markLimitBanner writes the bucket dead into usage-cache (keeps a known future reset, else bounded 5h/24h guess so the marker self-expires via reset-passed⇒full) + IMMEDIATELY maybePoolAutoSwitch — zero API calls, this is what makes pool auto-switch work for chat-only accounts. LIVE-VERIFIED get_usage envelope (2026-08-09, real session, ZERO inference — the CLI answers without any turn): payload at `control_response.response.response` (DOUBLE nesting); `utilization` = 0-100 INTEGERS (parser normalizes >1); NO `model_scoped` array — named nullable fields (`seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet`) + internal CODENAME buckets (no-resets_at codenames skipped); parseGetUsageResponse has the named-field fallback. Test: scripts/test-get-usage-parse.mjs (21, incl. the captured live fixture + all three live banner wordings). **2.266.1 IDENTITY-GROUP token resolution (user's correct logic: if the pool can BILL a conversation to the account, ⟳ must be able to READ its quota): the refresh route's token lookup walks every id in the target's identity group (usageIdentityAccountIds in server.js — org-merged accounts span `__global__` + the named sub, and whichever token a live session is refreshing is the fresh one; the other idles into expiry) and probeUsageForAccountKey matches any live session whose resolveUsageKey lands in the group (pooled sessions resolve to their real target, so a pooled conversation answers get_usage for the account it bills); the result always writes to the REQUESTED key's cache. 'No valid token' now means the whole identity truly has none.** **2.245.0: POST /api/usage/refresh `{host, account}` = host-HELD account quota — hosts.readRemoteSubOAuth reads the account's own `~/.vibespace/subs/<id>` token on the host (READ-ONLY, never refresh, same §ban-safety class), persists `usage-cache/host-<hid>-<aid>.json` (boot loader matches the `-(sub-…)` pattern FIRST — the plain `host-*` regex would swallow these files), served as /api/usage `hostAccounts` keyed `<hostId>:<accountId>`; throttle key `host:<hid>:<aid>`. The usage POPUP's Remote-hosts section is REMOVED — hosts + held accounts live in the Agents machine overview; the popup tail carries a "Full overview →" door.** 2.369.33: refreshViaCliPanel's cache merge projects WEEKLY resets (sevenDay + scopedWeekly) from the previous cache entry when the panel omits them (0% bucket): claude-quota.projectReset(prev, WEEK_SEC, now) + resetsAtEstimated:true (the popup shows ≈); the 5-hour bucket is never projected (its window starts with the first request).

**identityMismatch semantics (2.337.3, owner confusion report):** `_usageGlobalLink.email` comes from the GLOBAL ~/.claude.json oauthAccount — a field EVERY relocated dir login also stamps (CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR relocates only the secret store; the 2.244.2 consequence). After any Manage-Agents account login the config identity is therefore residue, NOT the machine login. The token-derived orgEmail/orgUuid is the truth and drives display + linking. The UI treats the mismatch as an informational note (residue explanation, "no action needed") — NEVER as a warning telling the user to /login (that advice would trigger a needless real global login switch).

### opslog.js

  opslog.js            — Optional persistent ops log (2.115.0, env-gated no-op): VIBESPACE_OPSLOG_DIR → console tee into daily-rotated files + boot/exit/crash markers (VIBESPACE_OPSLOG_CEPHFS_* = server kernel-mounts the path-scoped subtree first, same mechanism as My storage). Built for fleets: logs survive pod recreation on shared storage the admin scans directly — deliberately NOT a logging stack (no per-node agents/log DB; user architecture decision 2026-07-13). Hung-mount-proof: async-only writes + 10s circuit breaker (one stuck write disables logging, never blocks the app — §2.108.3 threadpool lesson); bounded queue (2000 lines) drops on wedged disk. Helm opslog.* (secret mons/fsName/client/key/path, defaults u-<user>-opslog)

### incident.js

  incident.js          — FREEZE THE SCENE for a panic-button capture (2.239.0, the admin's correction: users troubleshoot their own problems — ssh/resume/kill — and destroy the evidence before anyone looks). captureLocal: process table, dtach socket stats, VERBATIM copies of session-meta + wrapper-meta (clobbered by every create/kill), claude lock files (deleted on CLI exit), and per referenced conversation the transcript IDENTITY (size+mtime+**sha256**+frozen 512KB tail — a later manual `--resume` double-write/fork becomes provable). captureRemote: one bounded probe per machine over hosts._hostShell (ssh AND dial) — remote process table, locks, keeper/agentd state, transcript stat+sha256+last timestamps, project dirs, disk, CLI versions. ALL read-only + bounded (child processes with timeouts — never node fs on a possibly-hung mount, §2.108.3; ≤6 hosts, ≤12 conversations, size-capped copies) and per-step try/caught so a dead host degrades the bundle instead of failing it. Runs ASYNC after the id is returned; `env.json.pending` marks a cut-off freeze. Test: scripts/test-incident.mjs (16 asserts incl. frozen-copy-survives-clobber). **B-8ebb (last row, codex rollouts in the freeze):** the transcript-identity loop no longer knows only claude's project dirs — after the claude scan it iterates the HARNESS REGISTRY (`require('./harnesses').list()`, lazy) and asks each `store.locate(cid)` where its conversation lives: codex rollouts (`rollout-*-<tid>.jsonl` / `.jsonl.zst`, the local tree or the remote-jsonl cache) get the same size+mtime+sha256 identity + frozen tail (`<cid>__<dir>.tail.jsonl[.zst]`, `harness:'codex'`, `compressed:true` for a .zst whose raw tail bytes are the evidence, not lines); claude's locate dedups against the scan. The remote probe is generated the same way — `buildRemoteTranscriptProbe(cids)` emits one `find <root> <findExpr> | probe_transcript` line per (conversation × harness) from each descriptor's `store.remoteFind(id)` (claude's expression byte-identical to before, codex's `$HOME/.codex/sessions … rollout-*<id>.jsonl(.zst)` added; `probe_transcript` is one shell function; `lastts` greps with -a for compressed bytes) and the versions line asks codex too. Never a backend id branch in incident.js — a third harness freezes by registering a store. Test: scripts/test-incident.mjs (+8: a codex rollout in the cache is fingerprinted + tail-frozen + tagged, the claude conversation still freezes once, remote probe lines per harness, no id branch).

### routes/persistence.js

  routes/persistence.js — …also **LAYOUT ROLLBACK POINTS (2.296.0, after a bug flattened a user's whole desktop layout)**: `writeLayouts` is the ONE choke point every layout write passes through, so it snapshots the PREVIOUS state into `data/layout-history/` whenever the desktop→window SHAPE changes (per-desktop sorted window ids; geometry drags and z-order churn deliberately ignored or one drag would evict every useful restore point). A destructive write therefore always leaves the last good shape on disk — the thing the incident lacked, where two EMPTIED desktops made even the pre-damage mapping unrecoverable. Bounded 40; **filenames carry a sequence suffix — a bare ISO stamp has ms resolution and a mass-resume burst lands inside one millisecond, silently overwriting the older entry i.e. destroying the pre-damage state exactly when it matters (caught by the bounded-history assertion)**. Legacy top-level `autoSave` counted too (instances without virtual desktops otherwise read "0 windows"). Routes GET /api/layout-history + POST /api/layout-history/:id/restore (id regex-validated; restoring writes through the same path so it is ITSELF undoable). UI: ⚙ → Restore a previous layout… (setup-flows `_showLayoutHistory`). Test: scripts/test-layout-history.mjs (11).

### lib/incident-recorder.js

  lib/incident-recorder.js — "Report a problem" panic button (2.238.0, the timezone-gap fix): ALWAYS-ON client ring buffers (clicks/coarse keys — typed TEXT never recorded, only a per-burst marker; ws message TYPES both directions, never payloads; console errors/warns) + on-demand full client snapshot → POST /api/incident → server adds its own scene (activeSessions digest, 30-min sysinfo history, in-memory server console ring (600 lines, wraps console.* in server.js), hosts digest, resume-breaker table + remote-discovery cache digest — the two conversation-disappeared-class facts that survive nowhere else, 2.238.1) → data/incidents/<inc-id>/bundle.json (+followup.json auto-appended 2min later; newest 30 kept). **snapshot.chatTraces (2.264.0, B-21bc user request): each open chat window's SCROLL TRACER ring tail ships automatically** — chat-view's `_trace()` is an always-on capped ring again (the 2.111.8 removal stubbed it AND the documented Ctrl+Shift+J dump, leaving viewport-jump reports blind): paging/trim/jump/runsRestore breadcrumbs + a coarse scroll sampler (>400px moves w/ pin state + wheelAgo — user-scroll vs programmatic-yank discriminator) + an unpin breadcrumb; positions/op tags only, never content. User relays the short id; admin reads the bundle. Entry: ⚙ → Report a problem…; GET /api/incidents lists. **SEMANTIC OP BREADCRUMBS (2.296.0): `window.__vsOp(name, data)` pushes into a 4th ring (`op`, cap 300) — for ops that RELOCATE OR REPLACE a window/session, the class where "what did the app just do to my layout" is the whole question. The pool-collapse incident left NO trace of its desktop reassignments (the action ring has clicks, the ws ring has message types; neither answers 'what moved these windows'), so the sequence had to be reconstructed by reading code. Wired at desktop-move / pool-cold-restart / resume-place. Names+ids only — the privacy rule is unchanged. The server bundle also carries the LAYOUT TABLE (window→desktop+sid) and the layout-history index, which is what turns a flattened layout from JSON surgery into one click.** PRIVACY: the note is the only free text — keep it that way. Test: scripts/test-incident.mjs

### telemetry.js

  telemetry.js         — Telemetry (2.84.0, local-first observability): monthly ndjson shards data/telemetry/events-YYYY-MM.ndjson of {ts,kind:error|event|boot|server-error|metric,name,detail,stack,version,ua,value}; summary() = grouped errors + byName/byDay/byVersion + metrics (n/p50/p95/max/last per numeric metric — client boot-to-ready-ms/js-heap-mb/dom-nodes/open-windows/longtask-* per-min aggregates, server srv-rss-mb/srv-heap-mb/srv-evloop-max-lag-ms/srv-live-sessions/srv-ws-clients/srv-subagent-watchers/srv-normalizer-msgs/srv-buffer-files + HTTP rolling window (reqs/avg/max + slow-request events, route sanitized to 3 segments) every 5min, srv-usage-scan-ms/srv-jsonl-parse-ms on their operations; client also ws-outage-ms/gap-slab-load-ms/history-render-ms/chat-search-ms/session-create-roundtrip-ms/upload-mbps — 2.94.0-2.95.0; deep modules report via the global.__vsMetric hook); optional batch forwarding to telemetry.forwardUrl with an anonymous instance id (data/telemetry/.instance-id). Client side = src/lib/telemetry-client.js (window.onerror/unhandledrejection + boot event + coarse feature events like window-open:<type>/session-create; installed in client.js BEFORE new App() so App-constructor boot crashes are captured — the 'silent blank page' class; 60-events/page-session cap, 5 per identical error). ⚙ → Diagnostics report… (app.js _openDiagnostics) renders GET /api/telemetry/summary as a blob-URL HTML page in the embedded browser. NAMES ONLY, never content — see settings telemetry.enabled/forwardUrl/forwardToken. COLLECTOR MODE (2.101.0): ingestRemote() writes forwarded batches to central-YYYY-MM.ndjson shards (per-record inst id, remote ts/version preserved via _clean(ev,{remote:true}) — clamped ±sane window so a bad sender clock can't scatter shards); centralSummary() = per-instance {total,errors,lastTs,versions} + errors grouped ACROSS instances; route gating lives in server.js (VIBESPACE_TELEMETRY_INGEST_TOKEN)

### manage-agents.js

    manage-agents.js   — installManageAgents(App): Manage-Agents dialog + Anthropic/ChatGPT account rosters/wizard (~790 lines, split from app.js). NOTE (2.178.0 redesign): account rows are FLEX (icon·name/email·usage·★·⋯), NOT the old shared subgrid — the roster renders into the ~260px rail panel too and the fixed grid tracks crushed the name column there. Test/Re-login-on-this-machine (2.332.0: existing-dir helper flow, attempt-id watcher — signed-out rows say 'Log in on this machine…')/Rename/email/Remove live in the ⋯ menu (showContextMenu — item property is `action`, NOT onClick, bit once); the four Add… buttons collapse into one '+ Add account…' menu on the roster header. **Roster order = TYPE, never add-order (2.268.5, user request): pool → subscription → API key, name-sorted within a type — the SAME comparator is inlined in the billing switcher (session-lifecycle.js) and the New Session account dropdown (app.js updateAcctRow); keep the three in sync (divergent ordering between surfaces reads as a shuffle). Since 2.369.18 BOTH rosters use the module-level `rosterSort` (codex pools gave the codex roster a type dimension) and the module-level `ROSTER_ICONS` (one POOL glyph for every backend); the pool ⋯ menu is ONE block, `_poolMenuItems(id, a, refresh)`, spliced in by both rosters — never copy it per backend.** Modal + rail panel share ONE stylesheet: _showAgentsDialog/openPluginsDialog add the modal body class to the {container}, and .acct-list is a container-type:inline-size context so `@container (max-width:300px)` wraps the usage donuts under the name. Backend header is single-line (strips the redundant '(Claude Code)' version suffix). 2.179.1 (real overflow report): the panel is FLAT (`.rail-panel .ob-backend` hairline sections; flex-wrap ONLY on non-roster rows — wrap on a COLUMN flex makes align-items:stretch fill the LINE not the container, the unshrinkable-221px bug); usage is width-adaptive (`.acct-usage-mini` = tightest-bucket pill, container query ≤340px swaps donuts→pill); every nowrap leaf (name/email/roster title/add button) is flex-shrinkable w/ ellipsis. Verified by headless screenshots + scrollWidth audits at 260/340/460px. **THREE-TAB LAYOUT (2.262.0, user: 平铺缺分级+指令孤立): refresh() branches on localStorage `vibespace.agentsTab` — Accounts (default; the LOCAL section = management home) / Machines (one `<details class=agents-mach-acc>` accordion card per host, probe runs LAZILY on first expand — opening the panel no longer fans probes to every machine; open-set in `vibespace.agentsMachOpen`, single host auto-expands) / Instructions (the agents-adv fields, own tab, expanded). Refresh-all shared by the first two tabs; smoke drives the real tab navigation. FIXTURE LESSON: the 2.258.0 menu redesign moved 'Fa 41%' spacing from text to CSS gap and the smoke's /Fa \d+%/ text-regex went silently always-false — asserts that match textContent must tolerate CSS-provided spacing.** Pre-2.262.0 machine-sectioned notes still apply INSIDE each surface: **(2.245.0): the host selector is GONE — one `.agents-machine-sec` per host (`.usage-section-title` headers, per-machine `renderMachine(body=section, selectedHost, hostLabel, …)` fills in PARALLEL); host sections list VERDICT-usable accounts only (B-f531 — never client-computed; unreachable host says "unreachable", never "not installed"+Install) + the host CLI-login row; the LOCAL section stays the FULL roster (management home). Per-row usage follows verdict.how (host-login→hosts quota / host-held→`hostAccounts['<hid>:<aid>']` / else local passive cache). Header ⟳ "Refresh all" = `_refreshAllQuota` per-target fan-out (click-only §ban-safety, Anthropic calls STAGGERED ~1.5s — burst 429 risk §9; failures inline per-row `.acct-refresh-err`, never silent, so NO full refresh after). The codex roster's bare `racct` was a latent ReferenceError killing it on every host view (swallowed by try/catch) — fixed. **DONUT-COLUMN INVARIANT (2.245.2, pixel-measured regression): the usage cluster is right-anchored against `.acct-key-actions`, so EVERY row must carry the same [★][⋯] icon pair (min-width 48px belt for star-only rows) — row-specific actions (host ⟳/login/import) go in the ⋯ menu, NEVER inline (each inline button shifts that row's donuts left); `.acct-refresh-err` is one ellipsized flex-basis:100% line.** Smoke: scripts/test-agents-overview.mjs (36 asserts incl. the ±1px alignment sweep at 3 widths + a labelHtml escaping check). **POOLED ROW (2.263.0, real report '池化被当API账号渲染+菜单有查看key'): `isPool` branch — dedicated POOL icon (overlapping circles, never KEY), iconTitle names pooling, the usage cell shows the CURRENT TARGET's donuts (+dead-reckoned est), and 'Show key…' is gated `!isSub && !a?.pooled`. 2.267.1 finished the sweep: the billing SWITCHER row (`a.pooled` branch — `名 → target` + target's usage/est; remote = disabled local-only) and the New Session dropdown (pool fell into the API-key else-branch as '— API key …undefined') stopped mislabeling pools too; sessionAuth already emits source:'pooled'. Backend header nuance (2.268.4: with namedLoggedIn>0 the row's PRIMARY 'Log in' button hides — two look-alike green primaries next to '+ Add account…' — and the machine-wide login moves into the Add-account menu as 'Log in machine-wide (claude /login)…'; fresh machines keep the button): /api/backend-status carries `namedLoggedIn` (usable named identities) + `machineLoginState` (2.267.4: 'expired' = creds file present but token-less — worded 已失效, never claims idle; absent = never-set-up — worded 未配置机器登录) so a token-less MACHINE login under full pooling renders an honest two-state chip + 'N named account(s) in use' instead of the misleading '未登录'; mobile: the worst-of quota chip includes scoped weeklies (est-aware) and the window-switcher billing chip names pool → target.** `_acctUsageHtml(u, est)` renders estimates as a light conic arc + dashed outer ring (`.acct-donut-est`; pill fallback dashed-underlines) via utils.estDisplayPair — est<reading ⇒ window rolled ⇒ dark collapses to 0 (user-specified). **2.268.7: the age cell is a TWO-LINE micro-column — data age on top, reset countdown of the row's tightest bucket below (est-aware pick, bucket-pressure color; per-donut + pill tooltips carry per-bucket 'resets in …'); `.acct-usage-age` min-width 6.5ch fits the widest countdown so the donut-column alignment invariant holds — conditional/variable-width rendering there shifts donuts per row (the measured-28px-jump class).**

### session-lifecycle.js

    session-lifecycle.js — installSessionLifecycle(App): create/attach/resume/fork/view/kill + billing switcher + replayOpenSpec (~680 lines, split from app.js) ****2.369.8 restartConversationInPlace(sessLike)** = one-click Terminate+Resume (pool cold-switch machinery: kill → exited(+400ms flush) → resumeSession same cid, excludeWebuiId; dead sessions skip straight to resume; per-session config rides the respawn). Exposed on: style-menu pending row (chat-status-bar onRestartSession), window-title right-click (taskbar showWindowContextMenu session block: Restart/Terminate/Resume/Locate in sidebar/Session properties), sidebar card menu (live). locateSessionInSidebar = Folders panel + expand collapsed group + scrollIntoView + .locate-flash. RESUME PRESERVES THE HOME DESKTOP (2.295.0, a fleet user, inc-mso43urh): `_snapshotWinBounds` captures `_desktopId` and createSession moves the resumed window back to it (guarded on existence, never onto Stage) — without it a POOL cold-switch (which kill+resumes sessions across EVERY desktop at once while one is active) piled the whole layout onto the active desktop. A single billing switch is unchanged (home == active). Test: scripts/test-resume-desktop.mjs.**

### sidebar-mounts.js

    sidebar-mounts.js  — Remote tab: machines (ssh hosts) + storage (mounts) rendering (installSidebarMounts mixin, ~1000 lines; 2.152.0: "Pair a device" action + _showDevicePairDialog — dial-out device pairing, mints via POST /api/agentd/dial-pair and renders the full installer command incl BOTH --dial-token and --host-token; the host token is what the daemon verifies OUR mux hello against — omit it and the device dials in but rejects every command). **KEY IMPORT (2.246.0): `_askPrivateKey({text,error,showPass})` is the reusable, PREFILLABLE "Private key" sub-dialog** — resolves {privateKey, keyPassphrase} or null; the Add-machine POST wraps it in a RETRY LOOP (a KEY_ERR_CODES failure re-opens it with the key still pasted + the localized reason, so a wrong passphrase is never a dead end). **TWO ORDERING TRAPS, both real: (1) `createModalShell`'s close() fires onClose on EVERY path, so the submit handler must `resolve(value)` BEFORE `ctx.close()` — close-then-resolve lets the cancel-resolver win and "Use this key" behaves exactly like Cancel (the entire paste-a-key flow was dead; idempotency alone does NOT save you, the FIRST settle wins; caught only by the headless dialog test). (2) sub-dialogs REUSE the overlay id `mounts-dialog-overlay`, and createModalShell REMOVES the existing one — so from the first sub-dialog onward the Add-machine dialog is DETACHED and _mountsDialog's inline error element renders into nothing; the submit callback takes `err` and toasts when `!err.isConnected`.** Passphrase + key text are read RAW from the elements (a passphrase may legitimately have leading/trailing whitespace — verified ssh-keygen accepts `'p@ss w/ spaces '`), deliberately bypassing _mountsDialog's trimming field reader. Detection reveals affordances but NEVER blocks submit (server is authoritative; an always-available "Key has a passphrase?" toggle covers a misjudged format), and the passphrase row uses `style.display`, not a `hidden` class (no global `.hidden` rule in this project)

### sidebar-rail.js

    sidebar-rail.js    — Sidebar activity rail (2.176.0, vscode-style, setting `sidebar.activityRail` default ON): installSidebarRail mixin — ~44px icon strip replaces the 3-tab bar; panels Folders/Tasks/Remote reuse the tab render paths, NEW panel tabs ports/agents/plugins/system render into the list area via _renderRailPanel (agents/plugins reuse the modal renderers through a `{container}` option on _showAgentsDialog/openPluginsDialog — those redirect to the rail panel when it exists, so ⚙-menu entries focus the panel); Ports panel = active forwards (open-via-proxy/publish/unpublish/stop) + per-machine scans, live on port-forwards-updated/machine-ports-new; badges (tasks ⚠ rides refreshTaskAttention, mounts=offline dial hosts, ports=forward count, diagnostics=1-day error count, one probe per page load); re-click active item = sidebar collapse; setting-off live-restores the classic tabs (wrapper .sidebar-main stays); mobile never renders it. 2.177.0: `sidebar.railPersistent` (default ON) — collapse keeps the 44px strip (`.rail-collapsed` class, width !important beats the resizer inline style; _applySidebarLayoutWidth reserves 44px; rail click while collapsed EXPANDS, never re-collapses); header `.sidebar-title` names the active panel (RAIL_TITLES); `.rail-panel` compact CSS lifts the agents modal min-width + wraps account rows; ports actions are error-surfaced (fetchJson never throws — check `r?.error`) + frp-unconfigured publish is dimmed-with-tooltip instead of a silent no-op. 2.353.0 SCAN CACHE (owner report: every broadcast-driven re-render wiped the freshly scanned list): scan results live in `this._portScanCache` (Map hostId → ports; survives panel re-render AND reopen), `renderList()` replays the cache on rebuild with fresh forward state so forwarded/published chips update live, and a `machine-ports-new` broadcast MERGES its announced ports into the cache before re-rendering (the panel must show what the toast talked about) — never leave scan results DOM-only. **2.354.0 PROCESS MANAGER (`_buildProcManager`, the btop analogue, replaces the old top-8 list):** System panel gains a full interactive table — GET /api/sysinfo/procs on the same 5s tick (remote ~10s), sort chips CPU/MEM/PID/Name/Tree (tree = partial forest over the capped table, missing parents become roots), 150ms-debounced filter (name/user/pid), display cap 120 with an explicit "+ show all" expander, per-row CPU gradient + hot coloring, expand → full cmdline + pid/ppid/user/state/etime + actions (Terminate/Force kill behind showConfirmDialog danger, Pause/STOP↔Resume/CONT unconfirmed, Copy cmd) → POST /api/sysinfo/signal with outcome toasts (gone vs still-running honesty). STRUCTURE RULES: controls (search input + sort chips) build ONCE outside the re-rendered table zone — an input inside the 5s-swapped zone loses focus every tick; expansions keyed by pid in `this._prcExpanded` survive refreshes; a `seq` guard drops stale /procs responses after a host switch; `this._prcRefresh` is called by the host switcher for an immediate swap; every cmd/user/etime string is escHtml'd (they come from REMOTE machines — the stored-XSS law); the server's own row gets a "server" chip + kill refusal instead of buttons. `.prc-name` is a flex pair (`.prc-nm` ellipsizes, chips `flex:none`) so the server/paused/zombie chip survives narrow-panel truncation. CDP asserts live in test-sidebar-rail.mjs (system-panel section). 2.356.0 UX batch: PID column (dim, right-aligned); auto-refresh PAUSE toggle in the header (`this._prcPaused` — the interval's periodic call checks it, explicit refreshes (host switch, kill outcome, un-pause) bypass; count label turns amber); panel zone order = bars → history charts → process table (the table is long — charts stay visible); memory history chart auto-scales its Y axis to peak×1.2 capped at the limit (an axis pinned to the machine total drew real usage as a flatline — the CPU chart was already data-scaled). GOTCHA fixed alongside (settings.js): applyRemote skips the settings-updated echo while a local save is pending — the sender receives its own broadcast, and a set(false)→set(true) inside the 500ms debounce was PERMANENTLY reverted by the first save's stale echo. Smoke: scripts/test-sidebar-rail.mjs (worktree+CDP, 14 asserts)

### usage-window.js

    usage-window.js      — Usage dashboard window (2.61.0, ⚙ → Usage, window type 'usage', openSpec openUsage). **2.96.0: default view = configurable panel dashboard** (usage-dashboard.js: panel = metricS × dimension × chart — 2.97.0 multi-metric: `metrics:[]` checkbox editor; 2.99.3 split-series: panel.splitBy = a 2nd dimension (editor "Split series by") — line→one series per split key, bars→STACKED; data = server 2-D pivots (`?pivot=day:account` → aggregate({pivots}) → pivots['a:b'] rows, cells = same finalized bucket shape as group rows), `panelPivots(panels)` tells the window's fetch which crosses to request and onChange/preset-apply refetch when a new one appears; top-6 + Other, labels from groups[splitBy] meta; Chart.js GOTCHA: every dataset MUST set yAxisID/xAxisID to the configured scale or a phantom default axis renders, Chart.js v4 (modular ~150KB, the ONLY chart lib since 2.98.0 — line/bar/doughnut all share one interaction model: formatted hover tooltips, legend series-toggling; bars auto-orient by dimension type; per-unit auto dual axes; instances MUST be destroyed via destroyCharts() before canvas removal — global registry + ResizeObserver leak otherwise; panels attach to the document BEFORE charts render or CSS-var color probes resolve black); 2.99.1: `.usage-body` is a container-type:inline-size container — `@container ≤700px` folds `.udash-grid` to 1 column so the layout tracks the WINDOW width (`.udash-add` spans `1 / -1`, NEVER `span 2` — in a 1-col grid `span 2` forces an implicit second track and the whole grid overflows); 2.99.2 h-scrollbar elimination invariants (verified 0 overflow 420–1100px, both views — do not regress): grid tracks `minmax(0,1fr)` never bare `1fr` (grid items default min-width:auto → one wide stat/table blows the track), `.udash-panel` is min-width:0 + its OWN container-type:inline-size (stat font `clamp(14px,10cqw,30px)` scales with the panel), `.udash-panel-body` overflow-x:auto contains table scroll, `.usage-seg` wraps, classic `.usage-grid` floors at `min(340px,100%)`, `.usage-body` overflow-x:hidden as the final guarantee; per-panel ✎ editor + ⋯ menu, presets, layout persisted in settings 'usage.dashboard'; pre-2.96 fixed layout = 'Classic view' escape hatch). Reads /api/usage-stats: headline tiles (est API-equivalent cost / total tokens = the 4 peer component tiles cached-read+cache-write+fresh-input+output / cache-hit-ratio / requests), daily-trend canvas bar chart, breakdowns by billing-type/account(type badges)/model/project/mode/cache-efficiency/hour/weekday/top-sessions, range filter (+ Custom… from/to date pickers, 2.63.0) + backend filter + ACCOUNT filter chips (2.63.0 — All/each account/CLI login; chip options UNION across loads so a narrow range doesn't drop chips; when app._usageGlobal.accountId links the CLI login to a named account the two render as ONE chip whose key is the comma pair), CSV export, + a built-in Pricing editor (tier list from pricing.tiers keys, per-account discount%, PATCH /api/usage-stats/pricing). Cost = estimate (subscriptions plan-covered). **DEVICE filter (2.128.0, user directive "hosts are devices, not accounts")**: a top-level 设备 row (All/This machine/each remote host) gates the WHOLE view via aggregate hostFilter (/api/usage-stats `host` param, matches ev.host||'local'); host buckets (type 'host') are EVICTED from the Account chips, and selecting a remote device hides the Account row entirely (remote usage = the host's own login) + clears any account filter. Remote data arrives via the 2.127.0 harvest (see data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan entry); harvest is CONSUMING (remote cursor advances) — tests must use VIBESPACE_USAGE_CURSOR temp paths, never production cursors (real incident: an e2e force-harvest ate the increment into a throwaway ledger and the UI looked unimplemented; rid-dedup made the cursor-reset re-harvest lossless)
