### desktop-manager.js

    desktop-manager.js — DesktopManager (virtual desktops, Ubuntu-style previews, window routing) **DRAG-REORDER (2.250.0): desktop preview wrappers are draggable(text/desktop-id); dropping onto another preview → reorderDesktop → ws `desktop-reorder {order}` → server reconciles the id list against the stored set (unknown ids dropped, omitted appended, size-mismatch rejected) so a stale client can't drop/invent a desktop. The preview stays a DROP target for BOTH a window-move(text/window-id) and a reorder — resolved by which dataTransfer key is set. Right-click menu anchors by viewport half (top-docked taskbar put previews near the top where an unconditional bottom-anchor grew off-screen). Stage preview render must apply the SAME waiting/find-flash classes as the normal-desktop loop (they were missing — a staged window's blink/flash was invisible in its preview).**

### stage-manager.js

    stage-manager.js   — StageManager (2.112.0 dynamic desktop 'Stage', default OFF via desktop.dynamicEnabled — docs/design-dynamic-desktop.md is the blueprint+progress anchor. VIEW model: one window two geometries (home gridBounds vs shared slot); materialization intercepts wm.focusWindow (_stageBypass prevents recursion; createWindow's tail focus flows through it too); per-hero aux workspaces = {openSpec, stageBounds, extras(scroll walker)} in SyncStore 'stage' (slot/ws:<key>/lru), heroKey RE-DERIVED AT RECORD TIME (backendSessionId arrives late); placeholder is a REAL wm window type 'stage-placeholder' — onWindowCreated MUST check the TYPE, the _isStagePlaceholder flag lands after createWindow's tail hook (smoke-caught: placeholder got swept as transient); incoming hero z-stacks at BOTTOM (moved slot must not cover a recorded workspace); LRU evicts hidden AUX only — session windows are NEVER closed by the stage (userW's killed-session lesson); desktop autosave is SUPPRESSED while staged (layout.js gate) — stage state never leaks into desktop records; userW's four defenses ported: serialized latest-wins switches, keyed replay dedup 15s, programmatic-switch guard, separate _hiddenByStage flag. RESTORATION MATRIX (2.112.1-2, design §4b, all 17 openSpec actions): durable=file/info-probed then replayed; derived=archive-entry temps carry via:{archive,entry} recipe and RE-EXTRACT on replay (the zip-PDF case); volatile=blob/data pages+recipe-less temps+DIRTY EDITORS (winInfo._editorDirty(), CodeEditor.modified exposed 2.112.2) are NEVER LRU-evicted and skip+one-toast when dead at replay; deleted task groups + 404 workflows pre-checked. INIT-ORDER GOTCHA (2.112.3, real 'placeholder never moves' report): stage.init() MUST run after initStateSync — StateSync.set() SILENTLY DROPS writes for unregistered stores, so the slot never persisted while dragging worked fine; _sync() lazily registers the store on any access as the guard. Any new SyncStore consumer needs the same care. **2.112.4 field fixes (4 user reports, CDP-smoke-verified — do not regress):** ① leave()/_deactivateHero HAND THE HERO BACK to the desktop system — restore _stageHomeBounds→gridBounds, clear _isStageHero/_onStage (off-stage moves edit HOME bounds, not the slot), then dm._hideWin/target-show like a normal desktop window (bare _hideStage left it invisible on its home desktop AND at slot size — the reported bug); enter() re-borrows via the still-set _heroWinId with a FRESH home snapshot; STAGE_ID-created heroes have no home and stay stage-hidden; _materializeInner clears stale _hiddenByDesktop. ② stage grid persists via stage-store key 'grid' — the interception lives at the TOP of scheduleAutoSave (NOT _doAutoSave: the _restoring gates dropped the call before the stage gate ran, smoke-caught); enter() applies it. ③ stage↔desktop window drags blocked BOTH directions (user directive superseding the design-§4 unbind+move): stage.dragToDesktopBlocked(win) gates window.js preview hover + dm.moveWindowToDesktop (covers the right-click menu too); placeholder additionally healStray()-recaptured + excluded from captureState (real report: escaped placeholder stuck on a desktop). ④ desktop.dynamicEnabled applies LIVE (init() settings listener → render kick / leave-if-disabled); same class fixed in sidebar for sessionCard.* card-build-time settings — a liveApply:true schema flag is a CLAIM, every render-time-read setting needs a change listener that re-renders its surface. **2.112.5 MULTI-CLIENT (real cross-client window-loss report — do not regress):** materializing a session with NO local window used to mint a stage-owned hero under a FRESH winId; leaving to the desktop holding that session per OTHER clients' records captured a state missing the recorded winId → the broadcast CLOSED the window on every client. Fix = IDENTITY ADOPTION: `_adoptDesktopIdentity` scans dm._savedStates for the session and converges (wm.rekeyWindow onto the recorded winId — chain-less windows only — + home desktop/gridBounds/isMaximized), retried at leave/_deactivateHero for late ids (resume fills openSpec async). Structural gotchas: createWindow's TAIL focus runs materialization BEFORE app.js's desktop-tag wrapper assigns _desktopId (adoption gate accepts !win._desktopId; the wrapper must not clobber an id set during creation); maximized heroes un-maximize onto the slot and hand-back applies home element geometry BEFORE re-maximizing (toggleMaximize must record HOME px as prevBounds). Live sync: init() subscribes 'stage'/'*' (StateSync fires for REMOTE ops only) — slot → re-apply to occupant, grid → setGrid, ws:<activeHeroKey> → debounced _reconcileWorkspace (restore + close-absent aux, dirty editors exempt); _scheduleRecord(500ms) on aux create/move keeps records live. **Hero is SHARED across clients (2.112.6, user directive — 挂机/walk-over): 'hero' store key {key, openSpec}; _publishHero at materialize (skip while _applyingRemoteHero), _followRemoteHero on remote ops (pointer-down deferred) + at enter (shared hero WINS over the tab's stale hero); hero close → clear → placeholder everywhere; only staged-or-not stays per-tab. closeWindow auto-focus-next (and applyLayout/overlap filters) must skip _hiddenByStage — a stage-hidden previous hero got auto-focused on hero close and re-materialized fleet-wide (harness-caught).** **2.151.0 preview fixes (2nd ghost report — do not regress):** ① the 2.134.0 home-bounds substitution in dm._renderSwitcher read `_stageHomeBounds?.gridBounds` but _stageHomeBounds IS the flat bounds object → always undefined → hero ghost-painted at the SLOT position on its home preview for 17 releases (shape-mismatch no-op; now reads the flat shape); ② the preview render DIGEST must cover every field the render filters on — _hiddenByDesktop/_hiddenByStage/_onStage were missing, so leave()'s intermediate setGrid-triggered render (target windows still hidden) cached a BLANK preview and the post-show render early-returned (preview stayed white until visiting another desktop); leave() also mirrors switchTo's +400/+1300ms refreshSwitcher retries. COMMITTED smoke: scripts/test-stage-preview.mjs (throwaway git-worktree server + headless-chrome raw CDP, 9 assertions through the REAL materialize/leave paths — replaces the ephemeral harnesses). Verified: 2-CDP-client harness 10/10 + single-client 11/11 — harness needs --disable-background-timer-throttling + real per-client input for _userDirty. **2.209.0 pile-at-slot rootfix (userW's 超级重叠 — Stage↔desktop round trip stacked every stage-born session at the slot, accumulating per trip; repro'd 16/16 then fixed, scripts/test-stage-overlap.mjs): ① enter() re-shows ONLY the live workspace (remembered hero + aux bound to _heroKey/'__pending__') — the blanket every-_hiddenByStage re-show resurrected every slot-PARKED ex-hero; ② _borrowHero ALWAYS creates a home snapshot BEFORE applying the slot (a window born while staged has no gridBounds at the synchronous creation-tail borrow → pixel _captureGridBounds first — safe, stage flags not yet set — else _cascadeHome(); slot-near bounds never become home) and _handBackHero has a cascade belt for pre-fix degenerated field state — without this the slot was the window's ONLY geometry forever; ③ dm.switchTo routes through stage.leave()/enter() while staged and treats '__stage__' as never-a-target — a raw switchTo (add-desktop +, command-mode d/D, moveSessionWindow, _onRemoteDesktopUpdated) captured the stage's window set under desktops['__stage__'] (lazy-replayed later as REAL windows at slot bounds) and left stage._active desynced; client boot + server layout-sync both refuse-and-scrub the poisoned record; ④ _onRemoteDesktopUpdated exempts STAGE_ID windows from deleted-desktop reassignment (any remote desktop meta change used to retag the whole stage onto a normal desktop) and never force-switches a staged client; ⑤ _materializeInner aborts when !_active (queued materialize firing post-leave); ⑥ leave() validates the target desktop still exists (remote-deleted _prevDesktopId). KNOWN MINOR (parked): stale shared-hero follow — _publishHero early-returns on null _heroKey with no retry at id adoption, so multi-client walk-over can follow an outdated hero record.**

### session-palette.js

    session-palette.js — Ctrl+K session palette (fuzzy session switcher; installSessionPalette, desktop only). **SEARCH IS TOKENISED (2.249.1, userW inc-msjro90z-n6y3 — never regress to whole-query matching):** the query splits on whitespace with AND semantics; NAME hits (1000-pos) outrank path/host/id hits (300) outrank a fuzzy subsequence (100) CONFINED TO THE NAME. Matching the query as ONE literal string meant a space could never substring-hit a CamelCase/hyphenated name ("best ever" vs "BestEver-ToB-signing"), so EVERY multi-word query fell through to a subsequence over the whole haystack (name + cwd + 3 UUIDs) — a shared cwd prefix supplies almost any letter sequence, so unrelated sessions all tied at the same low score. Compounding it, the live bonus was `+1000` on that tie, sorting every live session above every stopped one, and `slice(0,12)` cut the list before the wanted STOPPED session could appear. Ranking is now `sc*1000 + (live?500:0)` — match quality dominates, live only breaks ties among equally-good matches. Fuzzy-matching against a UUID is pure noise; never re-add it. Test: scripts/test-session-palette-search.mjs (11 asserts on the real reported data).


### collab-row.js (PURE, B-7473)

  src/collab-row.js — the label/HTML builder for codex multi-agent collab rows. PURE tier, **zero requires** (CJS pulled into the bundle like ssh-key-format.js): the escaper, the translator and the icon set are INJECTED. That is not ceremony — it makes the XSS rule *verifiable*: scripts/test-codex-subagents ① passes a MARKER escaper and asserts that nothing outside the markers is model-controlled, then repeats with the real `escHtml` semantics. `collabRowParts` splits a row into `{pre, name, post}` so the plain-text label (which lands in the message's `tool_result` output → search previews, fold summaries, the minimap) and the HTML row can never drift; `collabRowsHtml` renders one row or the coalesced summary + one clickable chip per agent; `collabRowTitle` is the hover envelope and says *payload encrypted upstream* when the body was withheld. A row NEVER carries an encrypted blob. The default translator substitutes `{n}` so server-side text is complete without a client `t()`.

### chat-view.js
  chat-view.js — ChatView controller (virtual scroll, op dispatch, lifecycle; gap-seek mixin in chat-view-seek.js). FOLD KINDS (2.369.33; chat.collapseKinds, semantic, one set for every backend): thinking/bash/read/write/memory/mcp/skill/agent/search/image — memberKind() maps claude tool names (Bash→bash, Read→read, Write/Edit/Patch→write, Grep/Glob/LS→read, WebSearch/WebFetch→search, Read of an image file→image (2.369.34), ToolSearch→mcp, Skill→skill, Agent/Task→agent, mcp__*→mcp) while codex/ACP cards arrive with `collapseKind` stamped by their normalizer (codex web_search/web_fetch→search, view_image→image; ACP kind search→search). The run header's byKind counter must list EVERY kind the classifier returns — an unlisted kind counts NaN and vanishes from the summary (2.369.34). INVARIANT: a tool card whose kind resolves to null is not merely unfolded — it BREAKS the run around it (the 42-WebSearch session had nothing folded); every new tool name needs a kind. Users who customised the kinds list must tick a new kind themselves (sparse settings keep their explicit list). **MSG-META POPUP (2026-09-06, codex per-message meta):** `_showMsgMeta` is backend-neutral — additive rows only when the meta carries them: 'Reasoning tokens' (`usage.reasoning_output_tokens`), 'Effort' (`meta.effort`), the cache-write row falls back to codex's `usage.cache_write_input_tokens` when there is no claude `cache_creation` TTL split (claude usage never has that field ⇒ unchanged), and the id labels follow the normalizer's kind markers — `requestIdKind:'ledger'` ⇒ 'Ledger request key' (the cx synthetic key is never dressed as a vendor request id), `msgIdKind:'response'` ⇒ 'Response ID'; claude meta has no kinds ⇒ old labels. The billing row's `sessionBilling()` fallback used to read `auth.accountName`/`auth.kind` — fields the server's sessionAuth shape ({source,name,poolTarget,tail,detail,hostName}) never had, so it answered null for EVERY backend; it now reads `source` like session-props/session-card (pooled → "name → target", codex-subscription → name, codex-cli → 'ChatGPT login', subscription → name|'CLI login', api-console/api-key/api-other) and the local 'not in the ledger yet' state is prefixed with that identity; a found global-bucket event names the login of ITS harness (`r.be === 'codex'` → 'ChatGPT login', the route returns `be`/`model`/`effort` additively). The run header's per-kind summary ("N web searches") is the fold's ONLY search-specific text — the query itself lives on the card title (chat-renderers, below), not here. FOLD KINDS (2.369.33; chat.collapseKinds, semantic, one set for every backend): thinking/bash/read/write/memory/mcp/skill/agent/search/image — memberKind() maps claude tool names (Bash→bash, Read→read, Write/Edit/Patch→write, Grep/Glob/LS→read, WebSearch/WebFetch→search, Read of an image file→image (2.369.34), ToolSearch→**lookup** (2.369.37 — NOT mcp: the owner caught "1 次 MCP" over a run whose only non-Bash card was a ToolSearch; lookups fold under the MCP toggle via `foldToggleFor` but are labelled "N tool lookups", and the "(server)" suffix comes only from real mcp__ calls), Skill→skill, Agent/Task→agent, mcp__*→mcp) while codex/ACP cards arrive with `collapseKind` stamped by their normalizer (codex web_search/web_fetch→search, view_image→image; ACP kind search→search). **Since 2.369.37 the classifier + label live in the PURE module `src/lib/chat-run-summary.js`** (`messageKind`, `foldToggleFor`, `countKinds`, `runSummaryParts`, `runSummaryLabel`, `mcpParts` — chat-renderers re-exports that one mcpParts): `countKinds` zero-fills `RUN_KINDS` and test-fold-ux asserts `SUMMARY_ORDER` covers every kind, so the 2.369.34 class (an unlisted kind counted NaN and vanished) is structural. INVARIANT: a tool card whose kind resolves to null is not merely unfolded — it BREAKS the run around it (the 42-WebSearch session had nothing folded); every new tool name needs a kind. Users who customised the kinds list must tick a new kind themselves (sparse settings keep their explicit list). **RUN CHROME (2.369.37, expanded-run legibility — see kb-features Chat Mode for the DOM shape):** `_updateRuns` rebuilds `this._runs` = `[{header, members, footer, label, open}]` every pass; `_setRunOpen(run, open)` is the ONE writer of `.chat-run-collapsed` / `.chat-run-member` / `-first` / `-last` and of the `.chat-run-footer` (inserted after the last member only while open); header AND footer are non-`.chat-msg` list children that every pass removes (`:scope > .chat-run-header, :scope > .chat-run-footer`) — so `_withViewportAnchor` and the seek gap anchor SKIP them as anchor candidates (a run-chrome anchor is dead after any pass inside fn → estimate-skewed delta fallback; members are only class-toggled and stay stable). The floating `.chat-run-bar` (lazily created on the `.chat-view` container, never in the list) is updated by `_updateRunBar(scrollTop)` from the scroll handler's rAF — placed BEFORE the `_programmaticScroll` early return, with the frame's already-read scrollTop, header/footer offsets read in the same layout pass; `_scheduleRunBar` (rAF-coalesced) re-runs it after each pass / setRunOpen / resume; `setSuspended(true)` hides it; dispose cancels the rAF. Bar/footer collapse = `_collapseRunTo` → `_setRunOpen(false)` + `_landOnHeader` (absolute `scrollTop = header.offsetTop` under a 400ms `_programmaticScroll` mute via `_jumpGuardTimer`, `_lastStructuralAt` stamped with dir null, pin re-derived from the landing). Toggling a run outside a pass mutates childList (footer) → the observer schedules a pass, whose anchor restore lands on the same position (it anchors on the first non-chrome visible element). Rules that must hold: the bar never calls `_extendTop/_extendBottom/_trim*` or writes `_pinned` (test-fold-ux greps its body); no Esc binding (data-popover protocol); the footer never counts toward the rendered window (trims select `.chat-msg:not(.chat-gap-msg)`). FOLD KINDS (2.369.33; chat.collapseKinds, semantic, one set for every backend): thinking/bash/read/write/memory/mcp/skill/agent/search/image — memberKind() maps claude tool names (Bash→bash, Read→read, Write/Edit/Patch→write, Grep/Glob/LS→read, WebSearch/WebFetch→search, Read of an image file→image (2.369.34), ToolSearch→**lookup** (2.369.37 — NOT mcp: the owner caught "1 次 MCP" over a run whose only non-Bash card was a ToolSearch; lookups fold under the MCP toggle via `foldToggleFor` but are labelled "N tool lookups", and the "(server)" suffix comes only from real mcp__ calls), Skill→skill, Agent/Task→agent, mcp__*→mcp) while codex/ACP cards arrive with `collapseKind` stamped by their normalizer (codex web_search/web_fetch→search, view_image→image; ACP kind search→search). **Since 2.369.37 the classifier + label live in the PURE module `src/lib/chat-run-summary.js`** (`messageKind`, `foldToggleFor`, `countKinds`, `runSummaryParts`, `runSummaryLabel`, `mcpParts` — chat-renderers re-exports that one mcpParts): `countKinds` zero-fills `RUN_KINDS` and test-fold-ux asserts `SUMMARY_ORDER` covers every kind, so the 2.369.34 class (an unlisted kind counted NaN and vanished) is structural. INVARIANT: a tool card whose kind resolves to null is not merely unfolded — it BREAKS the run around it (the 42-WebSearch session had nothing folded); every new tool name needs a kind. Users who customised the kinds list must tick a new kind themselves (sparse settings keep their explicit list). **RUN CHROME (2.369.37, expanded-run legibility — see kb-features Chat Mode for the DOM shape):** `_updateRuns` rebuilds `this._runs` = `[{header, members, footer, label, open}]` every pass; `_setRunOpen(run, open)` is the ONE writer of `.chat-run-collapsed` / `.chat-run-member` / `-first` / `-last` and of the `.chat-run-footer` (inserted after the last member only while open); header AND footer are non-`.chat-msg` list children that every pass removes (`:scope > .chat-run-header, :scope > .chat-run-footer`) — so `_withViewportAnchor` and the seek gap anchor SKIP them as anchor candidates (a run-chrome anchor is dead after any pass inside fn → estimate-skewed delta fallback; members are only class-toggled and stay stable). The floating `.chat-run-bar` (lazily created on the `.chat-view` container, never in the list) is updated by `_updateRunBar(scrollTop)` from the scroll handler's rAF — placed BEFORE the `_programmaticScroll` early return, with the frame's already-read scrollTop, header/footer offsets read in the same layout pass; `_scheduleRunBar` (rAF-coalesced) re-runs it after each pass / setRunOpen / resume; `setSuspended(true)` hides it; dispose cancels the rAF. Bar/footer collapse = `_collapseRunTo` → `_setRunOpen(false)` + `_landOnHeader` (absolute `scrollTop = header.offsetTop` under a 400ms `_programmaticScroll` mute via `_jumpGuardTimer`, `_lastStructuralAt` stamped with dir null, pin re-derived from the landing). **Toggling a run OUTSIDE a pass is itself a childList mutation (the footer) and must be hidden from the observer exactly the way a pass drains its own records** (2026-09-06 adversarial review, measured in headless chrome: on a PINNED view with 2+ runs, clicking any header except the last opened the run and re-collapsed it ~180ms later). A user click is not a pass, so the footer record scheduled one, and that pass's PINNED AUTO-REFOLD (`built.slice(0, -1)` — while `_pinned`, only the LAST run keeps an open flag, so a run expanded to watch the live tail re-folds once the tail moves past it, 2.227.x “一部分没折叠”) closed the run the click had just opened: every non-last header was a no-op while pinned. `_setRunOpen` now sets `_runsMutating` around the footer insert/remove when called outside a pass and calls `takeRecords()` only if it REALLY mutated (never swallow an unrelated pending record), and a deliberate toggle on a NON-LAST run marks its members in `_runStickyOpen` — a WeakSet keyed by member element like `_runExpanded`, because run records are rebuilt every pass; the auto-refold skips marked runs and nothing else, collapsing (header/footer/bar) clears the mark, and an INHERITED open flag still re-folds. The anchor restore is unaffected (it anchors on the first non-chrome visible element). Overlay hygiene: the floating bar shares the list's top edge with `.chat-pos-indicator` AND the history-load pill `.chat-history-status` — both are positioned by chat.css (the load pill lost its inline `top`, which no selector could have overridden) and both drop to `top:34px` (42px ≤768px) under `.chat-view.chat-run-bar-on`, the transient overlap being paging up INSIDE an open run. **Fixture law learned here: a browser fixture with ONE run cannot reach the multi-run branches** — 51 asserts were green over a broken interaction; test-fold-ux now builds two runs (the long tool run + a short mcp run after a text turn) and pins first-run-stays-open at t+600ms and across an explicit pass, the last run's unchanged behaviour, and the inherited-flag negative control. Rules that must hold: the bar never calls `_extendTop/_extendBottom/_trim*` or writes `_pinned` (test-fold-ux greps its body); no Esc binding (data-popover protocol); the footer never counts toward the rendered window (trims select `.chat-msg:not(.chat-gap-msg)`). **SHORT-VIEW RESCUE after attach (2.369.43)**: content that doesn't fill the viewport has no scrollable range, so the scroll handler never runs and touch has no wheel-up path — a fold-dominated slab (50 cards collapsed into two run headers) is a dead end with thousands of unreachable messages above. `_shortViewNeedsFill(list)` is the decision (more history above, no scrollable range, not suspended/disposed/teleported, and `rendered + 50 <= 150` so the extra page can never trip a trim onto the live tail) and `_scheduleAttachFill()` takes TWO readings 700ms/1600ms after loadHistory, extending only if both agree and `_lastStructuralAt` didn't move between them — the collapsed-geometry artifact that motivated the 2.369.36 hardening is transient and cannot survive the second reading. The 2.369.36 form (`rendered < 30`) was UNSATISFIABLE — every attach ships tail(50) and folding hides members without removing them, so the rescue was dead code; both timers clear in dispose(). Because chat-view.js is DOM-free at import, the predicate + schedule are FUNCTIONALLY tested against fake geometry in scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs. A Read is 'image' only when its RESULT carried image blocks (`content[0].images`) — EVIDENCE, never the extension (image-card review round 2: 12/12 real `Read *.svg` results in the fleet are numbered TEXT, and an extension rule both mis-counted them and let them escape the fold); codex/ACP arrive with `collapseKind` stamped. **THE IMAGE MEMBER IS EXEMPT FROM THE FOLD, NOT THE RUN (2.369.43 → image-card review round 2):** 'image' ships ON in the default kinds and the "any tool card folds, even a lone one" rule applied to it too, so every media card landed inside "1 image read" — `display:none`, invisible until clicked, and its `loading="lazy"` thumbnail never even fetched (the owner asked to SEE the image). The first cut exempted whole runs made ONLY of image views, which is the RARE shape — in real sessions the card sits BETWEEN foldable cards (Bash → Read(png) → Bash) and stayed hidden. So `flush()` computes `run.inline` = the image-kind members: they stay members of the run for every other purpose (rail, first/last, sticky mark, the floating bar's span, the summary's "N image reads") but `_setRunOpen` never gives them `.chat-run-collapsed`, so the fold closes AROUND them. A run with nothing left to fold (every member an image) gets no header at all — a fold control that hides nothing is a dead control. Pinned by test-image-cards ⑦ + the test-fold-ux browser fixture (bash|image|bash: the media card's computed display is not 'none' and its `<img>` really loads, while the Bash cards fold under the header). MEDIA THUMBNAILS: the list carries a CAPTURE-phase `error` delegate (img error does not bubble) that flags a broken `.chat-tool-img`'s `.chat-media` as `.chat-media-broken` (CSS swaps the img for the honest line) — rendered HTML never carries inline handlers; the fold's `display:none` is what keeps `loading="lazy"` thumbnails of a collapsed run from fetching. **COLLAB CLICK-THROUGH (B-7473):** a message carrying `collab` renders through chat-renderers `_renderCollabMsg` (report card / compact rows, src/collab-row.js does the escaping); the agent NAME (`.chat-collab-name`, data-agent-path + data-thread-id) is delegated in the messageList click handler to `_openCollabAgent`, which opens the child's rollout in the EXISTING sub-agent viewer when the thread id is known (0.153.4 always knows it — SubAgentActivity.agent_thread_id) and otherwise asks `GET /api/subagents?backend=codex&threadId=<root>` and TOASTS when the child is not on this machine (never a silent no-op). The run header gains `{n} sub-agent messages` (counting `collab.rows[].dir === 'in'`) and a `{n} sub-agents: …` chip list built from the run's rows — those chips get their OWN onclick with `stopPropagation()`, because the header's click toggles the fold, and they de-duplicate on `row.agentPath`, which the normalizer normalises to an ABSOLUTE path for every direction (round-5 fix: outbound rows keyed the bare `task_name` and one agent drew two chips). **The two B-7473 summary lines live in the PURE module like every other line (integration 2026-09-06 + round-5 rebase):** `'report'` — a sub-agent's written answer — is a REAL fold kind in `RUN_KINDS`/`SUMMARY_ORDER` (offered in `chat.collapseKinds` but shipping UNCHECKED, because a report is the content the window was opened to read; the one-line rows stay `'agent'`, which IS default-on), and the inbound-message count is `SUMMARY_EXTRAS = ['subAgentIn']` — a count the CALLER assigns into `byKind` (never produced by `messageKind`, never zero-filled, so an unset one renders nothing and can never go NaN) whose position in the label is decided by the same single `SUMMARY_ORDER`. chat-view NEVER keeps a second kind table. **THE QUEUE CHIP'S CAPABILITY ARRIVES AFTER THE PIXELS (2026-09-06, round-3 review):** the chip on a user bubble is built by `renderUserMsg`, which asks the view (`getQueueCaps` → `_queueCaps()` = harness `inputModes` ∧ the RUNNING wrapper's `_queueSupported`) and hands `applyQueueChip` a null handler when the answer is no — which renders it `disabled`. `_queueSupported` starts FALSE and BOTH of its sources land AFTER the bubbles: `loadHistory` renders every message and calls `_applyLiveMeta` (the attach payload's `queueSupported`) at the END, and a live wrapper's baseline `queue_changed` (`_onMeta` subtype 'queue', `supported:true`) arrives frames after the first messages. So after ANY history load the chip was permanently dead. `_setQueueSupported` is therefore the ONE writer of the flag and a change in EITHER direction schedules `_refreshQueueChips()` — rAF-coalesced, ONE pass over `_elements`, re-running `ChatRenderers.applyQueueChip` for every rendered message that carries a `queueState` (false→true makes them live, true→false makes them inert; the pending frame is cancelled in dispose()). The queue STRIP never had this bug because `_setQueue` re-renders it — the chips live inside bubbles nobody rebuilds. Gate: test-queue-steer ⑦ (render-then-deliver for both messengers + the reverse flip + a one-writer pin). **AUTO-SCROLL IS AUTOMATIC REPOSITIONING, AND IT MUST LOSE TO THE READER (B-9702, round 5 of the desktop-resume campaign):** `_forceScrollToBottom` writes `scrollTop = scrollHeight` for up to 10 frames (~166 ms) under the `_programmaticScroll` mute — the pinned view's auto-follow AND what every resume re-tail rung calls. It now carries an EPOCH: `_cancelForcedScroll(via)` bumps it and is called from `_notePositioning` and `_noteUserNav` (never from `_noteUserInput` — a click moves nothing), so every frame already queued is orphaned instead of writing scrollTop once more, while a later `_forceScrollToBottom` starts a fresh epoch so a cancel can never kill auto-follow. The mute is released ONE FRAME after the final write, guarded on `_fsbActive`: a scroll event is delivered after the callback that wrote it, so clearing the flag in the write's own frame handed our displacement to the boundary decision as if a reader had produced it. Without both, a re-tail rung armed at resume+1240 ms was still writing when the reader wheeled up at +1400 ms, its last frame landed 3 ms after their scroll, the pin re-engaged off our own write (`repin st:1760 … fsb:-1 posAgo:3` — ordinary geometry, `collapsedGeomSkip` never fired) and `_extendTop`'s pinned-tail invariant re-asserted the bottom: 5 of 20 real wheel-ups undone, 0 of 20 after. The `repin` trace (the twin of `unpin`) is what named it and stays. Gates: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (DOM-free chain semantics against a fake rAF queue, including the "auto-follow survives a cancel" and "the mute is never permanent" controls) + the two +1400 ms wheel legs in scripts/test-desktop-resume-paging.mjs (the second arms a chain by hand for determinism, with `_cancelForcedScroll` neutered on the instance as its control).

**PAGING INTENT — ONE PREDICATE (inc-mtq5bpjt-0o0n, after 2.369.42).** Every AUTOMATIC upward-paging trigger asks `_autoPagingBlocked()` and gets a REASON back (`disposed / suspended / resume-settle / pinned / settling / no-input / input-resize`) — the scroll handler's laws, in ONE place. Its callers: the gap sentinel's `IntersectionObserver` (`_observeHistoryGap` → `_loadEarlierGap(el, null, {via:'io'})`) and the scroll/wheel-driven `_maybeSeekEarlier` — the gate itself lives INSIDE `_loadEarlierGap` (chat-view-seek.js), i.e. on the path that is alive in the failure state, and traces `gapSkip {why, via}`; an explicit retry CLICK passes `{auto:false}` and bypasses it. Before this, `_loadEarlierGap`'s tail-mode branch (`!teleported && windowStart>0 ⇒ _extendTop()`) was the ONE upward-paging entry point with no gate at all, and an IntersectionObserver is PURE GEOMETRY — it needs no event, so a desktop resume's re-measure (scrollTop momentarily 0 while pinned) paged three pinned windows into history. **A new automatic paging trigger routes through this predicate; a new DIRECT `_extendTop()` call site must justify why it is user intent.**

**RESUME SETTLE + THE PINNED-TAIL INVARIANT (same incident).** `setSuspended(false)` arms `_resumeSettleUntil` (`RESUME_SETTLE_MS` 1200 ms) on top of the 2.369.1 `_lastStructuralAt` stamp: while it is open the scroll handler no-ops BEFORE it touches the pin (transitional geometry must not unpin either), `_autoPagingBlocked` says `resume-settle`, the loadHistory auto-fill DEFERS with bounded re-arms instead of deciding, and the pinned re-tail runs again when it expires (`_resumeSettleTimer`, cleared in `dispose()`). The window is armed as `RESUME_SETTLE_MS + RESUME_RETAIL_SLACK_MS` (40 ms) so it ENDS at the re-tail — the 40 ms gap between expiry and re-tail was itself a door (an input-less `scrollTop = 0` landing there unpinned the window and the re-tail, asserting on the live flag, then refused: stranded in history) — and the re-tail asserts on `_pinnedAtSuspend`, the pin SNAPSHOT taken by `setSuspended(true)` before the geometry starts lying, restoring `_pinned` itself when it acts off it. Real user input (wheel/touchmove/pointerdown/keydown) calls `_endResumeSettle()` — settle AND snapshot — so the settle suppresses input-LESS displacement only, never a reader. And the invariant the trim broke: **a PINNED view never loses its tail** — `_extendTop` skips `_trimBottom()` while `_pinned` (traced `trimSkipPinned`) and re-asserts the bottom after the prepend (`pinnedRetail`), because under transitional geometry the element anchor fails (`anchored:false`) and the delta fallback clamps scrollTop to 0. DOM growth stays bounded: the live-append path trims the TOP while pinned, and the next genuine (unpinned) page-up trims normally. Pins: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (source pins + a FUNCTIONAL hide→show unit: a transitional unpin still re-tails, a real reader is left alone) + the end-to-end scripts/test-desktop-resume-paging.mjs (real >34MB transcript, real desktop switch, a resume+1210 ms input-less injection, source-level negative control).

**ROUND 2 — THE TWO HOLES THE SETTLE LEFT (same incident).** ① *The re-tail was blind to readers who navigate.* Only the FOUR message-list listeners (wheel/touchmove/pointerdown/keydown) called `_endResumeSettle()`, and the surfaces a reader actually uses to go somewhere are not on the list: the minimap's pointer events live on the CONTAINER, the floating run bar on `this._container`, and `jumpToIndex` / a search reveal touch neither — so a reader who jumped 400 ms into a resume was silently dragged back to the live tail when the re-tail fired (measured: at +2600 ms pinned at the tail again). Now every off-list positioning surface calls `_noteUserNav(via)` (stamp `_lastNavAt` + end the settle): the two minimap callbacks, `jumpToIndex`, a USER `jumpToBottom({user:true})` (the re-tail passes `user:false` so it cannot cancel its own series), `_jumpToFileTime`, `_landOnHeader` (run bar + footer) and ChatSearch's reveal through the new `onNav` callback. The re-tail captures `resumeAt` and returns when `_navigatedSince(resumeAt)` — the chat-view-seek `userScrolled = _lastUserScrollAt > max(jumpAt, revealAt)` idiom generalised over every positioning stamp we own (`_lastNavAt`, `_lastUserScrollAt`, `_lastJumpAt`, `_search._lastRevealAt`). ② *The protection was a one-shot CLIFF.* The settle ended at 1240 ms and the re-tail ran there; an input-less displacement at +1400 ms unpinned the window and stranded it (3/3 sessions — +1240/+1280 survived only because `_forceScrollToBottom`'s 10-frame chain happened to still be running). Now the re-tail is a BOUNDED SERIES (`RESUME_RETAIL_AT_MS` = [1240, 2000], `_resumeRetailTimers`, `_clearResumeRetail()` on suspend/dispose/real input) **and the UNPIN ITSELF is gated on positive evidence** like the paging triggers: `_resumeDisplacement()` = inside `RESUME_DISPLACEMENT_MS` (2800 ms — past the last rung, because the displacement the rungs cannot catch is the one that arrives between them), pin SNAPSHOT still true, and nothing the reader did has landed since ⇒ keep `_pinned`, re-assert the bottom, trace `unpinSkipResume`. A real wheel/touch/key/pointer or any nav drops the snapshot, so a reader at +1400 ms unpins and pages normally. ③ The settle return in the scroll handler moved BELOW the destructure + `_updateRunBar(scrollTop)` (next to the `_programmaticScroll` return) — the bar is a READOUT, not a decision, and returning above it froze the 2.369.45 floating run bar for the whole settle; everything below that line is a decision. WHICH guard owns which part of the horizon was MEASURED, not assumed (the chrome sweep captures per-leg traces): 1240 ms is inside the settle (the scroll handler decides nothing, the rung re-tails), 1400 ms still traces `collapsedGeomSkip` — the 2.301.0 guard owns it, because its `settling` window runs 1500 ms from the resume's structural stamp — and only at 1900/2400 ms is `unpinSkipResume` the sole thing between an input-less displacement and a stranded window. Pins: the trim-guard suite's source ORDER pin restates its budgets per segment (suspend→runBar ≤400, runBar→programmatic ≤200, programmatic→settle ≤1400, settle→atBottom ≤2400) and the chrome suite adds a nav-during-settle leg, the displacement sweep (outcome for all four offsets + the gate's own trace where it is the only guard left), a real wheel-up inside the horizon (it must page and must NOT trip the gate — its final pin state belongs to the collapsed-geometry guard, not to this fix), and a PER-MECHANISM negative control for each, neutered on the instance in the same run (the source-level control rebuilds with `RESUME_SETTLE_MS = 0` and cannot host a timing-dependent repro; its cliff leg injects at the SAME 1900 ms the fixed build passes at, because at 1400 ms the 1240 rung's own `_forceScrollToBottom` chain re-pins even with the gate off — the very accident the finding named).

**ROUND 3 — A CLICK IS NOT A POSITIONING ACT (same incident, reproduced with TRUSTED CDP input).** Every message-list listener ran the same `_endResumeSettle()`, which zeroes the settle window AND drops `_pinnedAtSuspend` AND clears the bounded re-tail series — so a plain left-click/tap anywhere in the list during the 1.24 s settle disarmed the whole repair and the resume's own input-LESS displacement then reproduced the incident BEHIND the click (measured: click at +300 ms, `scrollTop = 0` at +1400 ms → unpinned, `fromBottom 1883`). THE RULE, by grade: `_noteUserInput()` — a pointerdown, or a key that is not in `NAV_KEYS` — stamps `_lastUserScrollAt` (the paging gates' `no-input` evidence, which scrollbar and keyboard readers need) and may end the settle WINDOW, and that is ALL it does; `_notePositioning(via)` — wheel, touchmove, a navigation key (PageUp/PageDown/Home/End/Arrow*/Space), or a scrollbar drag — additionally stamps `_lastPositionAt` and runs `_endResumeSettle()` (window + snapshot + series), exactly like `_noteUserNav`'s six off-list surfaces. `_navigatedSince()` reads the POSITIONING stamps only (`_lastNavAt`, `_lastPositionAt`, `_lastJumpAt`, `_search._lastRevealAt`) and never `_lastUserScrollAt`. A SCROLLBAR DRAG has no event of its own, so it is recognised by a signature: `_pointerDragScroll(scrollTop)`, stamped in the scroll handler ABOVE the settle return (the drag must be able to end the very settle it starts inside; the order pin's budgets are now suspend→runBar ≤400, runBar→programmatic ≤200, programmatic→drag ≤700, drag→settle ≤1400, settle→atBottom ≤2400). Round 3 wrote that signature in TIME — a scroll within 400 ms of a pointerdown that moved the view by more than `POINTER_DRAG_PX` (2 px) — see ROUND 4 below for why that had to become a POSITION. The chrome suite drives all three with real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` — a click (the repair survives it), a `mouseWheel` up (unpins immediately and pages), and a native-scrollbar drag with `.chat-minimap-active` removed first (the semantic minimap sets `scrollbar-width: none`, so a native scrollbar only exists with the minimap off — with it ON the reader drags the minimap, which is already `_noteUserNav('minimap')`), each with its own instance-level control. KNOWN RESIDUAL (round-3 MINOR, backlog B-8e5e, not fixed here): a wheel-up at resume+1400 ms pages but is re-pinned ~1/3 of the time by the collapsed-geometry `atBottom` re-pin — that flag belongs to the 2.301.0 guard, and this fix does not assert it.

**ROUND 4 — THE DRAG IS WHERE THE PRESS LANDED, NOT WHEN A SCROLL FOLLOWS (same incident, two MAJORs against round 3's own signature).** ① The 400 ms window admitted the ARTIFACT: the incident's re-measure bounces run +366…+602 ms after the switch, so a plain CONTENT click with one of them landing behind it satisfied "a scroll soon after a pointerdown that moved the view" → `_notePositioning('scrollbar-drag')` → `_endResumeSettle()` (window + snapshot + series) → the incident, reproduced behind the click. Round 3's leg passed only because it put the click at +300 ms and the displacement at +1400 ms. ② …and it EXCLUDED a real reader: a drag whose first move comes later than 400 ms after the press (press, look, then drag) was never positioning, so the re-tail series yanked that reader back to the live tail — a NEW harm versus master. NOW: `_pointerOnScrollbar(e)` hit-tests the pointerdown against the list's scrollbar GUTTER — `offsetWidth - clientWidth` minus the borders, on the right in LTR and on the LEFT in RTL, plus the bottom strip for a horizontal scrollbar — with coordinates converted viewport→LAYOUT px first (the uiScale body `zoom` scales `getBoundingClientRect`/`clientX` but NOT `clientWidth`; mixing the two spaces is the 2.369.5 VNC-pointer bug). The verdict rides `_pointerDownOnScrollbar` for the LIFETIME of the press, cleared by WINDOW-scoped `pointerup`/`pointercancel` listeners bound to `winInfo._listenerCtl.signal` (a scrollbar drag routinely releases outside the list; `dispose()` also removes them, for a view torn down while its window lives on), and `_pointerDragScroll` is "the flag is set AND the view moved > `POINTER_DRAG_PX`". `POINTER_DRAG_MS` is DELETED — a content click never becomes positioning however close a displacement lands, and a press-and-hold-then-drag is positioning for the whole press. `_pointerOnScrollbar` is DOM-free (it reads injected geometry) and unit-tested in plain node; the chrome suite adds a click-at-+300 ms/displacement-at-+450 ms leg and a press-hold-900 ms-then-drag leg, each with an instance-level control that restores the pre-fix outcome (the old time-window signature strands the window; a false hit-test yanks the slow dragger back).

### chat-renderers.js
  chat-renderers.js — Message rendering (renderUserMsg/renderAssistantMsg/renderToolMsg/renderToolResult/renderSystemMsg, linkify, diffs, permissions). **Tool-card headers**: `toolHeaderHtml(name)` = curated localized display name (toolDisplayName's map — claude built-ins AND codex raw names `web_search`/`web_fetch` → t('Web search')/t('Fetch page'), 2.369.43) or the MCP short name + server chip; the raw identifier rides the card's title tooltip. **SEARCH-CARD TITLE CHIP (2.369.43, owner ask "see the query without expanding"):** `searchQueryChipHtml(block, msg)` appends `<span class="chat-tool-query" title="<full>">…</span>` to the header of every search-kind card — claude WebSearch (input.query) / WebFetch (input.url) by tool name, codex web_search/web_fetch and ACP search tools by the normalizer's `collapseKind:'search'` hint — with the text from the PURE shared `searchQueryOf` (src/search-card.js: query → action.queries joined ' | ' → action.query → 'pattern' in url → url → pattern). Three header paths call it (pending/interrupted tool_call, error result, generic result); '' when nothing to show (a codex card is an empty stub until item/completed). XSS LAW: the query is model/web-controlled text that syncs to every client — escHtml on the chip text AND its title attribute, truncated at 90 chars with the full string in the tooltip; CSS `.chat-tool-query` (chat.css) is theme-var only. Non-search cards are untouched. Gate: scripts/test-search-card-title.mjs (35: the pure helper over real input shapes, the REAL renderer esbuild-bundled for node with a DOM shim — claude/codex/ACP chips, XSS escaping, truncation, no chip on Bash, pending stub — plus wiring pins).

### search-card.js (PURE)
  src/search-card.js — ONE home for web-search cards on both sides of the wire (PURE tier; CJS pulled into the bundle like task-color-seq.js): `renderSearchOutput({query, action, results, error})` (server: the codex normalizer's only results renderer — the wrapper does NOT render, it relays codex's typed event), `searchQueryOf(input)` (client title chip + any server consumer), `searchActionKey(action, query)` (0.14x twin pairing: action-only key, the bare query stands in only when there is no action — an open_page end's query is its url and its call twin has no query at all). Caps: 20 results, 4096 bytes (TextEncoder, char-boundary cut — no Buffer, the module ships in the browser). actionType(action)`, `searchActionKey(action, query)`. **`action.type` is spelled TWO ways for the same thing and both reach here** — core protocol `search|open_page|find_in_page|other` (0.120-0.149 `web_search_end`/`web_search_call`) and v2 camelCase `search|openPage|findInPage|other` (the wrapper's live item AND the 0.153.4 rollout's Extension item); `actionType()` canonicalises, so every head/key/title path handles both. **`url`/`pattern` are NULLABLE in the v2 schema** (a real 0.153.4 `findInPage` carried `url:null`): the head is `found '<pattern>'` with no location rather than a fabricated one, and the title chip never trails a dangling ' in'. **'no results' is a CLAIM only an EMPTY ARRAY supports** (2026-09-06 refutation: 0.120/0.125/0.130 NEVER persist the key — 1698 ends, 0 with it — so absent means *nothing known*; 42 cards of one file claimed 'no results' about searches that had them). Absent key ⇒ the card states what was DONE: the `opened <url>` / `found '<p>'` head, else `searched: <query>`, else `status: completed`. 0.149.1 and 0.153.4 always persist the array. **Twin dedup (0.120-0.130 only)**: `searchActionKey` is ACTION-ONLY (the id-less `web_search_call` twin has no query; an open_page end's query is its url) and the bare query stands in only when there is no action at all — with `{type:'other'}` and NO-ACTION deliberately in ONE bucket, because an `{type:'other'}` end's twin carries no `action` key and keying them apart produced a second empty card per search (the owner's `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' shape; real rollout-2026-04-14T03-14-36 lines 6417-6421). Caps: 20 results, 4096 bytes (TextEncoder, char-boundary cut — no Buffer, the module ships in the browser).. actionType(action)`, `searchActionKey(action, query)`, `NO_SEARCH_DETAILS` (the label a record with NO query, NO action and NO results wears — head `web search (no details recorded)`, input `{note:…}`; the ONE 0.120.0 orphan in the local corpus used to render as the owner's `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' shape, and a card must never reproduce it byte for byte). **`action.type` is spelled TWO ways for the same thing and both reach here** — core protocol `search|open_page|find_in_page|other` (0.120-0.149 `web_search_end`/`web_search_call`) and v2 camelCase `search|openPage|findInPage|other` (the wrapper's live item AND the 0.153.4 rollout's Extension item); `actionType()` canonicalises, so every head/key/title path handles both. **`url`/`pattern` are NULLABLE in the v2 schema** (a real 0.153.4 `findInPage` carried `url:null`): the head is `found '<pattern>'` with no location rather than a fabricated one, and the title chip never trails a dangling ' in'. **'no results' is a CLAIM only an EMPTY ARRAY supports** (2026-09-06 refutation: 0.120/0.125/0.130 NEVER persist the key — 1698 ends, 0 with it — so absent means *nothing known*; 42 cards of one file claimed 'no results' about searches that had them). Absent key ⇒ the card states what was DONE: the `opened <url>` / `found '<p>'` head, else `searched: <query>`, else `status: completed`. 0.149.1 and 0.153.4 always persist the array. **Twin dedup (0.120-0.130 only)**: `searchActionKey` is ACTION-ONLY (the id-less `web_search_call` twin has no query; an open_page end's query is its url) and the bare query stands in only when there is no action at all — with `{type:'other'}` and NO-ACTION deliberately in ONE bucket, because an `{type:'other'}` end's twin carries no `action` key and keying them apart produced a second empty card per search (the owner's `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' shape; real rollout-2026-04-14T03-14-36 lines 6417-6421).
